# Psalms

## Psalms 1  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-02

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17638

<!-- post 17638 · 2026-07-02 05:39 · scripture -->

**Psalms 1**

*1  Blessed is the man that walketh not in the counsel of the ungodly, nor standeth in the way of sinners, nor sitteth in the seat of the scornful.*

*2  But his delight is in the law of the LORD; and in his law doth he meditate day and night.*

*3  And he shall be like a tree planted by the rivers of water, that bringeth forth his fruit in his season; his leaf also shall not wither; and whatsoever he doeth shall prosper.*

*4  The ungodly are not so: but are like the chaff which the wind driveth away.*

*5  Therefore the ungodly shall not stand in the judgment, nor sinners in the congregation of the righteous.*

*6  For the LORD knoweth the way of the righteous: but the way of the ungodly shall perish.*

<!-- post 17639 · 2026-07-02 06:10 · study -->

The book of Psalms, songs sung by the congregation, musicians and singers, in the called out assembly. 

Most are attributed to King David, whose playing of the harp soothe his predecessor, King Saul, and while **Psalm 1** isn't clearly introduced as David's, it follows his patterns of praise and doctrine, and generally is believed to be his. But more importantly, they are of the Spirit, like all scripture, and profitable for learning and reproof. 

And much like the poetic wisdom of Solomon, we find the gospel of Christ throughout. 

Verse 1 opens with a standard refrain found in **Psalms**; the blessedness of the man, and the understanding that it is by God's grace and mercy all blessings flow, Whose promises are sure. 

Here, the promise to bless those who do not listen to the ungodly, the wisdom of this world. Blessing those who do not go the way of sinners, or the scorners. 

In many ways, the Psalms reflect the wisdom of the Lord found in Proverbs, and give an insight to the mind and heart of the King, a man after God's own heart. 

David contrasts the ungodly sinners and scorners with the man who delights in the law of God. Who meditates upon the word day and night. This too is a frequent theme found in **Psalms**, and it shows the importance of scripture, and its role in the Christian life. 

Too often we see those opposed to Christ attacking the inerrant, inspired and preserved word of God. As Satan tempted Eve in the garden, saying "Yea, hath God said?" enemies of God attack His word, because it is the easiest way to cast doubt, or to put false doctrine, vain traditions and damnable heresy in place of the rock which Jesus tells us to build upon; these sayings of His. 

Even so-called professing Christian sects and denominations, that make their history, mythology and traditions the focal point of their faith will downplay and minimize the importance of scripture, mocking Bible believing Christians for making it the sole authority in our faith and practices. 

But what sayeth the scripture? Those that walk in the Spirit, seeking the blessings of God, meditate in His word day and night. By doing so, we become as a tree planted by the river, constantly fed and nourished. Bearing fruit for God. Not withering and being cut off, as the unfruitful. Prospering in all our ways. 

The ungodly have no fruit. They are compared to the chaff of wheat, that blows away in the wind on the threshing floor. Insubstantial. Unfruitful. Hollow husks with no purpose other than to be burned up in fire. 

The distinction between the two types of men, the saved believers, the righteous by faith and the ungodly, who do not walk in faith, is made clear. 

We will sit in judgment with Christ. We shall be the church of the righteous, the congregation of the elect in Jesus. But the ungodly will not. 

*For the LORD knoweth the way of the righteous: but the way of the ungodly shall perish.*

Just as Jesus asks Martha after the death of her brother, Lazarus: 

*And whosoever liveth and believeth in me shall never die. Believest thou this?*

David clearly did. The sinners perish. But the righteous in Christ are with the Lord, always.

## Psalms 1  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2025-08-04

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/16477

<!-- post 16477 · 2025-08-04 06:29 · scripture -->

**Psalm 1:1-4**
*Blessed is the man that walketh not in the counsel of the ungodly, nor standeth in the way of sinners, nor sitteth in the seat of the scornful. But his delight is in the law of the Lord; and in his law doth he meditate day and night. And he shall be like a tree planted by the rivers of water, that bringeth forth his fruit in his season; his leaf also shall not wither; and whatsoever he doeth shall prosper. The ungodly are not so: but are like the chaff which the wind driveth away*.

<!-- post 16478 · 2025-08-04 06:32 · study -->

**Zachariah 14:1-4,8**
*Behold, the day of the Lord cometh, and thy spoil shall be divided in the midst of thee. For I will gather all nations against Jerusalem to battle; and the city shall be taken, and the houses rifled, and the women ravished; and half of the city shall go forth into captivity, and the residue of the people shall not be cut off from the city. Then shall the Lord go forth, and fight against those nations, as when he fought in the day of battle. And his feet shall stand in that day upon the mount of Olives, which is before Jerusalem on the east, and the mount of Olives shall cleave in the midst thereof toward the east and toward the west, and there shall be a very great valley; and half of the mountain shall remove toward the north, and half of it toward the south...And it shall be in that day, that living waters shall go out from Jerusalem; half of them toward the former sea, and half of them toward the hinder sea: in summer and in winter shall it be.*

**John 7:38-39**
*He that believeth on me, as the scripture hath said, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water. (But this spake he of the Spirit, which they that believe on him should receive: for the Holy Ghost was not yet given; because that Jesus was not yet glorified.)*

**Revelation 22:1-2**
*And he shewed me a pure river of water of life, clear as crystal, proceeding out of the throne of God and of the Lamb. In the midst of the street of it, and on either side of the river, was there the tree of life, which bare twelve manner of fruits, and yielded her fruit every month: and the leaves of the tree were for the healing of the nations.*

<!-- post 16479 · 2025-08-04 06:59 · study -->

Here in the penultimate chapter of Ezekiel, continuing with the prophecy of the Temple, we find the prophet brought out with the man guiding him before, who had the measure in his hands, and observing the flowing of water out of the altar and beyond the Temple and the courtyard. 

And the further they went, the deeper the water became, from his ankle to his knees, to his loins. Eventually, to a mighty river that could not be passed over, deep enough to swim in. 

Returning to the river's brink, the shore along a steep rise, and there he observed the trees on both sides. The angels tells him that the waters themselves that issue out of the Temple will eventually fill the desert, and the seas, healing all they touch, everything *shall live whither the river cometh*. 

Living waters emanating from Jerusalem, trees lining their banks. 

In **Psalm 1**, the Bible says we who delight in the Lord and His word are like unto trees planted by a river, that brings forth fruit, and the leaves shall not whither. 

The prophet Zachariah preaches that in the day of the Lord, among the other things that occur, would be Jesus splitting the mount of Olives in Jerusalem, and in that day *living waters* shall go out from Jerusalem.

In the gospel of John, Jesus says that out of the belly of believers shall flow rivers of living water, and that this was a picture of the Spirit which they that believe on Him should receive.

Later, in the Revelation of Jesus given John, the new Heaven and heavenly Jerusalem has a river, and the tree of life upon it, which gives 12 different fruits every month, and whose leaves were for healing the nations. 

The natural application for the initial prophecy given Ezekiel is a restored kingdom, with a river of waters healing the land, trees grown up from its shores, and full of all types of fish in abundance. 

But spiritually, this is fulfilled in the ministry of Christ, and the spreading of the Gospel, as living waters, the Spirit, and wherever it went, healing the nations, and believers being as trees bearing fruit, other believers, springing up alongside the brink. 

Prophetically, this matches the pattern in the end times shown John, of New Jerusalem. 

If you are like the righteous man in **Psalm 1**, the believer in **John 7**, you are a tree bearing fruit, and from you flows the Spirit, living waters, His word. 

Notice, the river begins in Jerusalem and gets deeper and deeper the further it travels. 

And note too, that there are miry places, salt marshes that shall not be healed. 

That the Mt Olive prophecy in Zachariah talks about the split, and a war between Judah. 

There are those who will not receive the truth. There were Jews who would not believe, and they killed Christ and persecuted His followers.

<!-- post 16480 · 2025-08-04 07:07 · study -->

Finally, in the final verse of Ezekiel 47, we are again shown that this The empire and Kingdom would not be exclusive to the Hebrews. The 12 tribes of Israel given land, Joseph's double portion making up for the Levites, are joined by strangers among them, the circumcised of the heart talked about in chapter 46. 

These Gentiles are given the same inheritance, the same birthright of those among the tribes of Israel. Wherever they sojourn, in whatever tribe they settle, there it will be for them and inheritance. 

**Ephesians 2:12-19**
*That at that time ye were without Christ, being aliens from the commonwealth of Israel, and strangers from the covenants of promise, having no hope, and without God in the world: But now in Christ Jesus ye who sometimes were far off are made nigh by the blood of Christ. For he is our peace, who hath made both one, and hath broken down the middle wall of partition between us; Having abolished in his flesh the enmity, even the law of commandments contained in ordinances; for to make in himself of twain one new man, so making peace; And that he might reconcile both unto God in one body by the cross, having slain the enmity thereby: And came and preached peace to you which were afar off, and to them that were nigh. For through him we both have access by one Spirit unto the Father. Now therefore ye are no more strangers and foreigners, but fellowcitizens with the saints, and of the household of God*

This shows regardless of the covenant, regardless of which Testament that man was under, it was never exclusive to the Jews. Never. It was always about those circumcised of the heart and believing on Christ, calling upon His name. 

There is neither Jew nor Greek. 

The Dispensationalist Zionists seek to exclude the Gentiles from the inheritance and the promises. To say that Jews are the seed of Abraham, rather than all who believe on THE seed, being singular; Christ Jesus. 

They ignore the inconvenient truths in the New Testament, but you can see that they also ignore those prophecies in the Old. 

They have the spirit of Edom, like Esau, giving away the inheritance for pottage. 

God's redemptive plan was ALWAYS to include people from every nation, kindred and tongue. It always did, as we see in multiple OT figures who came to believe. Many grafted into the line of Christ (Tamar, Ruth, Rahab, likely also Bathsheba). 

The genetic false gospel of special favors to the Jews was always unbiblical. Regardless of how the prophecies ultimately played out.

## Psalms 2  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-03

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17644

<!-- post 17644 · 2026-07-03 06:59 · scripture -->

**Psalms 2**
*1 Why do the heathen rage, and the people imagine a vain thing?*

*2 The kings of the earth set themselves, and the rulers take counsel together, against the LORD, and against his anointed, saying,*

*3 Let us break their bands asunder, and cast away their cords from us.*

*4 He that sitteth in the heavens shall laugh: the LORD shall have them in derision.*

*5 Then shall he speak unto them in his wrath, and vex them in his sore displeasure.*

*6 Yet have I set my king upon my holy hill of Zion.*

*7 I will declare the decree: the LORD hath said unto me, Thou art my Son; this day have I begotten thee.*

*8 Ask of me, and I shall give thee the heathen for thine inheritance, and the uttermost parts of the earth for thy possession.*

*9 Thou shalt break them with a rod of iron; thou shalt dash them in pieces like a potter’s vessel.*

*10 Be wise now therefore, O ye kings: be instructed, ye judges of the earth.*

*11 Serve the LORD with fear, and rejoice with trembling.*

*12 Kiss the Son, lest he be angry, and ye perish from the way, when his wrath is kindled but a little. Blessed are all they that put their trust in him.*

<!-- post 17645 · 2026-07-03 07:16 · study -->

Why do the heathen rage? Why do people think they can go against God? Kings and rulers, presidents and prime ministers, popes and priests, all seek to be freed from the ways of the Lord, to do what they will. 

Especially the religious and political leaders in the time of Jesus. 

**Acts 4:27**
*For of a truth against thy holy child Jesus, whom thou hast anointed, both Herod, and Pontius Pilate, with the Gentiles, and the people of Israel, were gathered together*.

They believed if they killed the Son, they would break the bands, Their bonds, and be free of God. 

Jesus addressed this in a parable, about a Lord who planted a vineyard and lent it out to husbandmen to dress and keep it. And when the Lord sent servants to gather the harvest, the stewards beat and killed them. So finally, the Lord sends his son;

**Matthew 21:38**
*But when the husbandmen saw the son, they said among themselves, This is the heir; come, let us kill him, and let us seize on his inheritance*.

David prophetically writes of this here in **Psalm 2**. 

But, it is folly. The Lord holds them in derision. He laughs at their vain efforts, because Christ, His anointed, rose again. 

He set His Son upon Zion, and upon the throne. 

**Acts 13:32–33**
*And we declare unto you glad tidings, how that the promise which was made unto the fathers, God hath fulfilled the same unto us their children, in that he hath raised up Jesus again; as it is also written in the second psalm, Thou art my Son, this day have I begotten thee.*

Jesus' inheritance are all the nations of the world, and possession of the uttermost parts of the world. And as Lord of lords and King of kings, He will rule them. 

**Revelation 2:26–27**
*And he that overcometh, and keepeth my works unto the end, to him will I give power over the nations: And he shall rule them with a rod of iron; as the vessels of a potter shall they be broken to shivers: even as I received of my Father.*

This prophetic Psalm gives warning to the rulers of this world, reminding them of their place. That they serve at the pleasure of the Lord, and He will deliver vengeance. 

It's an amazing and consistent message, across the entire Bible; The Christ is God's anointed, and Jesus is the Christ. All who believe on Christ Jesus shall be saved. His grace and mercy giving us the chance to serve Him in righteousness. 

*Serve the LORD with fear, and rejoice with trembling. Kiss the Son, lest he be angry, and ye perish from the way, when his wrath is kindled but a little. Blessed are all they that put their trust in him.*

Amen

## Psalms 3  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-07

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17654

<!-- post 17654 · 2026-07-07 06:59 · scripture -->

**Psalms 3**
*Lord, how are they increased that trouble me! many are they that rise up against me.*

*2 Many there be which say of my soul, There is no help for him in God. Selah.*

*3 But thou, O LORD, art a shield for me; my glory, and the lifter up of mine head.*

*4 I cried unto the LORD with my voice, and he heard me out of his holy hill. Selah.*

*5 I laid me down and slept; I awaked; for the LORD sustained me.*

*6 I will not be afraid of ten thousands of people, that have set themselves against me round about.*

*7 Arise, O LORD; save me, O my God: for thou hast smitten all mine enemies upon the cheek bone; thou hast broken the teeth of the ungodly.*

*8 Salvation belongeth unto the LORD: thy blessing is upon thy people. Selah.*

<!-- post 17655 · 2026-07-07 07:07 · study -->

When the world seems against you, and the wicked seem to prosper, and scoffers mock your trust in the Lord, we can grow weary and discouraged. 

Knowing there are others who share this feeling, and have experienced similar despair, but found comfort and solace in prayer, as David does here, is such a blessing. And it is part of what makes the Bible so different from other religious texts. 

The Lord is our shield. He does lift up our heads when we are feeling down. When everything feels lost, and we don't have the strength to continue we can cry out to the Lord, and He will hear us. 

The same Lord over all that gives us life, and a new day and another chance, is the same Lord Who protects us from all evil. 

Vengeance is His. And He is faithful to answer our plea for it. Turn to the Lord rather than wrath or despair, and see His faithful love for us. 

Knowing that *Salvation belongeth unto the LORD: thy blessing is upon thy people.*

## Psalms 4  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-08

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17657

<!-- post 17657 · 2026-07-08 05:46 · scripture -->

**Psalms 4**
*Hear me when I call, O God of my righteousness: thou hast enlarged me when I was in distress; have mercy upon me, and hear my prayer.*

*2 O ye sons of men, how long will ye turn my glory into shame? how long will ye love vanity, and seek after leasing? Selah.*

*3 But know that the LORD hath set apart him that is godly for himself: the LORD will hear when I call unto him.*

*4 Stand in awe, and sin not: commune with your own heart upon your bed, and be still. Selah.*

*5 Offer the sacrifices of righteousness, and put your trust in the LORD.*

*6 There be many that say, Who will shew us any good? LORD, lift thou up the light of thy countenance upon us.*

*7 Thou hast put gladness in my heart, more than in the time that their corn and their wine increased.*

*8 I will both lay me down in peace, and sleep: for thou, LORD, only makest me dwell in safety.*

<!-- post 17658 · 2026-07-08 06:10 · study -->

Psalms of prayer are the most common. The cries to the Lord to save us from affliction and thanksgiving for having delivered us. 

We often don't know how to pray, let alone in the midst of trouble. We often don't take our troubles directly to God. And when we do, we do, are at a loss for what to say. 

**Romans 8:26**
*Likewise the Spirit also helpeth our infirmities: for we know not what we should pray for as we ought: but the Spirit itself maketh intercession for us with groanings which cannot be uttered*

But the Psalms are given as a foundation of prayer, to know we have a strong deliverer, a Lord that hears us, Who has given us help in our distress and Who grants mercy to His people. 

David calls out to the Lord, *O God of my righteousness,* not telling God of his own righteousness, but the knowledge that whatever righteousness we have, is His. 

David acknowledges that the Lord has delivered him in the past, and is asking to be heard to do it again. Praise and gratitude. Humility. These are the foundations of an effective prayer. 

Lamenting the ways of those who trouble us, the sons of men who vex the righteous with their filthy conversation. They scoff at the faithful. They love vain things, worldly ideas that profit nothing. They seek after leasing, which means to pursue lies, falsehoods, or deceit. But we are not like they are. 

Or at least we shouldn't be. 

The Lord has set us apart, His holy people. And He will hear when we reach out to Him. 

*Stand in awe, and sin not: commune with your own heart upon your bed, and be still. Selah.*

How often do we lay awake in prayer. Pondering our own sins, and seeking the Lord? How often do we wake in the morning, and praise God for His mercy, asking to be used by Him today? 

We have the power given us by Christ, to be sons of God. To boldly go before the throne. Stand in awe, and sin not. Make yourselves a living sacrifice, in all you do. Put your trust in the Lord. Comfort yourself with this relationship with Him, that He has mercifully granted to us. 

David writes of the many who have no hope, who don't know the Lord is good to those that love Him. Don't despair as they do. Ask Him for His grace, to shine His light upon us. To look your way. Going to Him in confidence that whatever is His will, it shall be done. Those that trust in the Lord shall not be ashamed. 

Let His light fill you, rejoice. We are more blessed than those of the world. More blessed than those in time of bounty. He has made us His children. He has given us eternal life. Rejoice in that. Be still and know He is faithful. 

*I will both lay me down in peace, and sleep: for thou, LORD, only makest me dwell in safety.* Amen!


And in the dark night, when you call out to Him in prayer, know He is with you, even His Spirit that dwells within you.

## Psalms 5  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-10

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17662

<!-- post 17662 · 2026-07-10 06:15 · scripture -->

**Psalms 5**
*Give ear to my words, O LORD, consider my meditation.*

*2 Hearken unto the voice of my cry, my King, and my God: for unto thee will I pray.*

*3 My voice shalt thou hear in the morning, O LORD; in the morning will I direct my prayer unto thee, and will look up.*

*4 For thou art not a God that hath pleasure in wickedness: neither shall evil dwell with thee.*

*5 The foolish shall not stand in thy sight: thou hatest all workers of iniquity.*

*6 Thou shalt destroy them that speak leasing: the LORD will abhor the bloody and deceitful man.*

*7 But as for me, I will come into thy house in the multitude of thy mercy: and in thy fear will I worship toward thy holy temple.*

*8 Lead me, O LORD, in thy righteousness because of mine enemies; make thy way straight before my face.*

*9 For there is no faithfulness in their mouth; their inward part is very wickedness; their throat is an open sepulchre; they flatter with their tongue.*

*10 Destroy thou them, O God; let them fall by their own counsels; cast them out in the multitude of their transgressions; for they have rebelled against thee.*

*11 But let all those that put their trust in thee rejoice: let them ever shout for joy, because thou defendest them: let them also that love thy name be joyful in thee.*

*12 For thou, LORD, wilt bless the righteous; with favour wilt thou compass him as with a shield.*

<!-- post 17663 · 2026-07-10 06:39 · study -->

The song of **Psalm 5** opens again with prayer, asking the Lord to hear and harken to the singer. David, the psalmist, King of Israel calls out to *his* King and his God. 

Many of the Psalms open with this plea, and serve as a model for prayer. "Lord, my God, hear me" is an excellent start any time you go boldly before the Throne. *Unto Thee will I pray.*

And as **Psalm 4** was about praying in the night before sleeping, here in **Psalm 5** it is prayer to start the morning. Day and night, we should be in prayer to our Father. Directing our prayer to Him. 

**Matthew 6:9–10**
*After this manner therefore pray ye: Our Father which art in heaven, Hallowed be thy name. Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven.*

Hallowed be His name. He is holy, and hates the wicked, the foolish, the workers of iniquity. 

We see imprecatory cursing of the liars and the bloody deceitful men, followed by the pledge to submit to God's mercy, and in fear worship Him in His house. 

Then, the plea to show us His will, that the Lord's paths be made known to us, by His righteousness and in the face of our enemies seeking to destroy us, asking that God's will be done. 

The enemies of God are wicked, and their throat, an open sepulchre, an open grave, giving off the stench of their own corruption. 

Jesus uses this term so effectively in **Matthew 23** against the Pharisees.

**Matthew 23:27–28**
*Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye are like unto whited sepulchres, which indeed appear beautiful outward, but are within full of dead men’s bones, and of all uncleanness. Even so ye also outwardly appear righteous unto men, but within ye are full of hypocrisy and iniquity.*

He curses these workers of iniquity, in terms they know from scripture. The death their words bring forth to those that follow them and their lies, Jesus will say to them, begone, *I never knew you.* 

The Psalmist asks God to destroy these people that rise against the Lord and against truth. To bring them down by their own wicked plans, to cast them out in their rebellion against God. 

But, for those that trust in the Lord, rejoice. Shout for joy! Let those that love His precious name, who love Jesus, forever shout for joy, because the Lord defends us from the wicked. And redeemed us from our sin in His mercy and grace. 

*For thou, LORD, wilt bless the righteous; with favour wilt thou compass him as with a shield.* Amen.

## Psalms 6  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-11

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17665

<!-- post 17665 · 2026-07-11 06:22 · scripture -->

**Psalms 6**
*O LORD, rebuke me not in thine anger, neither chasten me in thy hot displeasure.*

*2 Have mercy upon me, O LORD; for I am weak: O LORD, heal me; for my bones are vexed.*

*3 My soul is also sore vexed: but thou, O LORD, how long?*

*4 Return, O LORD, deliver my soul: oh save me for thy mercies’ sake.*

*5 For in death there is no remembrance of thee: in the grave who shall give thee thanks?*

*6 I am weary with my groaning; all the night make I my bed to swim; I water my couch with my tears.*

*7 Mine eye is consumed because of grief; it waxeth old because of all mine enemies.*

*8 Depart from me, all ye workers of iniquity; for the LORD hath heard the voice of my weeping.*

*9 The LORD hath heard my supplication; the LORD will receive my prayer.*

*10 Let all mine enemies be ashamed and sore vexed: let them return and be ashamed suddenly.*

<!-- post 17666 · 2026-07-11 06:43 · study -->

**Psalm 6** continues the prayer and plea theme, with David now up all night with sweat and tears and crying out to the Lord for mercy and for the destruction of his enemies. 

Appealing to the mercies of the Lord, to not rebuke us in anger, to not chasten us while He is hot and fiery in His wrath, is wisdom that all Christians should take heed of. 

When we sin, when we have been goaded into a reaction by the temptations in our lives, our first instinct should be to ask God to forgive us. 

The Devil knows our weaknesses. Our enemies know what buttons to push. How to frustrate us and to get us to react. We are weak. We need God to heal us. 

David is crying out that his body and soul are vexed and he is in real pain, and only the Lord can heal him. 

Before anything else, the Psalmist has prioritized calling out to God, humbling himself spiritually, physically and mentally, admitting fault and decency on God. This should be our starting position in prayer. 

Praise, humility, mercy, then judgment.

Jesus taught us to pray; lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil. To bring an end to the tests and trials, and deliver us from the evil set against us.

Many of us may use prayer as a means of complaining, to gripe to God for whatever trouble there is in our life, and to whine about it being unfair that we are suffering at all. I think all Christians can fall into that during our despair. But the problem with this is it lacks the self-reflection and humility, and acknowledgement that we are to blame for many of our own problems. Begging for forgiveness, and forgiving others, recognizing our sin before God, and then asking for relief from His chastening puts the matter right in our heart. 

And so, settling this, David transitions to judgment, asking how long will the Lord allow David's enemies to vex him? How long will the trials and temptations continue?

<!-- post 17667 · 2026-07-11 06:47 · study -->

One of the effective means of prayer taught in Psalms, and reinforced by Jesus, is importunity. Persistent prayer. David will frequently ask the Lord to deliver his enemies and ask how long must he suffer the affliction in his life. There is nothing wrong with persistently going to God and asking for His resolution to the matter to come quickly. 

**Luke 18:1–8**
*And he spake a parable unto them to this end, that men ought always to pray, and not to faint; Saying, There was in a city a judge, which feared not God, neither regarded man: And there was a widow in that city; and she came unto him, saying, Avenge me of mine adversary. And he would not for a while: but afterward he said within himself, Though I fear not God, nor regard man; Yet because this widow troubleth me, I will avenge her, lest by her continual coming she weary me. And the Lord said, Hear what the unjust judge saith. And shall not God avenge his own elect, which cry day and night unto him, though he bear long with them? I tell you that he will avenge them speedily. Nevertheless when the Son of man cometh, shall he find faith on the earth?*

We should not be afraid to bother the Lord with long-standing issues, be it health, or work, or enemies or anything else. Praying for resolution of a matter, while understanding that His will be done, and resigning yourself for whatever the end of the matter is, is crucial in the Christian prayer life. 

And often, settling it in your heart to be glad with whatever God has given you in terms of an answer will lead to Him blessing you with yet a better alternative or outcome. 

I have personally experienced this. I have witnessed it in others. Persistent prayer and genuine gratitude for whatever His will is, can often work miracles and bring the Lord to not only give you mercy, but shower you with His grace.

<!-- post 17668 · 2026-07-11 06:59 · study -->

*Return, O LORD, deliver my soul: oh save me for thy mercies’ sake. For in death there is no remembrance of thee: in the grave who shall give thee thanks?*

Here, David appeals to the promise of God to save us. To remind God of His word shows you actually know it, and trust in it. I think this is pleasing to the Lord.

He means the physical death here, but it has the same application spiritually. How are we to praise Him, if we are dead? How can we show the world His mercies if His promises aren't kept. 

I wonder how many appeals to the throne David sat though, that showed him this pattern of petition. 

Letting God know how weary it has become, how the vexation has worn you down, is also an effective means of working it through yourself. 

David says he is weary with his groaning. He's sick of hearing his own laments. It's all he thinks about, and it is destroying him. The desperate plea to God appeals to His love for us. 

He finishes this Psalm as he has others, letting his enemies know his faith in the Lord is unwavering and his confidence in God is strong. 

No matter how weary we are, no matter how long we are vexed, no matter how long our temptations, we know we have already overcome in Christ. 

The Lord has heard our plea. He promised, and so we know He has. He will receive our prayers. Our enemies will know He is with us, when we keep these models of prayer in mind, and humble ourselves to God's will. He will not make us ashamed.

## Psalms 7  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-14

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17671

<!-- post 17671 · 2026-07-14 05:47 · scripture -->

**Psalms 7**
*O LORD my God, in thee do I put my trust: save me from all them that persecute me, and deliver me:*

*2 Lest he tear my soul like a lion, rending it in pieces, while there is none to deliver.*

*3 O LORD my God, if I have done this; if there be iniquity in my hands;*

*4 If I have rewarded evil unto him that was at peace with me; (yea, I have delivered him that without cause is mine enemy:)*

*5 Let the enemy persecute my soul, and take it; yea, let him tread down my life upon the earth, and lay mine honour in the dust. Selah.*

*6 Arise, O LORD, in thine anger, lift up thyself because of the rage of mine enemies: and awake for me to the judgment that thou hast commanded.*

*7 So shall the congregation of the people compass thee about: for their sakes therefore return thou on high.*

*8 The LORD shall judge the people: judge me, O LORD, according to my righteousness, and according to mine integrity that is in me.*

*9 Oh let the wickedness of the wicked come to an end; but establish the just: for the righteous God trieth the hearts and reins.*

*10 My defence is of God, which saveth the upright in heart.*

*11 God judgeth the righteous, and God is angry with the wicked every day.*

*12 If he turn not, he will whet his sword; he hath bent his bow, and made it ready.*

*13 He hath also prepared for him the instruments of death; he ordaineth his arrows against the persecutors.*

*14 Behold, he travaileth with iniquity, and hath conceived mischief, and brought forth falsehood.*

*15 He made a pit, and digged it, and is fallen into the ditch which he made.*

*16 His mischief shall return upon his own head, and his violent dealing shall come down upon his own pate.*

*17 I will praise the LORD according to his righteousness: and will sing praise to the name of the LORD most high.*

<!-- post 17672 · 2026-07-14 06:07 · study -->

Again, David writes a playful song of faith in God to deliver him from evil. And again, the pattern of praise, plea, confession, and confidence in the Lord is present. 

Because of their nature, the Psalms are dripping with doctrine. Here we see the faith and confidence in God lends itself to appealing to His mercy and holiness. We learn that we should ask Him to search us. To show us what sins we have in our life, that we may be cleansed and pleasing to Him. 

David sings out to the Lord to allow the enemies that compass him about to destroy him, if there is anything he had done to deserve this. 

When we are being persecuted or falling into diverse temptations, tests and trials, self-examination should be on our mind. Dealing honestly with ourselves and with God, that maybe it is the result of something we have done. Chastening of the Lord for iniquity in our dealings with others. Sins against the Father. Confession is rewarded by God. He loves an honest soul, humbly seeking Him for forgiveness. 

So, like David, call out in crisis, but be willing to accept our role in our calamity, and have a clean conscience towards God. Ask Him to judge you according to your integrity and righteousness, that we don't deceive ourselves. That we don't fall into a victim mentality. 

Pray for God's judgment, not only upon you, but your enemies. That His will be done on earth, and upon all people, that He will stand up the righteous and lay low the wicked. Knowing that our defense is of God. 

*God judgeth the righteous, and God is angry with the wicked every day.* This important doctrine is a necessary one, to develop a healthy fear of the Lord, the beginning of wisdom.

The same wisdom here that we find so often in David's son, Solomon and his Proverbs. 

That those that deal in iniquity will fall victim to their own mischief. The pits they dig for others to fall in will swallow them up. His violence come back upon his own head.

This expectation of God's judgment is a key portion of our faith. Not only the Lord's mercy upon His children, but His wrath on evil-doers. 

God is love. But God is also burning vengeance. His anger is kindled and wrath is stored against the wicked. 

**Romans 2:2–6**
*But we are sure that the judgment of God is according to truth against them which commit such things. And thinkest thou this, O man, that judgest them which do such things, and doest the same, that thou shalt escape the judgment of God? Or despisest thou the riches of his goodness and forbearance and longsuffering; not knowing that the goodness of God leadeth thee to repentance? But after thy hardness and impenitent heart treasurest up unto thyself wrath against the day of wrath and revelation of the righteous judgment of God; Who will render to every man according to his deeds*

This truth is as important for understanding God as His love. And is why He deserves our praise as we petition Him for our deliverance. 

*I will praise the LORD according to his righteousness: and will sing praise to the name of the LORD most high.* Amen

<!-- post 17674 · 2026-07-15 06:45 · study -->

**Psalms 7:6–9**
*Arise, O LORD, in thine anger, lift up thyself because of the rage of mine enemies: and awake for me to the judgment that thou hast commanded. So shall the congregation of the people compass thee about: for their sakes therefore return thou on high. The LORD shall judge the people: judge me, O LORD, according to my righteousness, and according to mine integrity that is in me. Oh let the wickedness of the wicked come to an end; but establish the just: for the righteous God trieth the hearts and reins.*

This passage in **Psalms 7** is prophetic to the day of the Lord, Jesus' second coming, the rapture and the wrath. 

The congregation of the people, the resurrection of the saints, gathered together in the air to meet Him at His glorious coming. 

**Matthew 24:27–31**
*For as the lightning cometh out of the east, and shineth even unto the west; so shall also the coming of the Son of man be. For wheresoever the carcase is, there will the eagles be gathered together. Immediately after the tribulation of those days shall the sun be darkened, and the moon shall not give her light, and the stars shall fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens shall be shaken: And then shall appear the sign of the Son of man in heaven: and then shall all the tribes of the earth mourn, and they shall see the Son of man coming in the clouds of heaven with power and great glory. And he shall send his angels with a great sound of a trumpet, and they shall gather together his elect from the four winds, from one end of heaven to the other.*

As eagles, carrion birds, circle a carcass, so will the risen saints "compass about" Jesus, as we meet Him in the air. When He returns with His own, the spirits of the dead, raising them first, and then those which are alive and remain:

**1 Thessalonians 4:14–17**
*For if we believe that Jesus died and rose again, even so them also which sleep in Jesus will God bring with him. For this we say unto you by the word of the Lord, that we which are alive and remain unto the coming of the Lord shall not prevent them which are asleep. For the Lord himself shall descend from heaven with a shout, with the voice of the archangel, and with the trump of God: and the dead in Christ shall rise first: Then we which are alive and remain shall be caught up together with them in the clouds, to meet the Lord in the air: and so shall we ever be with the Lord.*

David looked forward to this day of judgement, the day of the Lord. The day of redemption.

## Psalms 8  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-17

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17683

<!-- post 17683 · 2026-07-17 05:37 · scripture -->

**Psalm 8**
*O LORD, our Lord, how excellent is thy name in all the earth! who hast set thy glory above the heavens.*

*2 Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast thou ordained strength because of thine enemies, that thou mightest still the enemy and the avenger.*

*3 When I consider thy heavens, the work of thy fingers, the moon and the stars, which thou hast ordained;*

*4 What is man, that thou art mindful of him? and the son of man, that thou visitest him?*

*5 For thou hast made him a little lower than the angels, and hast crowned him with glory and honour.*

*6 Thou madest him to have dominion over the works of thy hands; thou hast put all things under his feet:*

*7 All sheep and oxen, yea, and the beasts of the field;*

*8 The fowl of the air, and the fish of the sea, and whatsoever passeth through the paths of the seas.*

*9 O LORD our Lord, how excellent is thy name in all the earth!*

<!-- post 17684 · 2026-07-17 06:05 · study -->

The excellent name of our Lord is Jesus. 

**Psalm 8** is a song of praise for the Christ. The name of the Lord, who has been set by the LORD, God the Father, over all things. Who also is God. 

Jesus is the anointed, Who is ordained to strength, and mighty. 

**Hebrews 2:4–9**
*God also bearing them witness, both with signs and wonders, and with divers miracles, and gifts of the Holy Ghost, according to his own will? For unto the angels hath he not put in subjection the world to come, whereof we speak. But one in a certain place testified, saying, What is man, that thou art mindful of him? or the son of man, that thou visitest him? Thou madest him a little lower than the angels; thou crownedst him with glory and honour, and didst set him over the works of thy hands: Thou hast put all things in subjection under his feet. For in that he put all in subjection under him, he left nothing that is not put under him. But now we see not yet all things put under him. But we see Jesus, who was made a little lower than the angels for the suffering of death, crowned with glory and honour; that he by the grace of God should taste death for every man.*

The New Testament reveals the intent and interpretation of the Old. We see Jesus using Psalm 8:2 against the Pharisees, showing that He is the name of God;

**Matthew 21:15–17**
*And when the chief priests and scribes saw the wonderful things that he did, and the children crying in the temple, and saying, Hosanna to the son of David; they were sore displeased, And said unto him, Hearest thou what these say? And Jesus saith unto them, Yea; have ye never read, Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings thou hast perfected praise? And he left them, and went out of the city into Bethany; and he lodged there*.

Through His name was all creation made:

**Colossians 1:16–17**
*For by him were all things created, that are in heaven, and that are in earth, visible and invisible, whether they be thrones, or dominions, or principalities, or powers: all things were created by him, and for him: And he is before all things, and by him all things consist.*

**John 1:1–3**
*In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God. The same was in the beginning with God. All things were made by him; and without him was not any thing made that was made.*

*The same was in the beginning*, when men first called upon the name of the Lord.

**Genesis 4:25–26**
*And Adam knew his wife again; and she bare a son, and called his name Seth: For God, said she, hath appointed me another seed instead of Abel, whom Cain slew. And to Seth, to him also there was born a son; and he called his name Enos: then began men to call upon the name of the LORD.*

Jesus has been made to have dominion over all things, that the glory and honor be given to Him. 

**Ephesians 1:9–10**
*Having made known unto us the mystery of his will, according to his good pleasure which he hath purposed in himself: That in the dispensation of the fulness of times he might gather together in one all things in Christ, both which are in heaven, and which are on earth; even in him*

And the excellency of His name is revealed in Christ Jesus. 

**Philippians 2:9–11**
*Wherefore God also hath highly exalted him, and given him a name which is above every name: That at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth; And that every tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is Lord, to the glory of God the Father.*

Jesus is the name of the Lord. Of the great I Am. 

**John 8:56–58**
*Your father Abraham rejoiced to see my day: and he saw it, and was glad. Then said the Jews unto him, Thou art not yet fifty years old, and hast thou seen Abraham? Jesus said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, Before Abraham was, I am.*

No other name is given by God to be worshipped as God. 

**Acts 4:12**
*Neither is there salvation in any other: for there is none other name under heaven given among men, whereby we must be saved.*

<!-- post 17685 · 2026-07-17 06:27 · study -->

Jerusalem, was chosen by God to "place His name";

**Deuteronomy 12:5, 11**
*But unto the place which the LORD your God shall choose out of all your tribes to put his name there, even unto his habitation shall ye seek, and thither thou shalt come...Then there shall be a place which the LORD your God shall choose to cause his name to dwell there; thither shall ye bring all that I command you; your burnt offerings, and your sacrifices, your tithes, and the heave offering of your hand, and all your choice vows which ye vow unto the LORD*

The OT scriptures repeatedly say that Jerusalem is where God chose to place His name. Where the Son of God would be revealed. Where His name would be killed for our transgressions:

**Isaiah 53:3–5**
*He is despised and rejected of men; a man of sorrows, and acquainted with grief: and we hid as it were our faces from him; he was despised, and we esteemed him not. Surely he hath borne our griefs, and carried our sorrows: yet we did esteem him stricken, smitten of God, and afflicted. But he was wounded for our transgressions, he was bruised for our iniquities: the chastisement of our peace was upon him; and with his stripes we are healed.*

The same place Abraham was told to bring Isaac, at Mount Moriah, Zion:

**Genesis 22:2**
*And he said, Take now thy son, thine only son Isaac, whom thou lovest, and get thee into the land of Moriah; and offer him there for a burnt offering upon one of the mountains which I will tell thee of.*

But God did not require the sacrifice of man, but rather gave His only begotten Son:

**Genesis 22:8**
*And Abraham said, My son, God will provide himself a lamb for a burnt offering: so they went both of them together.*

In this place, where the altar was made, and the son was to be sacrificed, where a ram without blemish was caught in the thick of thorns was provided, Abraham recognized it for what it was:

**Genesis 22:14**
*And Abraham called the name of that place Jehovahjireh: as it is said to this day, In the mount of the LORD it shall be seen.*

The place where the temple would be, pointing to the Messiah:


**1 Kings 8:28–29**
*Yet have thou respect unto the prayer of thy servant, and to his supplication, O LORD my God, to hearken unto the cry and to the prayer, which thy servant prayeth before thee to day: That thine eyes may be open toward this house night and day, even toward the place of which thou hast said, My name shall be there: that thou mayest hearken unto the prayer which thy servant shall make toward this place.*

God says that His promise to David that the anointed one would come from his line, and His determined use of Jerusalem as the city where He would place His name, is the only reason He did not wipe out Judah as He did Israel. 

**Isaiah 37:34–35**
*By the way that he came, by the same shall he return, and shall not come into this city, saith the LORD. For I will defend this city to save it for mine own sake, and for my servant David’s sake.*

**Zechariah 9:9**
*Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; shout, O daughter of Jerusalem: behold, thy King cometh unto thee: he is just, and having salvation; lowly, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass.*

There, in Jerusalem would He place His name, Christ Jesus. The Alpha and Omega. The Lord of lords and King of kings. 

**Isaiah 9:6**
*For unto us a child is born, unto us a son is given: and the government shall be upon his shoulder: and his name shall be called Wonderful, Counsellor, The mighty God, The everlasting Father, The Prince of Peace.*

<!-- post 17686 · 2026-07-17 06:33 · study -->

The entire Old Testament is about Jesus, the Name of the Lord. And so when we see in Moses and the prophets and the Psalms, when they speak of the name of the Lord, we know that it is Jesus.

**Luke 24:44–45**
*And he said unto them, These are the words which I spake unto you, while I was yet with you, that all things must be fulfilled, which were written in the law of Moses, and in the prophets, and in the psalms, concerning me. Then opened he their understanding, that they might understand the scriptures*

**Acts 10:43**
*To him give all the prophets witness, that through his name whosoever believeth in him shall receive remission of sins.*

This witness is true, and revealed now, in the last times. That the name of the Lord is Jesus. Amen

<!-- post 17687 · 2026-07-17 06:42 · study -->

**Psalm 8** as a song of praise and worship for the Christ, the Messiah, Who was made a little lower than the angels, and was raised into glory, is a beautiful and heartfelt prayer. 

When we consider all that the Lord has created and look upon ourselves, we stand in awe that God's grace would ever extend to us. That He would visit us at all, let alone become man and die for our sins. 

**Hebrews 12:2–3**
*Looking unto Jesus the author and finisher of our faith; who for the joy that was set before him endured the cross, despising the shame, and is set down at the right hand of the throne of God. For consider him that endured such contradiction of sinners against himself, lest ye be wearied and faint in your minds.*

The awe we should feel in consideration of His grace and mercy - that He became as man, lived, tempted at all points as we are yet without sin, and then despised and brutalized for our sins, and raised again onto glory, that His name be exalted above all - is beautifully summarized in this brief song. 

*O LORD our Lord, how excellent is thy name in all the earth!*

## Psalms 9  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-24

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17693

<!-- post 17693 · 2026-07-24 08:27 · scripture -->

**Psalms 9**
*I will praise thee, O LORD, with my whole heart; I will shew forth all thy marvellous works.*

*2 I will be glad and rejoice in thee: I will sing praise to thy name, O thou most High.*

*3 When mine enemies are turned back, they shall fall and perish at thy presence.*

*4 For thou hast maintained my right and my cause; thou satest in the throne judging right.*

*5 Thou hast rebuked the heathen, thou hast destroyed the wicked, thou hast put out their name for ever and ever.*

*6 O thou enemy, destructions are come to a perpetual end: and thou hast destroyed cities; their memorial is perished with them.*

*7 But the LORD shall endure for ever: he hath prepared his throne for judgment.*

*8 And he shall judge the world in righteousness, he shall minister judgment to the people in uprightness.*

*9 The LORD also will be a refuge for the oppressed, a refuge in times of trouble.*

*10 And they that know thy name will put their trust in thee: for thou, LORD, hast not forsaken them that seek thee.*

*11 Sing praises to the LORD, which dwelleth in Zion: declare among the people his doings.*

*12 When he maketh inquisition for blood, he remembereth them: he forgetteth not the cry of the humble.*

*13 Have mercy upon me, O LORD; consider my trouble which I suffer of them that hate me, thou that liftest me up from the gates of death:*

*14 That I may shew forth all thy praise in the gates of the daughter of Zion: I will rejoice in thy salvation.*

*15 The heathen are sunk down in the pit that they made: in the net which they hid is their own foot taken.*

*16 The LORD is known by the judgment which he executeth: the wicked is snared in the work of his own hands. Higgaion. Selah.*

*17 The wicked shall be turned into hell, and all the nations that forget God.*

*18 For the needy shall not alway be forgotten: the expectation of the poor shall not perish for ever.*

*19 Arise, O LORD; let not man prevail: let the heathen be judged in thy sight.*

*20 Put them in fear, O LORD: that the nations may know themselves to be but men. Selah.*

<!-- post 17694 · 2026-07-24 08:48 · study -->

**Psalm 9** continues the prayerful praise of the Lord, the call for His righteous judgment, destruction of enemies, rebuke of the heathen, and the eternal salvation to come. 

The first of the two great commandments, *Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind*, is reflected in the praise and worship of David's song. Praising God with our whole heart, acknowledging all His wonderful and marvelous works, rejoicing in them. 

Note, David is beset by enemies, his cause is right, yet many are against him, he is oppressed, and yet, David begins with praise and rejoicing. 

It's been said before, but Jesus does not take away our burdens and cares, He simply makes those burdens light. His yoke is easy. When we are buffeted by the cares and worries of this world, we should stop and praise God for all He has done. 

Gratitude and praise should be in the front of our minds and love for Him in our hearts, because in the end, His grace, His saving us is sufficient. We are on the winning side. We have overcome the world in Jesus. So sing praise to His name, *O Thou most High.* 

*When mine enemies are turned back*, not if, but when, when they are destroyed by His presence, it is because His enemies are ours. 

**Matthew 5:43–44**
*Ye have heard that it hath been said, Thou shalt love thy neighbour, and hate thine enemy. But I say unto you, Love your enemies, bless them that curse you, do good to them that hate you, and pray for them which despitefully use you, and persecute you*

We are called to love our personal enemies. David had multiple opportunities to kill Saul, yet he spared him, and appealed to the King in love. 

But those that oppress the righteous, and the wicked full of iniquity, God will judge. And we can absolutely pray imprecatory prayers against them, that God will repay. That His vengeance be done, knowing that in the end, there is no escape for the wicked. Their cities will burn, their line will be cut off, there will be no memory of them, and their soul will be in flames of eternal torment. 

What little we can do in our wrath? Better to rejoice in the wrath of God, knowing He will handle it. 

His throne is established forever in righteousness. He will judge. He will take up the cause of the poor and oppressed. Even in great tribulation, it shall be cut short for the elects' sake. 

Because we have placed our trust in His holy name, the Lord Jesus, Who will not forsake those that seek Him. He hears the cry of the humble.

<!-- post 17695 · 2026-07-24 08:56 · study -->

There are so many problems we will have in our life, as men on the earth, and persecutions and attacks added on top of them for believers, if we do the works of God. If we serve Him, we will see trouble. 

How often do we experience or witness the attacks of the devil on believers who turn to God, and do His work? 

I was talking with a younger sister in Christ who just won someone to the Lord, and how awesome that was. Within a few days, she was being afflicted from all sides by multiple travails and trials. 

It was good to be able to give her this perspective. To know to patiently endure it, and see her faith grow. To turn to trusting the Lord to see her through it, and to love on those around her so that they will see her light so shine, that they'll praise God in Heaven. That the unsaved might be persuaded by her chaste conversation. Her behavior in the face of adversity. 

David's story is a wonderful example of how we should view things. His Psalms help sharpen that focus on God when we are really going through it. 

We can humbly ask God for His mercy, and know that He who has lifted us up from the gates of death will save us, while the heathen and enemies of God will be snared by their own sin and turned into Hell. Amen

<!-- post 17696 · 2026-07-24 09:05 · study -->

Brief note on "Higgaion. Selah." 

Higgaion is a Hebrew word that means 'meditation,' or something like it, and seems to be a cue for a musical break, heavy harp, where the singers and congregation considered the words just said. It is used just a couple of times in the Psalms. 

Selah, which is used much more frequently, seems to serve as a pause, perhaps for a musical bridge. 

The definitions are somewhat educated guess, and opinion. I feel that 'selah' is a bit of a 'think about that,' and 'amen' combined. But I am not a musician, and it may seem more of a way to bring the music back around to those that are. 

I think it is more important to look at what's being said, and not get caught up in the pedantic. Selah.

<!-- post 17697 · 2026-07-24 09:14 · study -->

*And he shall judge the world in righteousness, he shall minister judgment to the people in uprightness.*

The Apostle Paul quotes **Psalm 9:8** during the Sermon at Mars Hill, where Paul preaches Christ to the heathen. 

**Acts 17:22–31**
*Then Paul stood in the midst of Mars’ hill, and said, Ye men of Athens, I perceive that in all things ye are too superstitious. For as I passed by, and beheld your devotions, I found an altar with this inscription, TO THE UNKNOWN GOD. Whom therefore ye ignorantly worship, him declare I unto you. God that made the world and all things therein, seeing that he is Lord of heaven and earth, dwelleth not in temples made with hands; Neither is worshipped with men’s hands, as though he needed any thing, seeing he giveth to all life, and breath, and all things; And hath made of one blood all nations of men for to dwell on all the face of the earth, and hath determined the times before appointed, and the bounds of their habitation; That they should seek the Lord, if haply they might feel after him, and find him, though he be not far from every one of us: For in him we live, and move, and have our being; as certain also of your own poets have said, For we are also his offspring. Forasmuch then as we are the offspring of God, we ought not to think that the Godhead is like unto gold, or silver, or stone, graven by art and man’s device. And the times of this ignorance God winked at; but now commandeth all men every where to repent: Because he hath appointed a day, in the which he will judge the world in righteousness by that man whom he hath ordained; whereof he hath given assurance unto all men, in that he hath raised him from the dead.*

The cry of David against the heathen, that those who don't believe in the power of the Lord be destroyed, is turned into a warning by Paul to the heathen of Athens. The time of God winking at their pagan idolatry is over. The judgment is here, because in Christ there is no excuse. 

God was made flesh, died for all men and rose again. That assurance is sure, and every man must come to repentance, trusting on the name of Jesus.

## Psalms 10  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-26

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17698

<!-- post 17698 · 2026-07-26 08:27 · scripture -->

**Psalms 10**
*Why standest thou afar off, O LORD? why hidest thou thyself in times of trouble?*

*2 The wicked in his pride doth persecute the poor: let them be taken in the devices that they have imagined.*

*3 For the wicked boasteth of his heart’s desire, and blesseth the covetous, whom the LORD abhorreth.*

*4 The wicked, through the pride of his countenance, will not seek after God: God is not in all his thoughts.*

*5 His ways are always grievous; thy judgments are far above out of his sight: as for all his enemies, he puffeth at them.*

*6 He hath said in his heart, I shall not be moved: for I shall never be in adversity.*

*7 His mouth is full of cursing and deceit and fraud: under his tongue is mischief and vanity.*

*8 He sitteth in the lurking places of the villages: in the secret places doth he murder the innocent: his eyes are privily set against the poor.*

*9 He lieth in wait secretly as a lion in his den: he lieth in wait to catch the poor: he doth catch the poor, when he draweth him into his net.*

*10 He croucheth, and humbleth himself, that the poor may fall by his strong ones.*

*11 He hath said in his heart, God hath forgotten: he hideth his face; he will never see it.*

*12 Arise, O LORD; O God, lift up thine hand: forget not the humble.*

*13 Wherefore doth the wicked contemn God? he hath said in his heart, Thou wilt not require it.*

*14 Thou hast seen it; for thou beholdest mischief and spite, to requite it with thy hand: the poor committeth himself unto thee; thou art the helper of the fatherless.*

*15 Break thou the arm of the wicked and the evil man: seek out his wickedness till thou find none.*

*16 The LORD is King for ever and ever: the heathen are perished out of his land.*

*17 LORD, thou hast heard the desire of the humble: thou wilt prepare their heart, thou wilt cause thine ear to hear:*

*18 To judge the fatherless and the oppressed, that the man of the earth may no more oppress.*

<!-- post 17699 · 2026-07-26 08:50 · study -->

**Psalm 10** acts as a pair to **Psalm 9**, and a meditation on the wicked, against who David is seeking God's judgment. 

David opens the Psalm feeling like God's delay in judgment is His hiding Himself, standing afar off, allowing it to continue. We may all feel this way at times. We know God hears us, we have experienced His mercy and blessings and seen His wrath revealed from Heaven, that no man has any excuse to not fear Him...yet, His perfect timing can make us impatient. Even as the saints in Heaven, martyred in tribulation cry out;

**Revelation 6:10**
*And they cried with a loud voice, saying, How long, O Lord, holy and true, dost thou not judge and avenge our blood on them that dwell on the earth?*

Obviously, the wrath of God pours out soon after, His vengeance is sure. We can trust it will come. But it is hard for our human minds to wait. 

The wicked, through pride, do not seek God. They boast of their wicked desires, and bless those that join in their covetousness. They persecute the poor with their evil devices. Set snares and nets, dig pits, and scheme mischief against the fatherless and the poor. Their iniquity knows no limit. 

While their murder and violence is obvious, some of the ways of their deceit is less so. 

*He lieth in wait secretly as a lion in his den: he lieth in wait to catch the poor: he doth catch the poor, when he draweth him into his net.*

The subtle ways of the wicked, the nets they spread, are not only to blatantly rob the poor of whatever meager wealth they have, but to trap them in temptation. 

They are the drug dealers, booze peddlers, pimps and whoremongers, pornographers, bookies running gambling parlors, scam artists with get rich quick schemes...all of the ways, big and small that evil men seek to take advantage of those who are struggling, who have no father to guide them, who are desperate to escape their poverty, who are not strong in faith and so are easily caught up in vice. 

The description of these men in verse 9 echos the description of their father, the devil. 

**1 Peter 5:8**
*Be sober, be vigilant; because your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour.*

These men are as the devil, seeking to take men into their maw, and to bring the righteous to falling, that the adversary can accuse the brethren before God. 

David, praying to spur action against these men, reminds God of their pride and arrogant way, dismissing any thought of God making recompense against them. They say that He will not require it, He won't make them pay the wages of their sin. That they mock God, so they deserve swift and immediate recompense. 

David often uses these formulas for the Psalms, praising God, reminding Him Who He is, what He's promised, who the enemy are, what the Lord has done against them in the past, what they deserve now, faith in what He will do to them in the future. 

Because, God is the judge to the fatherless and defender of the oppressed.

<!-- post 17700 · 2026-07-26 09:01 · study -->

The reoccurring theme of the Lord defending the widow, the fatherless and the poor, and our role in carrying out righteous judgment for them, is probably the most talked about doctrine outside of Who God is, and the doctrines of salvation in Christ. 

More than anything else, we are to clothe and feed the truly needy, to make sure people don't take advantage of those who have no one to defend them. No husband, no father. To do unto the least of these, and in doing so, doing it for Jesus. 

**James 1:27**
*Pure religion and undefiled before God and the Father is this, To visit the fatherless and widows in their affliction, and to keep himself unspotted from the world.*

This is the ultimate expression of our faith. The outward practice God expects. Self-sacrifice for others, not the selfish sacrifice of the works-based religions of the world. 

**Hosea 6:6**
*For I desired mercy, and not sacrifice; and the knowledge of God more than burnt offerings*

All the penance and "Eucharist" sacrifices, and all the other rites and rituals men do to get the Lord to be pleased with them are vain. God wants us to exercise mercy and judgment. 

**Proverbs 21:3** 
*To do justice and judgment is more acceptable to the LORD than sacrifice.*

Jesus, reading Isaiah, gives us the mission statement for Christians;

Luke 4:18–19
*The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me to preach the gospel to the poor; he hath sent me to heal the brokenhearted, to preach deliverance to the captives, and recovering of sight to the blind, to set at liberty them that are bruised, To preach the acceptable year of the Lord.*

Amen
