## Psalms 11  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-29

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17701

<!-- post 17701 · 2026-07-29 06:26 · scripture -->

**Psalms 11**
*In the LORD put I my trust: how say ye to my soul, Flee as a bird to your mountain?*

*2 For, lo, the wicked bend their bow, they make ready their arrow upon the string, that they may privily shoot at the upright in heart.*

*3 If the foundations be destroyed, what can the righteous do?*

*4 The LORD is in his holy temple, the LORD’s throne is in heaven: his eyes behold, his eyelids try, the children of men.*

*5 The LORD trieth the righteous: but the wicked and him that loveth violence his soul hateth.*

*6 Upon the wicked he shall rain snares, fire and brimstone, and an horrible tempest: this shall be the portion of their cup.*

*7 For the righteous LORD loveth righteousness; his countenance doth behold the upright.*

<!-- post 17702 · 2026-07-29 07:16 · study -->

**Psalm 11** is a short song addressing a doubtful disputation of an unknown interlocer, someone who may say our faith in the Lord is insufficient to save us from our enemies. 

Our trust is in the Lord. We have no need to fear. To flee. Especially in the end times this Psalm's dual-fulfillment of prophecy, portends. 

I think of all the fairweather Christians who spend their days in fear, prepping to flee to the mountains in the Tribulation. Who plan to flee as a bird to their mountain. 

The wicked seek to shoot their arrows at the hearts of the upright, even now. In the Tribulation, they won't need to do it privily, but will openly seek the lives of the children of God. 

David asks, rhetorically, *If the foundations be destroyed, what can the righteous do?*

If the Lord isn't true to save us, if the foundation of our faith is destroyed, we would of all men be most miserable. 

**1 Corinthians 15:13–17**
*But if there be no resurrection of the dead, then is Christ not risen: And if Christ be not risen, then is our preaching vain, and your faith is also vain. Yea, and we are found false witnesses of God; because we have testified of God that he raised up Christ: whom he raised not up, if so be that the dead rise not. For if the dead rise not, then is not Christ raised: And if Christ be not raised, your faith is vain; ye are yet in your sins.*

Because there is no other foundation upon which we may trust;

**1 Corinthians 3:11**
*For other foundation can no man lay than that is laid, which is Jesus Christ.*

Knowing this sure foundation, and knowing He is risen, we can place our trust in Him. 

We know with certainty the Lord is in His holy Temple, the Lord is on His throne. We know He is the omnipotent judge of men. 

He tries, or tests the righteous to increase our faith. He hates the wicked and the violent, and shall rain down His wrath upon them. This is the portion of their cup. This is a promise of God. 

**Revelation 14:10–11**
*The same shall drink of the wine of the wrath of God, which is poured out without mixture into the cup of his indignation; and he shall be tormented with fire and brimstone in the presence of the holy angels, and in the presence of the Lamb: And the smoke of their torment ascendeth up for ever and ever: and they have no rest day nor night, who worship the beast and his image, and whosoever receiveth the mark of his name.*

But the righteous are before the face of the Lord, always.

<!-- post 17703 · 2026-07-29 07:43 · study -->

I want to take the opportunity here, with such a short and concise Psalm, to deal with the way many modern "theologians" approach the Old Testament, and the Psalms in particular. 

When I read and react to the text, all I see is Jesus, the gospel, and the clear and present application of worship in our lives, and the prophetic nature, which clearly is reflected in New Testament end times scripture. 

Nearly every Psalm is an uplifting song of praise, worship and trust, with the warnings against the wicked and promises of deliverance from their evil, and God's wrath upon them. 

Meanwhile, most studies become hyper fixated on the context David wrote them in, the situation he was facing. Often totally invented by the author (or parroting an earlier study of another man).

Seeking to be wise, they become fools. They want to impress the reader with how clever they are and how their "knowledge of the Hebrew" and extrabiblical sources puts them above the normal believer, who may read the plain text and see themselves, and the future promises of God, as the primary application today. 

A typical example;

"Actually, Psalm 11 is best understood as David’s response to well-meaning but fearful advisers during a time of personal danger, most likely when political enemies or rebellious forces threatened the stability of his rule. The opening dialogue (“Flee as a bird to your mountain”) reflects counsel to seek temporary physical safety amid apparent societal collapse, while David’s reply anchors his confidence in Yahweh’s unchanging character: the Lord remains in His holy temple and on His heavenly throne, testing the righteous and preparing judgment for the wicked. This likely was written during one of the many crises King David faced.   Notably, the Hebrew of verse 3—kî haššātôṯ yēhārēsûn (“for the foundations are being destroyed”)—employs a rare plural form of šātâ that some scholars conjecture may allude to the subterranean pillars of cosmic order rather than mere institutional collapse, thereby elevating the crisis from the merely political to the ontological; yet even this linguistic subtlety serves primarily to underscore David’s immediate existential plight rather than any distant eschatological horizon."

These men miss the forest for the trees, and create this sterile and unrelatable version of the word, buried under jargon, and with little to no edification or doctrine. 

I don't claim to ever exhaust the full meaning and layers of the text in my morning studies. God's word is deep and there are passages that carry a meaning and purpose today, and then read later, convey something totally different. Where the Spirit leads. But when a study becomes an exercise in dazzling the reader with theology, vain philosophy, jargon and concepts that come more from linguistics, semantics and traditions, than the Spirit, I recommend they be avoided.

## Psalms 12  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-07-30

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17704

<!-- post 17704 · 2026-07-30 06:28 · scripture -->

**Psalms 12**
*Help, LORD; for the godly man ceaseth; for the faithful fail from among the children of men.*

*2 They speak vanity every one with his neighbour: with flattering lips and with a double heart do they speak.*

*3 The LORD shall cut off all flattering lips, and the tongue that speaketh proud things:*

*4 Who have said, With our tongue will we prevail; our lips are our own: who is lord over us?*

*5 For the oppression of the poor, for the sighing of the needy, now will I arise, saith the LORD; I will set him in safety from him that puffeth at him.*

*6 The words of the LORD are pure words: as silver tried in a furnace of earth, purified seven times.*

*7 Thou shalt keep them, O LORD, thou shalt preserve them from this generation for ever.*

*8 The wicked walk on every side, when the vilest men are exalted.*

<!-- post 17705 · 2026-07-30 06:46 · study -->

**Psalm 12** opens with the most plaintive cry, yet. Calling upon the Lord, "Help." 

That simple prayer, asking for help during our trials, is perhaps the most often prayed. 

"Help, Lord" because the circumstances have led to humble ourselves before Him. Our admission of weakness allows God to be our strength, and glorifies Him that deserves all glory. 

Even the erstwhile atheist, the fool who says in their heart, "there is no God," cries out for help from the deity they profess doesn't exist. 

We often will ask someone we give the gospel to, have you ever called out to the Lord for help? Because of health, or the health of another. Cry out in fear, because of a crisis or an emergency need has left them no other way to turn, except towards the Lord and His mercy. Or something as simple as a job, or test. Nearly all will say "Yes" if they're being honest. 

From there, it becomes easier to lead them to the truth that they need to call upon the name of the Lord specifically to save them, eternally. To receive forgiveness of sin and everlasting life. 

If man struggles with the things of this world on his own, how much more do we need help for the spiritual things of God? 

Have you cried out to the Lord? Have you come before God, unable to go on, let alone articulate the need you are petitioning, beyond a simple "Help?" 

Have you called upon the Lord for salvation?

<!-- post 17706 · 2026-07-30 07:14 · study -->

David's plea for help, like many of the Psalms, comes from the vexation of spirit in dealing with the wicked of this world. That in a world where believers, Christians, are called to be separated and holy as He is holy, the fallen world full of the iniquity of man troubles us. It is the lot, no pun intended, of the believer.

**2 Peter 2:6–9**
*And turning the cities of Sodom and Gomorrha into ashes condemned them with an overthrow, making them an ensample unto those that after should live ungodly; And delivered just Lot, vexed with the filthy conversation of the wicked: (For that righteous man dwelling among them, in seeing and hearing, vexed his righteous soul from day to day with their unlawful deeds;) The Lord knoweth how to deliver the godly out of temptations, and to reserve the unjust unto the day of judgment to be punished*

This is a feeling we can all relate to, if you walk in the Spirit. 

The vexation is not just bearing witness to the unlawful deeds of the wicked, but in *hearing* them as well. 

David says they speak vanity to each other, and flatter, and with a double-heart, speak lies. They pridefully boast. It is the wickedness of words that sees David cry out. 

**Matthew 12:34–37**
*O generation of vipers, how can ye, being evil, speak good things? for out of the abundance of the heart the mouth speaketh. A good man out of the good treasure of the heart bringeth forth good things: and an evil man out of the evil treasure bringeth forth evil things. But I say unto you, That every idle word that men shall speak, they shall give account thereof in the day of judgment. For by thy words thou shalt be justified, and by thy words thou shalt be condemned.*

Jesus shows us that words are the window to the heart of a man. An evil heart brings forth evil words, and it is those words that condemn them. 

**Matthew 15:10–11**
*And he called the multitude, and said unto them, Hear, and understand: Not that which goeth into the mouth defileth a man; but that which cometh out of the mouth, this defileth a man.*

This understanding, that it wasn't the ritual washings and dietary laws, cleaning the outside of the cup -but the heart, and what proceeds out from it though the mouth, that makes a man unclean, is often neglected by Christians, today. But one of the most repeated commands in scripture is to watch what you say. 

Proverbs is full of advice to remain silent, to not speak like the fool or the froward. To speak truth in all things. To refrain from vain, useless speech. 

But the wicked refuse to hear God's word and follow His way. Men *Who have said, With our tongue will we prevail; our lips are our own: who is lord over us?*

Of course, by admitting they have power over their own tongue, they condemn themselves.

<!-- post 17707 · 2026-07-30 07:22 · study -->

James, the half-brother of Christ, writes in his epistle, about the importance of controlling your tongue. 

**James 3:2–8**
*For in many things we offend all. If any man offend not in word, the same is a perfect man, and able also to bridle the whole body. Behold, we put bits in the horses’ mouths, that they may obey us; and we turn about their whole body. Behold also the ships, which though they be so great, and are driven of fierce winds, yet are they turned about with a very small helm, whithersoever the governor listeth. Even so the tongue is a little member, and boasteth great things. Behold, how great a matter a little fire kindleth! And the tongue is a fire, a world of iniquity: so is the tongue among our members, that it defileth the whole body, and setteth on fire the course of nature; and it is set on fire of hell. For every kind of beasts, and of birds, and of serpents, and of things in the sea, is tamed, and hath been tamed of mankind: But the tongue can no man tame; it is an unruly evil, full of deadly poison.*

Controlling your speech will go far in controlling your body. We talk ourselves into a multitude of sins. We give cause for the enemies of Christ to blaspheme by our publicly spoken words, far more than our sins done in secret. 

When we curse, profane and use vulgarity, when we lie and double-deal. When we oppress and offend others. When we puff up with vain words and flattering lips. We defile the whole body by that tiny member, the tongue.

<!-- post 17708 · 2026-07-30 07:40 · study -->

*For the oppression of the poor, for the sighing of the needy, now will I arise, saith the LORD; I will set him in safety from him that puffeth at him.*

This is a promise from God Himself. These are His words. 

**2 Peter 1:20–21**
*Knowing this first, that no prophecy of the scripture is of any private interpretation. For the prophecy came not in old time by the will of man: but holy men of God spake as they were moved by the Holy Ghost.*

The psalmist was moved by the holy Ghost to pen the words of the Lord. His promises are our sure foundation. His word settled forever in Heaven. Jesus is the Word made flesh. The promise fulfilled. 

Faith cometh by hearing the word of God. Those that hear and do the things the Lord says are built upon a rock. We live by every word that proceeds from the mouth of God. All scripture is given by inspiration of God. It is the source of all our knowledge and understanding. His precepts give us understanding. We are told to meditate in His word day and night. To bind His words upon our heart. His words are powerful;

**Hebrews 1:1–3**
*God, who at sundry times and in divers manners spake in time past unto the fathers by the prophets, Hath in these last days spoken unto us by his Son, whom he hath appointed heir of all things, by whom also he made the worlds; Who being the brightness of his glory, and the express image of his person, and upholding all things by the word of his power, when he had by himself purged our sins, sat down on the right hand of the Majesty on high*

The word which we preach is the power to save men's souls to all who trust His word:

**Ephesians 1:12–14**
*That we should be to the praise of his glory, who first trusted in Christ. In whom ye also trusted, after that ye heard the word of truth, the gospel of your salvation: in whom also after that ye believed, ye were sealed with that holy Spirit of promise, Which is the earnest of our inheritance until the redemption of the purchased possession, unto the praise of his glory.*

And so, the preservation of God's word for all generations is a divine role. God supernaturally has revealed His word to man in sundry times and diverse manners, and He keeps His word pure. 

*The words of the LORD are pure words: as silver tried in a furnace of earth, purified seven times. Thou shalt keep them, O LORD, thou shalt preserve them from this generation for ever.*

If man needs every word of God to live, as much as bread, man needs access to every word He has given us. 

The blessing of having a consolidated, complete, incorruptible source, an every-word Bible, cannot be overstated. The King James Bible is the every-word source, in English, without error. 

The 6 earlier English attempts, like a refiner's fire. We have the purified silver of the Authorized version. We can know the Lord has preserved His word. We can trust and build upon that sure foundation.

<!-- post 17709 · 2026-07-30 07:50 · study -->

Speaking of the sure word, preserved, modern versions change and corrupt this text. Rather than the promise to keep His words for all generations, they make it about preserving man;

The ESV: You, O Lord, will keep them; you will guard us[a] from this generation forever.

The footnotes cast doubt. The changes remove the Psalm's comparison of the vain words of the wicked with certainty of God's word of promise. 

The Lord promises to hear the sighing of the needy, and to arise to set them that call upon Him in safety. It is that promise that is preserved. And must be, or God is a liar. 

The first thing modern versions do is create doubt and deny the preservation of the inspired word. Just as Satan tempted Eve in the garden, asking "Yea, hath God said?" 

*The wicked walk on every side, when the vilest men are exalted.* 

But the hope is in the help of the Lord, promised to us before the foundation of the world.

## Psalms 13  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-01

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17710

<!-- post 17710 · 2026-08-01 07:02 · scripture -->

**Psalms 13**
*How long wilt thou forget me, O LORD? for ever? how long wilt thou hide thy face from me?*

*2 How long shall I take counsel in my soul, having sorrow in my heart daily? how long shall mine enemy be exalted over me?*

*3 Consider and hear me, O LORD my God: lighten mine eyes, lest I sleep the sleep of death;*

*4 Lest mine enemy say, I have prevailed against him; and those that trouble me rejoice when I am moved.*

*5 But I have trusted in thy mercy; my heart shall rejoice in thy salvation.*

*6 I will sing unto the LORD, because he hath dealt bountifully with me.*

<!-- post 17711 · 2026-08-01 07:20 · study -->

Psalm 13 beautifully captures a very real feeling of the Christian life, the impatient waiting for God's perfect timing, turning into an acknowledgement that He has dealt bountifully with us. 

It can be easy to slip into despair when facing trials. It can be easy for us as humans, weak in the flesh, to see the enemy seemingly triumph. To pray and wait upon the Lord, but feel like He may have forgotten you in regards to your trouble. 

We've discussed the idea that even as martyred saints in Heaven, this impatient call for the Lord to hurry and fulfill His judgment, occurs. How much more can this take hold in our finite flesh? 

As Peter reminds us;

**2 Peter 3:8–9**
*But, beloved, be not ignorant of this one thing, that one day is with the Lord as a thousand years, and a thousand years as one day. The Lord is not slack concerning his promise, as some men count slackness; but is longsuffering to us-ward, not willing that any should perish, but that all should come to repentance*.

God calls upon His faithful to wait. And in that waiting, there is promise as well.

**Isaiah 40:31**
*But they that wait upon the LORD shall renew their strength; they shall mount up with wings as eagles; they shall run, and not be weary; and they shall walk, and not faint.*

In our patience, God gives us added strength. It is part of a ladder of virtue in the Christian life, elevating us to godliness. 

**2 Peter 1:5–8**
*And beside this, giving all diligence, add to your faith virtue; and to virtue knowledge; And to knowledge temperance; and to temperance patience; and to patience godliness; And to godliness brotherly kindness; and to brotherly kindness charity. For if these things be in you, and abound, they make you that ye shall neither be barren nor unfruitful in the knowledge of our Lord Jesus Christ.*

In the end, our prayers for God to act, and reminders that He has promised to tend to our welfare, and bring us peace from our enemies must inevitably lead to our recognition of His mercies and blessings. Of His salvation. 

Because, as we call out to Him, it is impossible to not remember the very reason we are trusting in Him, beginning with eternal life granted through Jesus. And with the heavenly perspective of God's grace, it is incredibly difficult to continue in complaint. *Because he hath dealt bountifully with* us. We've already received more than we could ever deserve.

<!-- post 17712 · 2026-08-01 07:37 · study -->

**2 Corinthians 12:7–11**
*And lest I should be exalted above measure through the abundance of the revelations, there was given to me a thorn in the flesh, the messenger of Satan to buffet me, lest I should be exalted above measure. For this thing I besought the Lord thrice, that it might depart from me. And he said unto me, My grace is sufficient for thee: for my strength is made perfect in weakness. Most gladly therefore will I rather glory in my infirmities, that the power of Christ may rest upon me. Therefore I take pleasure in infirmities, in reproaches, in necessities, in persecutions, in distresses for Christ’s sake: for when I am weak, then am I strong. I am become a fool in glorying; ye have compelled me: for I ought to have been commended of you: for in nothing am I behind the very chiefest apostles, though I be nothing.*

It's also good to remember that, if a prayer remains "unanswered" by our measure, it very well may be answered in His. That His grace is sufficient. We've already been spared, hell and deaf, and given the glory of eternal life in Christ. 

Remember the Israelites, who when delivered out of Egypt and bondage, complained incessantly in the wilderness. They complained about a lack of water. They complained about the daunting task of taking the land, despite God promising to drive the inhabitants out before them. They believed the bad spies report, and ignored Joshua and Caleb. They complained about mana, and they complained about quail. And God had to constantly remind them that He had delivered them in the first place. 

Sometimes if we feel the need to complain to God, we should remember that he's delivered us in the first place. That His grace is sufficient. Reminding ourselves of what we're going through currently is nothing in comparison to the abundance we've already received. 

**Romans 8:14–18**
*For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, they are the sons of God. For ye have not received the spirit of bondage again to fear; but ye have received the Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry, Abba, Father. The Spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God: And if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together. For I reckon that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed in us.*

Keeping that eternal perspective gives us faith in the Lord's of ability to deliver us. The suffering of this present time is nothing. Life is but a vapor. 

So, having persistent prayer and letting God know how you feel while waiting for His perfect timing to occur is fine. But keep that perspective in mind. And understand that patience builds godliness. And everything to His glory, amen.

## Psalms 14  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-02

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17713

<!-- post 17713 · 2026-08-02 10:19 · scripture -->

**Psalms 14**
*The fool hath said in his heart, There is no God. They are corrupt, they have done abominable works, there is none that doeth good.*

*2 The LORD looked down from heaven upon the children of men, to see if there were any that did understand, and seek God.*

*3 They are all gone aside, they are all together become filthy: there is none that doeth good, no, not one.*

*4 Have all the workers of iniquity no knowledge? who eat up my people as they eat bread, and call not upon the LORD.*

*5 There were they in great fear: for God is in the generation of the righteous.*

*6 Ye have shamed the counsel of the poor, because the LORD is his refuge.*

*7 Oh that the salvation of Israel were come out of Zion! when the LORD bringeth back the captivity of his people, Jacob shall rejoice, and Israel shall be glad.*

<!-- post 17714 · 2026-08-02 10:26 · study -->

The opening 3 verses of **Psalm 14** stand as the definitive and true statements of the doctrine that all men have sinned and falled short of the glory of God. Quoted in part by Paul in **Romans 3:10–12**
*As it is written, There is none righteous, no, not one: There is none that understandeth, there is none that seeketh after God. They are all gone out of the way, they are together become unprofitable; there is none that doeth good, no, not one*.

It serves as a universal appeal to the need for the gospel, and the reconciliation of fallen men with a holy God. There is none good but God. All have gone astray. 

The opening verse is an even deeper truth, showing the self-deception that leads fools to claim there is no God. They may say this, but ultimately every man knows that God is, and the judge of all things. 

Like the scribes and Pharisees that Jesus preaches against in **Matthew 23**, these fools, the workers of iniquity, devour the poor. They eat up the people as bread. 

They call not upon the Lord. They shame the counsel of the poor, they profane the hope of the righteous, they deny the Lord that bought them.

<!-- post 17715 · 2026-08-02 17:24 · study -->

*Oh that the salvation of Israel were come out of Zion! when the LORD bringeth back the captivity of his people, Jacob shall rejoice, and Israel shall be glad.*

Again, the eschatological application is clearly seen in the text, and the prophetic words of David's prayer is ultimately about Jesus. 

He is the salvation come out of Zion, and at His coming, Jesus brings all the risen saints, death and Hell defeated. 

This was the hope David had. And the hope that God's judgment and resurrection of the righteous and wrath to the wicked, was the refuge of the poor and the oppressed. 

This is our future, rejoice.

## Psalms 15  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-03

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17716

<!-- post 17716 · 2026-08-03 05:55 · scripture -->

**Psalms 15**
*Lord, who shall abide in thy tabernacle? who shall dwell in thy holy hill?*

*2 He that walketh uprightly, and worketh righteousness, and speaketh the truth in his heart.*

*3 He that backbiteth not with his tongue, nor doeth evil to his neighbour, nor taketh up a reproach against his neighbour.*

*4 In whose eyes a vile person is contemned; but he honoureth them that fear the LORD. He that sweareth to his own hurt, and changeth not.*

*5 He that putteth not out his money to usury, nor taketh reward against the innocent. He that doeth these things shall never be moved.*

<!-- post 17717 · 2026-08-03 06:07 · study -->

The Christian life is one of separation. Of leaving behind the things of this world, and the wickedness men do, separated unto His gospel, and proclaiming His truth. 

Epistles in the New Testament show us examples of godly living. Admonishments against the behavior that the lost may do, but we should strive to reject even the company of those that behave as the heathen. 

**1 Corinthians 5:11**
*But now I have written unto you not to keep company, if any man that is called a brother be a fornicator, or covetous, or an idolater, or a railer, or a drunkard, or an extortioner; with such an one no not to eat.*

**2 Corinthians 12:19–21**
*Again, think ye that we excuse ourselves unto you? we speak before God in Christ: but we do all things, dearly beloved, for your edifying. For I fear, lest, when I come, I shall not find you such as I would, and that I shall be found unto you such as ye would not: lest there be debates, envyings, wraths, strifes, backbitings, whisperings, swellings, tumults: And lest, when I come again, my God will humble me among you, and that I shall bewail many which have sinned already, and have not repented of the uncleanness and fornication and lasciviousness which they have committed.*

Here, David gives a similar refrain. Against backbiting, talking behind someone's back, railing and gossiping. Against evil done to your neighbor. The very traits David spends multiple Psalms praying imprecatory prayers against workers of iniquity for doing, should not be spoken of among God's children. 

We should condemn vile persons, not partake in promoting them, or their pride in sin. We should honor and fear the Lord. In all our ways, acknowledging Him. 

We should keep our vows and promises, especially when it is not to our favor. Where we suffer loss for our honesty's sake. 

We should avoid usury, and taking advantage of the poor and desperate. Instead, with gratitude to the Lord, lend freely. By our actions we establish ourselves in truth. Building upon the rock, by being doers of the word. 

We shall do these things and shall never be moved.

<!-- post 17718 · 2026-08-03 06:11 · study -->

Imagine you are a King, and powerful among the people, yet your empathy and thoughts are for justice and the poor, widows, fatherless. Making your priorities about others.

David, far from being sinless, yet this was the mind within him. His power wasn't for him, but from God to help the helpless. 

This is what made him the apple of God's eye. And it is the calling we are to answer, as princes of the Kingdom. The same instructions in humility and decency that Solomon, David's heir, taught in Proverbs. The same as the teachings and example of Christ Jesus in His ministry as a Servant. 

May this be our starting position, every morning. In our prayers and supplication before the throne, seeking the benefit of others and the glorification of God.

## Psalms 16  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-04

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17719

<!-- post 17719 · 2026-08-04 07:00 · scripture -->

**Psalms 16**
*Preserve me, O God: for in thee do I put my trust.*

*2 O my soul, thou hast said unto the LORD, Thou art my Lord: my goodness extendeth not to thee;*

*3 But to the saints that are in the earth, and to the excellent, in whom is all my delight.*

*4 Their sorrows shall be multiplied that hasten after another god: their drink offerings of blood will I not offer, nor take up their names into my lips.*

*5 The LORD is the portion of mine inheritance and of my cup: thou maintainest my lot.*

*6 The lines are fallen unto me in pleasant places; yea, I have a goodly heritage.*

*7 I will bless the LORD, who hath given me counsel: my reins also instruct me in the night seasons.*

*8 I have set the LORD always before me: because he is at my right hand, I shall not be moved.*

*9 Therefore my heart is glad, and my glory rejoiceth: my flesh also shall rest in hope.*

*10 For thou wilt not leave my soul in hell; neither wilt thou suffer thine Holy One to see corruption.*

*11 Thou wilt shew me the path of life: in thy presence is fulness of joy; at thy right hand there are pleasures for evermore.*

<!-- post 17720 · 2026-08-04 07:29 · study -->

**Psalm 16** presents another sacred hymn celebrating the Lord's salvation, the eternal security of the believer, and the promise of the risen Christ. 

The preservation of the saints is the hope of all who place their trust in God. Our very being cries out to Him for salvation, recognizing He is Lord. 

Note here that this isn't "Lordship salvation," a false gospel that says you must "make Jesus the Lord of your life" to be saved. It is saved believers, already trusting Him to save us, recognizing that He IS the Lord, as a statement of fact. 

David writes, *Thou art my Lord: my goodness extendeth not to thee; But to the saints that are in the earth, and to the excellent, in whom is all my delight.* The good that we do isn't for Him, it adds nothing to the holy and perfect God. 

The idea that Jesus needs us to make Him Lord, that by our "turning from sin," we justify ourselves before God, is hubris. It's a false gospel. 

The good we do comes from Him, and is poured out to the brothers and sisters, the saints. We benefit others, and they glorify God. We obey His judgements, to one another's profit. 

**Matthew 5:16**
*Let your light so shine before men, that they may see your good works, and glorify your Father which is in heaven.*

We submit ourselves to the righteousness of God. We do not seek to establish our own righteousness, but to magnify His. 

**Romans 10:3–4**
*For they being ignorant of God’s righteousness, and going about to establish their own righteousness, have not submitted themselves unto the righteousness of God. For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to every one that believeth.*

David's burden is for the saints in the earth, his delight is to the excellent of God. Just as we are commanded to. 

**John 13:34–35**
*A new commandment I give unto you, That ye love one another; as I have loved you, that ye also love one another. By this shall all men know that ye are my disciples, if ye have love one to another.*

While we should seek to live peaceably with all men, we should especially look with love upon our fellow believers. 

**Galatians 6:9–10**
*And let us not be weary in well doing: for in due season we shall reap, if we faint not. As we have therefore opportunity, let us do good unto all men, especially unto them who are of the household of faith.*

This is the message that we have heard from the beginning. It is our job as Christians. It is how we are to know one another. Not keeping to ourselves or seeking out own profit. Certainly not by hating our brothers. 

1 John 3:10–11
*In this the children of God are manifest, and the children of the devil: whosoever doeth not righteousness is not of God, neither he that loveth not his brother. For this is the message that ye heard from the beginning, that we should love one another.*

This is the mind of Christ, to be servants, one to another. To delight in others. 

The ultimate expression of humility isn't thinking less of yourself, but not thinking of yourself at all. And the humbleness of submission to God's righteousness should naturally turn to our service for others. Not to add to God's goodness our filthy rags, but to glorify His goodness in us.

<!-- post 17721 · 2026-08-04 08:20 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalm 16**, David calls out the vanity and ultimate end of those that call not upon the Lord for salvation. Who go after another god. 

They make drink offerings of blood, and speak other names in their prayers. 

The most prominent example of this today is Roman Catholicism. They make drink offerings, claiming it to be the blood of Christ, profaning His name, and bow to and serve idols representing their own gods. 

But for the saved, the Lord is our inheritance, we are co-heirs with Christ the Bible says, and He is our cup, the blood shed for us on the cross:

**Luke 22:20**
*Likewise also the cup after supper, saying, This cup is the new testament in my blood, which is shed for you.*

The very new testament which we preach is founded upon the blood shed by Jesus. Not a chalice of gold held up by a priest to an image, sacrificing Jesus daily, as if that were possible or necessary, to call Him down from heaven again.  

And it is God that maintains our lot. Who holds us by His power. Not carnal sacrifices made over and over again. 

**Hebrews 10:10–14**
*By the which will we are sanctified through the offering of the body of Jesus Christ once for all. And every priest standeth daily ministering and offering oftentimes the same sacrifices, which can never take away sins: But this man, after he had offered one sacrifice for sins for ever, sat down on the right hand of God; From henceforth expecting till his enemies be made his footstool. For by one offering he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified.*

This is our goodly heritage. This is the promise of God. The Son at the right hand of the Father. We shall not be moved. And so we rejoice. 

We rest in hope.

<!-- post 17722 · 2026-08-04 08:25 · study -->

*For thou wilt not leave my soul in hell; neither wilt thou suffer thine Holy One to see corruption.*

The Psalm is quoted in the preaching of Peter before the crowds at Pentecost. 

**Acts 2:25–28**
*For David speaketh concerning him, I foresaw the Lord always before my face, for he is on my right hand, that I should not be moved: Therefore did my heart rejoice, and my tongue was glad; moreover also my flesh shall rest in hope: Because thou wilt not leave my soul in hell, neither wilt thou suffer thine Holy One to see corruption. Thou hast made known to me the ways of life; thou shalt make me full of joy with thy countenance.*

The New Testament gives context to the Old, If it wasn't obvious already, the meaning in the psalm. We know David was speaking of Jesus. David, looking forward to the Christ, his savior and Lord. Seeing Jesus in all things, that blessed hope we rest in.

And because Jesus was not left in hell and His Holy One did not see corruption, we have the promise of eternal life, by His resurrection. So neither will we be left to Hell. We can rejoice knowing that He will forever be before us. Amen

## Psalms 17  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-05

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17724

<!-- post 17724 · 2026-08-05 05:53 · scripture -->

**Psalms 17**
*Hear the right, O LORD, attend unto my cry, give ear unto my prayer, that goeth not out of feigned lips.*

*2 Let my sentence come forth from thy presence; let thine eyes behold the things that are equal.*

*3 Thou hast proved mine heart; thou hast visited me in the night; thou hast tried me, and shalt find nothing; I am purposed that my mouth shall not transgress.*

*4 Concerning the works of men, by the word of thy lips I have kept me from the paths of the destroyer.*

*5 Hold up my goings in thy paths, that my footsteps slip not.*

*6 I have called upon thee, for thou wilt hear me, O God: incline thine ear unto me, and hear my speech.*

*7 Shew thy marvellous lovingkindness, O thou that savest by thy right hand them which put their trust in thee from those that rise up against them.*

*8 Keep me as the apple of the eye, hide me under the shadow of thy wings,*

*9 From the wicked that oppress me, from my deadly enemies, who compass me about.*

*10 They are inclosed in their own fat: with their mouth they speak proudly.*

*11 They have now compassed us in our steps: they have set their eyes bowing down to the earth;*

*12 Like as a lion that is greedy of his prey, and as it were a young lion lurking in secret places.*

*13 Arise, O LORD, disappoint him, cast him down: deliver my soul from the wicked, which is thy sword:*

*14 From men which are thy hand, O LORD, from men of the world, which have their portion in this life, and whose belly thou fillest with thy hid treasure: they are full of children, and leave the rest of their substance to their babes.*

*15 As for me, I will behold thy face in righteousness: I shall be satisfied, when I awake, with thy likeness.*

<!-- post 17725 · 2026-08-05 06:11 · study -->

**Psalm 17** again shows us the model of importunity in prayer. 

Importunity is a noun that means asking for something over and over again in a strong, pushy, or annoying way. It is the act of persistent begging or making urgent demands that wear people down. David utilizes this in Psalms to great effect. Pursuing an answer from the throne of God, with repeated supplication. 

It may seem contrary to the fear of the Lord and humility before Him, but scripture shows it is effective. Jesus taught us this as well. 

**Luke 18:1–8**
*And he spake a parable unto them to this end, that men ought always to pray, and not to faint; Saying, There was in a city a judge, which feared not God, neither regarded man: And there was a widow in that city; and she came unto him, saying, Avenge me of mine adversary. And he would not for a while: but afterward he said within himself, Though I fear not God, nor regard man; Yet because this widow troubleth me, I will avenge her, lest by her continual coming she weary me. And the Lord said, Hear what the unjust judge saith. And shall not God avenge his own elect, which cry day and night unto him, though he bear long with them? I tell you that he will avenge them speedily. Nevertheless when the Son of man cometh, shall he find faith on the earth?*

We often find ourselves in difficult situations, where we have not because we ask not. We also tend to move on, rather than "bother God" with whatever it is we need of Him.  

Praying without ceasing, staying strong in prayer because we are weak, this is pleasing to the Lord. So we should pray without fainting, and in doing so, exercise our faith that should Christ return in our time, He finds that faith upon the earth. 

Notice too, that the woman before the unjust judge is pleading for vengeance against her adversary, just as David is here in this psalm. Just as the martyred under the throne in Heaven plead with God for His wrath to pour out. 

Imprecatory prayers are Biblical. Acknowledging God's ownership of vengeance, while suffering for His sake, is an act of great faith. And as we have seen previously in **Psalms,** patience is a virtue that leads us to godliness. 

Importunity in prayer, and imprecatory prayer feels wrong to many. I have seen supposed Christian teachers make the claim that there is no room for this in the New Testament. But they are a mighty tool in our arsenal of faith.

<!-- post 17726 · 2026-08-05 06:47 · study -->

The psalmist calls for the Lord's judgment, that His sentence be given against the wicked. But it is foolish to cry out for God's perfect justice to be administered, while you yourself are guilty. 

It is like a thief going to the police because his partners robbed him of his share of their stolen things. Of a drug dealer complaining about being ripped off in a deal gone wrong. 

And so, David wisely shows us the pattern of praying imprecatory prayers includes self-examination and willingness for God to judge him the same way he is judging others. 

*Thou hast proved mine heart; thou hast visited me in the night; thou hast tried me, and shalt find nothing; I am purposed that my mouth shall not transgress.*

David credits God's word to keep him from the *paths of the destroyer.* A key doctrine, that it is God's word that we judge ourselves and others by, not our own feelings or desires. We judge righteously when we allow God to tell us good from evil. 

David then calls for the Lord to hold up his goings, to keep him on the right path. 

So we see that it isn't simply cursing your enemies and expecting God to overlook your sin. But praying for God's judgement, knowing He is just and seeking His confirmation that you are blameless in the matter. Also, praising Him for keeping you from sin in the first place. 

All glory goes to God. 

Having the faith to be judged fairly by God, knowing that because of God you have walked in His ways, but accepting any fault of your own that He may find...this humility should be at the heart of your prayer life.

<!-- post 17727 · 2026-08-05 07:08 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalms 17**, we see that David's importunity comes from his faith in God to hear those who call upon Him. That we may go boldly before the throne of grace, because He has shown us that He listens to our cries. The initial call for salvation in the Lord's name gives us the hope that He listens to us for far less important matters, such as our suffering in this life. 

The Lord's lovingkindness is already apparent to those that put their trust in Him, that have been saved by His strong right-hand. Christ Jesus. We know that our Savior is at the right hand of the Father. The New Testament is clear on this. But David is writing hundreds of years earlier, showing that Old Testament saints knew and understood the concept, even if they didn't have the full revelation and details. 

*Keep me as the apple of the eye, hide me under the shadow of thy wings, From the wicked that oppress me, from my deadly enemies, who compass me about.*

Keep me, o Lord. It is His power that keeps the children of God under His protection and secure in His promises. We shall not fear, because we are under His mighty wing, as a mother hen keeps her chicks. 

Jesus laments the unfaithful Jews for refusing to be gathered thus. 

**Matthew 23:37–39**
*O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, thou that killest the prophets, and stonest them which are sent unto thee, how often would I have gathered thy children together, even as a hen gathereth her chickens under her wings, and ye would not! Behold, your house is left unto you desolate. For I say unto you, Ye shall not see me henceforth, till ye shall say, Blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord*.

And His protection is needed in this world, where they hate us for His holy name. Where we are often at the mercy of forces beyond our reckoning. Where the judgment of God against our nation is executed by the hands of the wicked. They are His sword. 

They may profit in this life. They may have riches, while we are poor. They may have children and an inheritance. But we have the eternal promise of Christ, which is far more precious than any treasure of this life. 

*As for me, I will behold thy face in righteousness: I shall be satisfied, when I awake, with thy likeness.*

This is the eternal perspective that Christians should seek. That our satisfaction is in the resurrection, and the current, temporal troubles are nothing compared to the everlasting. 

When we awake, when we are raised up, we will see Jesus, the express likeness of God. But, maybe David is also speaking of the glorified body we will receive in His likeness. 

**1 John 3:1–2**
*Behold, what manner of love the Father hath bestowed upon us, that we should be called the sons of God: therefore the world knoweth us not, because it knew him not. Beloved, now are we the sons of God, and it doth not yet appear what we shall be: but we know that, when he shall appear, we shall be like him; for we shall see him as he is.*

This is our blessed hope. To awake into glory with Him. When we see Jesus as sons of God. Gathered up to the Lord, and with Him shall we ever be. 

Ultimately, this is the only satisfaction we need. If our enemies prosper, if God allows them to have advantage over us, while we suffer them, we know His grace is sufficient. That ultimately, vengeance will be His. That in the Lord's love for us, we will see Him as He is, and be like Him. 

That no matter what may come in this life, we have peace in the next. Amen

## Psalms 18  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-07

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17728

<!-- post 17728 · 2026-08-07 06:57 · scripture -->

**Psalms 18**
*I will love thee, O LORD, my strength.*

*2 The LORD is my rock, and my fortress, and my deliverer; my God, my strength, in whom I will trust; my buckler, and the horn of my salvation, and my high tower.*

*3 I will call upon the LORD, who is worthy to be praised: so shall I be saved from mine enemies.*

*4 The sorrows of death compassed me, and the floods of ungodly men made me afraid.*

*5 The sorrows of hell compassed me about: the snares of death prevented me.*

*6 In my distress I called upon the LORD, and cried unto my God: he heard my voice out of his temple, and my cry came before him, even into his ears.*

*7 Then the earth shook and trembled; the foundations also of the hills moved and were shaken, because he was wroth.*

*8 There went up a smoke out of his nostrils, and fire out of his mouth devoured: coals were kindled by it.*

*9 He bowed the heavens also, and came down: and darkness was under his feet.*

*10 And he rode upon a cherub, and did fly: yea, he did fly upon the wings of the wind.*

*11 He made darkness his secret place; his pavilion round about him were dark waters and thick clouds of the skies.*

*12 At the brightness that was before him his thick clouds passed, hail stones and coals of fire.*

*13 The LORD also thundered in the heavens, and the Highest gave his voice; hail stones and coals of fire.*

*14 Yea, he sent out his arrows, and scattered them; and he shot out lightnings, and discomfited them.*

*15 Then the channels of waters were seen, and the foundations of the world were discovered at thy rebuke, O LORD, at the blast of the breath of thy nostrils.*

*16 He sent from above, he took me, he drew me out of many waters.*

*17 He delivered me from my strong enemy, and from them which hated me: for they were too strong for me.*

*18 They prevented me in the day of my calamity: but the LORD was my stay.*

*19 He brought me forth also into a large place; he delivered me, because he delighted in me.*

*20 The LORD rewarded me according to my righteousness; according to the cleanness of my hands hath he recompensed me.*

*21 For I have kept the ways of the LORD, and have not wickedly departed from my God.*

*22 For all his judgments were before me, and I did not put away his statutes from me.*

*23 I was also upright before him, and I kept myself from mine iniquity.*

*24 Therefore hath the LORD recompensed me according to my righteousness, according to the cleanness of my hands in his eyesight.*

*25 With the merciful thou wilt shew thyself merciful; with an upright man thou wilt shew thyself upright;*

*26 With the pure thou wilt shew thyself pure; and with the froward thou wilt shew thyself froward.*

*27 For thou wilt save the afflicted people; but wilt bring down high looks.*

*28 For thou wilt light my candle: the LORD my God will enlighten my darkness.*

*29 For by thee I have run through a troop; and by my God have I leaped over a wall.*

*30 As for God, his way is perfect: the word of the LORD is tried: he is a buckler to all those that trust in him.*

*31 For who is God save the LORD? or who is a rock save our God?*

*32 It is God that girdeth me with strength, and maketh my way perfect.*

*33 He maketh my feet like hinds’ feet, and setteth me upon my high places.*

*34 He teacheth my hands to war, so that a bow of steel is broken by mine arms.*

*35 Thou hast also given me the shield of thy salvation: and thy right hand hath holden me up, and thy gentleness hath made me great.*

*36 Thou hast enlarged my steps under me, that my feet did not slip.*

*37 I have pursued mine enemies, and overtaken them: neither did I turn again till they were consumed.*

*38 I have wounded them that they were not able to rise: they are fallen under my feet.*

*39 For thou hast girded me with strength unto the battle: thou hast subdued under me those that rose up against me.*

*40 Thou hast also given me the necks of mine enemies; that I might destroy them that hate me.*

*41 They cried, but there was none to save them: even unto the LORD, but he answered them not.*

<!-- post 17729 · 2026-08-07 06:57 · scripture -->

*42 Then did I beat them small as the dust before the wind: I did cast them out as the dirt in the streets.*

*43 Thou hast delivered me from the strivings of the people; and thou hast made me the head of the heathen: a people whom I have not known shall serve me.*

*44 As soon as they hear of me, they shall obey me: the strangers shall submit themselves unto me.*

*45 The strangers shall fade away, and be afraid out of their close places.*

*46 The LORD liveth; and blessed be my rock; and let the God of my salvation be exalted.*

*47 It is God that avengeth me, and subdueth the people under me.*

*48 He delivereth me from mine enemies: yea, thou liftest me up above those that rise up against me: thou hast delivered me from the violent man.*

*49 Therefore will I give thanks unto thee, O LORD, among the heathen, and sing praises unto thy name.*

*50 Great deliverance giveth he to his king; and sheweth mercy to his anointed, to David, and to his seed for evermore.*

<!-- post 17730 · 2026-08-07 07:22 · study -->

**Psalm 18** is one of the first long-form songs of David, and it reflects his own experience with the Lord, his trust in God, and deliverance from his enemies, while weaving together prophetic views of Jesus, the doctrines of salvation, the end times wrath and Kingdom of God. 

Ultimately, the Psalm gives us understanding of Who the Lord is and why He is worthy of our trust, today. 

The Lord is our strength. Our rock. Our fortress. Our deliverer. Our strength. Trustworthy. Our buckler, the horn of our salvation, our high tower. He is worthy to be praised. He is merciful to those that are merciful. Upright in all His ways to those that walk in them. Pure to the pure and froward to the froward.

His way is perfect, His word is tried and trusted, and the strength and shield of those that place that trust in Him. 

*For who is God save the LORD? or who is a rock save our God?*

There is no other rock than the Lord. There is no other name given but Jesus. He is the fulfillment of the word, the word made flesh, the promise of God come down from Heaven. The Christ. And He liveth. 

The shield of salvation is given, by the right-hand of God. His gentleness has made those who trust Him great. 

*The LORD liveth; and blessed be my rock; and let the God of my salvation be exalted.*

The Lord is praised among the heathen. The strangers hear and obey. The people who were not a people are come, and are subdued under the throne of David, to his Seed. To His anointed, evermore. 

The enemies of David, like the enemies of Christ, are crushed before them by the fiery and fierce judgment of God. Rains of fire and brimstone, hail and lightenings, leading the wicked to cry out, and hide themselves from His fury. And ultimately be destroyed by His coming. 

The Lord then is a rewarder of those that trust in Him, who diligently seek Him. Who follow His ways and obey His statutes. He will reward us for the righteousness we have shown, and the recompense for our cleanliness in His sight. The righteousness of Christ imputed on His children. The gold, silver and precious stones we build upon that foundation. 

He lifts us up, strengthening us to do great deeds in His name. He fills us with His light. 

He hears our cries. He comes to our aid. He destroys the wicked for our sake, in the anger kindled by their sin against Him and their treatment of His children. 

*Therefore will I give thanks unto thee, O LORD, among the heathen, and sing praises unto thy name.* Amen

<!-- post 17731 · 2026-08-07 07:31 · study -->

Songs of deliverance and praise, with the appearance of the Lord as a consuming fire, yet displaying tender mercies to His beloved, those that place their trust in Him, and who call upon the Lord, are found throughout the book of Psalms. 

The reassurance to believers that what God has done for David will be done for us, His name and word are worthy, and are to be trusted. The picture of salvation and judgment. All are detailed repeatedly, just as much as songs of despair and pleas for God's intervention. But both styles of song contain thanksgiving and praise for His grace and mercy, and all the blessings He has already provided those that love Him. 

Taken as a whole, Psalms gives us the complete picture of the Christian life. They give us a model of prayer and praise that fits every stage of our growth and development, while also dripping with doctrine, teaching us the truth of God.

*40 Thou hast also given me the necks of mine enemies; that I might destroy them that hate me.*

*41 They cried, but there was none to save them: even unto the Lord, but he answered them not.*

*42 Then did I beat them small as the dust before the wind: I did cast them out as the dirt in the streets.*

<!-- post 15901 · 2025-04-11 09:31 · scripture -->

*43 Thou hast delivered me from the strivings of the people; and thou hast made me the head of the heathen: a people whom I have not known shall serve me.*

*44 As soon as they hear of me, they shall obey me: the strangers shall submit themselves unto me.*

*45 The strangers shall fade away, and be afraid out of their close places.*

*46 The Lord liveth; and blessed be my rock; and let the God of my salvation be exalted.*

*47 It is God that avengeth me, and subdueth the people under me.*

*48 He delivereth me from mine enemies: yea, thou liftest me up above those that rise up against me: thou hast delivered me from the violent man.*

*49 Therefore will I give thanks unto thee, O Lord, among the heathen, and sing praises unto thy name.*

## Psalms 19  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-10

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17734

<!-- post 17734 · 2026-08-10 05:40 · scripture -->

**Psalms 19**
*The heavens declare the glory of God; and the firmament sheweth his handywork.*

*2 Day unto day uttereth speech, and night unto night sheweth knowledge.*

*3 There is no speech nor language, where their voice is not heard.*

*4 Their line is gone out through all the earth, and their words to the end of the world. In them hath he set a tabernacle for the sun,*

*5 Which is as a bridegroom coming out of his chamber, and rejoiceth as a strong man to run a race.*

*6 His going forth is from the end of the heaven, and his circuit unto the ends of it: and there is nothing hid from the heat thereof.*

*7 The law of the LORD is perfect, converting the soul: the testimony of the LORD is sure, making wise the simple.*

*8 The statutes of the LORD are right, rejoicing the heart: the commandment of the LORD is pure, enlightening the eyes.*

*9 The fear of the LORD is clean, enduring for ever: the judgments of the LORD are true and righteous altogether.*

*10 More to be desired are they than gold, yea, than much fine gold: sweeter also than honey and the honeycomb.*

*11 Moreover by them is thy servant warned: and in keeping of them there is great reward.*

*12 Who can understand his errors? cleanse thou me from secret faults.*

*13 Keep back thy servant also from presumptuous sins; let them not have dominion over me: then shall I be upright, and I shall be innocent from the great transgression.*

*14 Let the words of my mouth, and the meditation of my heart, be acceptable in thy sight, O LORD, my strength, and my redeemer.*

<!-- post 17735 · 2026-08-10 06:07 · study -->

**Psalm 19** is fairly short, yet packed with doctrine, teaching the purpose of creation, the testimony of God to all creatures, the picture of Christ, and the establishment of the law of the Lord in nature itself, that there is no excuse, while the believer is given constant reminders of His judgements. 

**Romans 1:18–20**
*For the wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hold the truth in unrighteousness; Because that which may be known of God is manifest in them; for God hath shewed it unto them. For the invisible things of him from the creation of the world are clearly seen, being understood by the things that are made, even his eternal power and Godhead; so that they are without excuse*

The heavens declare the glory of God. Looking upon the night sky we see His precision and power. And as the day follows day and night follows night, each with the passing of the sun and the stars, we are given evidence of His might. 

The things of creation speak without words, that all man may understand. They don't need a language. They don't need to be explained. Man sees the passage of time, seasons, years, decades even, in heavenly bodies. 

The vain hypothetical of a savage on a remote island, never hearing the gospel, is undone by the understanding that all nature is a parable and a testament of God, showing men truth. 

The boldness of the sun breaking forth in the morning is as the risen Christ, returning in glory, a picture of the Creator Who is blessed forever, amen. 

Nature teaches us God's law. His ways. 

**1 Corinthians 11:14**
*Doth not even nature itself teach you, that, if a man have long hair, it is a shame unto him?*

His wrath is revealed against those who sin, even without the oracles of God documenting His commandments. The Spirit showing mankind the results of their transgressions. The understanding that murder, adultery, lying, stealing, all of these things that do harm to your neighbor and are a shame before God, are known. 

**Romans 2:11–16**
*For there is no respect of persons with God. For as many as have sinned without law shall also perish without law: and as many as have sinned in the law shall be judged by the law; (For not the hearers of the law are just before God, but the doers of the law shall be justified. For when the Gentiles, which have not the law, do by nature the things contained in the law, these, having not the law, are a law unto themselves: Which shew the work of the law written in their hearts, their conscience also bearing witness, and their thoughts the mean while accusing or else excusing one another;) In the day when God shall judge the secrets of men by Jesus Christ according to my gospel.*

Man can see the law of God, know it written in their hearts, watch the outcome of their behavior and the wrath upon those who sin, and know they are guilty before the consuming fire which is God. 

How much more are we who have His statutes and commandments by the oracles of God? 

**Romans 3:1–2**
*What advantage then hath the Jew? or what profit is there of circumcision? Much every way: chiefly, because that unto them were committed the oracles of God.*

The advantage given Israel, and held by the Jews in Paul's day, is now given to the world. That everyone may know His word. 

*The law of the LORD is perfect, converting the soul: the testimony of the LORD is sure, making wise the simple.*

If the day declares His righteousness and the night His knowledge, and the very nature of things speaks to His wrath, then the oracles of God give us the hope of His mercy. God is a rewarder to those that diligently seek Him. 

The fear of the Lord is clean, and known to all men. The fool may say in their heart there is no God, but the wise fear Him, even if they don't know scripture. 

The truth is more desirable than gold, sweeter than honey. Its pursuit is its own reward, spurring us to seek Him. The warnings and rewards, motivation to learn His ways. 

And, in that knowledge, we find His word, and from that word, salvation.

<!-- post 17736 · 2026-08-10 06:07 · study -->

In Him we are cleansed from our iniquity. In Him we are conformed to His image, kept back from presumptuous sin. Made upright. His righteousness imputed on us that we may be transformed. To be our strength and the Lord our Redeemer.

<!-- post 17737 · 2026-08-10 06:15 · study -->

We are blessed to have the knowledge of the truth before us. His inspired and preserved word, that we may know Him. And God does not leave those without the oracles lacking. 

But for the children of God, creation gives us reassurance and opportunities to worship Him for His might and glory. To have reminders that He loves us. 

To every seed we sow that comes to fruition. To every shooting star, or bird in flight, or wave crashing on the shore, we are able to look at the Lord in awe. Babies born to the death of the elderly, spring following winter, the setting sun and darkest night...all creation is a parable to those with ears to hear and eyes to see. 

To know God loves us. And His mercy upon us is evidence to others seeking truth. The light in us shines that others may see and glorify God. 

We are to be as the firmament, as the rising sun and the crashing wave. His children are meant to teach the heathen who seeks the Lord, as much as anything in creation. Because we are new creatures in Christ, brought forth as a testimony to God's greatness. 

*Let the words of my mouth, and the meditation of my heart, be acceptable in thy sight, O LORD, my strength, and my redeemer.* Amen

## Psalms 20  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-11

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17740

<!-- post 17740 · 2026-08-11 06:32 · scripture -->

**Psalms 20**
*The LORD hear thee in the day of trouble; the name of the God of Jacob defend thee;*

*2 Send thee help from the sanctuary, and strengthen thee out of Zion;*

*3 Remember all thy offerings, and accept thy burnt sacrifice; Selah.*

*4 Grant thee according to thine own heart, and fulfil all thy counsel.*

*5 We will rejoice in thy salvation, and in the name of our God we will set up our banners: the LORD fulfil all thy petitions.*

*6 Now know I that the LORD saveth his anointed; he will hear him from his holy heaven with the saving strength of his right hand.*

<!-- post 17741 · 2026-08-11 06:38 · study -->

**Psalm 20** is yet another song of praise and worship for the salvation that is in Christ Jesus. 

The name of the God of Jacob. The Lord that strengthens us out of Zion. The one offering forever, the burnt sacrifice. Christ Jesus. 

The love of God has been granted to us in his grace, and his counsel is wisdom; trust in the Lord. 

We will rejoice in the salvation given to us in the name of our God. We will set up banners in His holy name. He is Christ Jesus, and God hears all our petitions, lifted up in His name. 

The Lord saves His anointed. He did not leave his soul to see corruption. His anointed was not left in hell. The saving strength of His right hand. Christ Jesus. 

Some trust in the power of this world, their own strength, their own works. And they will be brought down low. He will say depart from me. I never knew you. 

But to those of us who trust in the name of the Lord, our God, we are risen and made to stand upright. In Christ Jesus. 

Save, Lord. In the name of the King of Kings and the Lord of lords, Christ Jesus.

<!-- post 17742 · 2026-08-11 06:45 · study -->

*Some trust in chariots, and some in horses: but we will remember the name of the LORD our God.*

This verse is often quoted, and has inspired generations of believers. But in practice it can be difficult. We often seek reassurance from our material, wealth and position. 

David, writing this. Psalm with dual purpose (at least), the prophetic imagery of Christ Jesus and the surety of his salvation, but also as an intercessional prayer before a battle against a well-armed and equipped foe. 

David's showing us that it is better to trust in the Lord than all the horses and chariots in the world. That in our battles, even if we are seemingly overmatched by our enemy, we are still victorious in God. 

What a comfort. What a blessing. That we can stand fast, and be sure of our deliverance. That we can trust in the Lord in all things, because we've first trusted in Him, our eternal security. 

When we are weak we are made strong. God uses us to confound the wise. We have already overcome in Christ. And we should rejoice in our salvation.

## Psalms 21  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-12

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17745

<!-- post 17745 · 2026-08-12 05:37 · scripture -->

**Psalms 21**
*The king shall joy in thy strength, O LORD; and in thy salvation how greatly shall he rejoice!*

*2 Thou hast given him his heart’s desire, and hast not withholden the request of his lips. Selah.*

*3 For thou preventest him with the blessings of goodness: thou settest a crown of pure gold on his head.*

*4 He asked life of thee, and thou gavest it him, even length of days for ever and ever.*

*5 His glory is great in thy salvation: honour and majesty hast thou laid upon him.*

*6 For thou hast made him most blessed for ever: thou hast made him exceeding glad with thy countenance.*

*7 For the king trusteth in the LORD, and through the mercy of the most High he shall not be moved.*

*8 Thine hand shall find out all thine enemies: thy right hand shall find out those that hate thee.*

*9 Thou shalt make them as a fiery oven in the time of thine anger: the LORD shall swallow them up in his wrath, and the fire shall devour them.*

*10 Their fruit shalt thou destroy from the earth, and their seed from among the children of men.*

*11 For they intended evil against thee: they imagined a mischievous device, which they are not able to perform.*

*12 Therefore shalt thou make them turn their back, when thou shalt make ready thine arrows upon thy strings against the face of them.*

*13 Be thou exalted, LORD, in thine own strength: so will we sing and praise thy power.*

<!-- post 17746 · 2026-08-12 05:47 · study -->

The dominant theme of the book of Psalms is placing all your faith in God alone, to redeem, to save from danger, to exact vengeance and recompense, to be the strong tower and shield, a ready comfort, faithful to His promises, generous with His mercy and gracious in His gifts. 

Trusting in the Lord for every category, especially for our eternal life. 

*He asked life of thee, and thou gavest it him, even length of days for ever and ever.*

Those that have called upon the name of the Lord for salvation, who trust in His promises, are given eternal life. Forever. This is the confidence we have. The sure hope that we place our trust in. 

And we trust that the wicked will be dealt with according to His perfect and holy judgment. 

Just as sure as our exaltation and glory is guaranteed, that through the Lord's mercy we shall not be moved, we can know the wicked who reject Him will be swallowed up in His wrath. That His fire shall devour them, as the smoke of their torment rises forever. 

Eternal life and salvation, contrasted with eternal wrath and fire. 

We who trusted in Him, sing praises and worship Him. We exalt the Lord, we sing praise to His power. Amen

<!-- post 17747 · 2026-08-12 06:11 · study -->
