(It is equally amazing that they'll deny the idea of trusting and scripture alone when Psalm 119 exists)

## Psalms 22  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-15

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17750

<!-- post 17750 · 2026-08-15 06:42 · scripture -->

**Psalms 22**
*My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me? why art thou so far from helping me, and from the words of my roaring?*

*2 O my God, I cry in the day time, but thou hearest not; and in the night season, and am not silent.*

*3 But thou art holy, O thou that inhabitest the praises of Israel.*

*4 Our fathers trusted in thee: they trusted, and thou didst deliver them.*

*5 They cried unto thee, and were delivered: they trusted in thee, and were not confounded.*

*6 But I am a worm, and no man; a reproach of men, and despised of the people.*

*7 All they that see me laugh me to scorn: they shoot out the lip, they shake the head, saying,*

*8 He trusted on the LORD that he would deliver him: let him deliver him, seeing he delighted in him.*

*9 But thou art he that took me out of the womb: thou didst make me hope when I was upon my mother’s breasts.*

*10 I was cast upon thee from the womb: thou art my God from my mother’s belly.*

*11 Be not far from me; for trouble is near; for there is none to help.*

*12 Many bulls have compassed me: strong bulls of Bashan have beset me round.*

*13 They gaped upon me with their mouths, as a ravening and a roaring lion.*

*14 I am poured out like water, and all my bones are out of joint: my heart is like wax; it is melted in the midst of my bowels.*

*15 My strength is dried up like a potsherd; and my tongue cleaveth to my jaws; and thou hast brought me into the dust of death.*

*16 For dogs have compassed me: the assembly of the wicked have inclosed me: they pierced my hands and my feet.*

*17 I may tell all my bones: they look and stare upon me.*

*18 They part my garments among them, and cast lots upon my vesture.*

*19 But be not thou far from me, O LORD: O my strength, haste thee to help me.*

*20 Deliver my soul from the sword; my darling from the power of the dog.*

*21 Save me from the lion’s mouth: for thou hast heard me from the horns of the unicorns.*

*22 I will declare thy name unto my brethren: in the midst of the congregation will I praise thee.*

*23 Ye that fear the LORD, praise him; all ye the seed of Jacob, glorify him; and fear him, all ye the seed of Israel.*

*24 For he hath not despised nor abhorred the affliction of the afflicted; neither hath he hid his face from him; but when he cried unto him, he heard.*

*25 My praise shall be of thee in the great congregation: I will pay my vows before them that fear him.*

*26 The meek shall eat and be satisfied: they shall praise the LORD that seek him: your heart shall live for ever.*

*27 All the ends of the world shall remember and turn unto the LORD: and all the kindreds of the nations shall worship before thee.*

*28 For the kingdom is the LORD’s: and he is the governor among the nations.*

*29 All they that be fat upon earth shall eat and worship: all they that go down to the dust shall bow before him: and none can keep alive his own soul.*

*30 A seed shall serve him; it shall be accounted to the Lord for a generation.*

*31 They shall come, and shall declare his righteousness unto a people that shall be born, that he hath done this.*

<!-- post 17751 · 2026-08-15 07:05 · study -->

*Eli, Eli, lama sabachthani?* 

In the 9th hour, Jesus cried out from the cross the words of the Psalm, the opening line of a song of desperation, penned by David centuries earlier. 

An event recorded twice in the gospels:

**Matthew 27:46**
*And about the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, saying, Eli, Eli, lama sabachthani? that is to say, My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me?*

**Mark 15:34**
*And at the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, saying, Eloi, Eloi, lama sabachthani? which is, being interpreted, My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me?*

The man Christ Jesus, after a day of betrayal, abuse, beaten beyond human recognition, scourged, shamed and mocked, cursed, hung on the tree, and become sin for all mankind, acknowledging in death that, by taking on all of the sin in all of creation, He is, in that moment, no longer looked upon by the Father Whom He has been with, and a part of, since everlasting. 

The prophetic words of the Psalm, Christ's words echoed through time to the psalmist, words Jesus Himself knew from before the foundation of the world, conveying the heartbreaking moments before the perfect and holy Lord would willingly give His life that we may live. 

Jesus cried out in Aramaic, the daily language of the people, the language He grew up in life speaking, conveying His humanity in that moment. 

The Hebrew words, long sung by Israel in the congregation, translated, and again, interpreted into Greek, and given to us in English, never losing a scintilla of power. Given to us as the blessed hope, an example of the endurance and steadfastness we should look to:

**Hebrews 12:2-3** 
*Looking unto Jesus the author and finisher of our faith; who for the joy that was set before him endured the cross, despising the shame, and is set down at the right hand of the throne of God. For consider him that endured such contradiction of sinners against himself, lest ye be wearied and faint in your minds*.

Jesus, our scapegoat, receiving our sin in atonement, without the camp. Taking them utterly away. Not as the blood of bulls and goats, offered yearly for sanctification by the High Priest of the Tabernacle, but once and for all, shed for man. And He, as THE High Priest, entered into the Heavenly Tabernacle, and applied His own precious blood upon the mercy seat in Heaven. 

No greater love than this has ever been shed towards us, that God became flesh, and lived among us, sinless and perfect, that He might drink of this cup. Going to the cross that we might live. 

The tragic nature of these words reveals the depth of the love of God. That while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.

<!-- post 17752 · 2026-08-15 07:57 · study -->

**Psalm 22** continues from its powerful open, to convey the desperation that Jesus' flesh must have felt in that moment, which is felt by everyone of us at times in our life. Certainly, it was David's own state of mind when the Spirit led him to compose it. 

That we cry out to God, and feel like He is far from us. That He doesn't hear. That He may have abandoned us to our plight. 

But there is always the hope of faith, and trust in the holy God, Who has kept His promises and delivers those who trust in Him. 

The promises made to the fathers, kept. He heard the cries in Egypt, and brought them out of the land. He heard their cries during the time in the wilderness. He delivered their children to the promised land. He heard them in the days of judges, where their own sin brought them low, sending men to raise them up and bring them out of evil.

Those that trusted Him were not confounded. 

**Hebrews 12:1**
*Wherefore seeing we also are compassed about with so great a cloud of witnesses, let us lay aside every weight, and the sin which doth so easily beset us, and let us run with patience the race that is set before us*

We know that God is good, and cannot lie. And so our deliverance is assured, even when our flesh is weak and we feel abandoned. 

The almighty God's consistent faithfulness in keeping his word to those he foreknew, gives us confidence and strength. These things were written for our admonition. That we may know Him as a rewarder of those who diligently seek Him. 

**Romans 15:4**
*For whatsoever things were written aforetime were written for our learning, that we through patience and comfort of the scriptures might have hope.*

We are given that knowledge of the past that we might have the surety of the future.

<!-- post 17753 · 2026-08-15 08:36 · study -->

The prophetic nature of the God-inspired Psalm of David continues, showing the children of Israel the truth of Christ, through a glass darkly. In David's personal pain, we see a shadow of the suffering of Jesus, a reproach of men, despised of the people, laughed to scorn. 

**Matthew 27:39–43**
*And they that passed by reviled him, wagging their heads, And saying, Thou that destroyest the temple, and buildest it in three days, save thyself. If thou be the Son of God, come down from the cross. Likewise also the chief priests mocking him, with the scribes and elders, said, He saved others; himself he cannot save. If he be the King of Israel, let him now come down from the cross, and we will believe him. He trusted in God; let him deliver him now, if he will have him: for he said, I am the Son of God.*

Compassed about by bulls, by dogs, men like lions, surrounded by the Roman soldiers doing the work of the Jews, piercing Jesus' hands and feet with nails. Splayed out upon the cross, His joints distended and twisted to support the weight of His body. The sinking feeling of death settling in His guts, as He struggled to breathe. His bones in sharp relief to His stretched out skin.

Crying out for water, Jesus is given vinegar to drink, raised up on hyssop, a bitter gall that could not quench His overwhelming thirst. The soldiers then parted His garments by lot:

**Matthew 27:34–35**
*They gave him vinegar to drink mingled with gall: and when he had tasted thereof, he would not drink. And they crucified him, and parted his garments, casting lots: that it might be fulfilled which was spoken by the prophet, They parted my garments among them, and upon my vesture did they cast lots.*

The desperation and loneliness, the mental anguish Jesus suffered on the cross, He had also carried with Him for 33 years. Understanding the suffering that awaited Him at Calvary, knowing His anointed part in the great redemptive plan, while at all points being touched by the infirmity of the flesh, and tempted as we are. His prayers at Gethsemane, Son to the Father, that if it were possible to take this cup from Him, please let it be so. But steadfast to do the will of the Father, even knowing the physical and emotional pain He would endure. These stanzas in this psalm tragically capture it all, before transitioning to the joyful hope found in every tribulation those who love God can take comfort in:

**Romans 8:28**
*And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose.*

<!-- post 17754 · 2026-08-16 07:24 · study -->

Psalm 22 continues. The desperate plea of the condemned man, compassed by dogs, scorned and ridiculed, pierced and heartbroken, feeling abandoned by God, turns to a prayer of certain hope. 

We know God has delivered every promise He has made. He has heard the cries of His people and, in due season, saved them. 

We may persist in our prayers, reminding God, and ourselves, of His faithfulness, as we ask for more immediate relief. His perfect timing may not be ours, but at our weakest He shows His strength.

*Deliver my soul from the sword; my darling from the power of the dog. Save me from the lion’s mouth: for thou hast heard me from the horns of the unicorns.*

From the proverbial horns of a dilemma, from the immediate threat, we call upon the name of the Lord. Not only the saved, His darling, but the lost often find His strength in their time of trial. Foxhole converts.

The Potter often moves the clay in such a way, that we finally see our shortcomings and impotency, and need His hand to lift us up, that we may be what He has planned for us to become. 

If even Jesus Christ was to be made a reproach and murdered by bloody men in a wicked act, that we may know eternal life, as the risen and triumphant Son of God, then our trials are an opportunity to overcome by His power and to give the Lord the glory. 

*I will declare thy name unto my brethren: in the midst of the congregation will I praise thee.*

In the midst of tribulation, we shall praise Him. That is the mind of a mature Christian. It is difficult to see, sometimes, and we may feel isolated, alone, but in our most desperate hour, we praise Him. We declare His holy name to our brethren, in the congregation we hold up His excellence. 

(It may not be a primary (or even secondary) application of this psalm, but it strikes me that we cannot lift up our voice in praise to the congregation, to declare His name unto the brethren, if we avoid church. If we forsake the assembly. Go to church!)

<!-- post 17755 · 2026-08-16 07:39 · study -->

*Ye that fear the LORD, praise him; all ye the seed of Jacob, glorify him; and fear him, all ye the seed of Israel.*

The praise and glory and fear of the Lord comes from knowledge of the doctrines of God. The understanding of His ways, the past performance of His promise, the teachings of the fathers through the inspired and preserved word of God. 

Faith cometh by hearing.

In the midst of the congregation, among those that fear the Lord and sing His glory, blessing God and His holy name, we are not only given the opportunity to give testimony of what He has done for us, but hear what He has done for others. To learn doctrine and see that He has heard those who call out to Him.

We can know, as Elijah on the mountain, alone, that there are a remnant of believers. 

**Romans 11:3–4**
*Lord, they have killed thy prophets, and digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life. But what saith the answer of God unto him? I have reserved to myself seven thousand men, who have not bowed the knee to the image of Baal.*

We have fellowship in our gatherings with the brethren. We have understanding from preaching and support of prayer, and the opportunity to worship God with those who have gone through what we have. To edify those going through things we have endured. 

**Hebrews 10:23–25**
*Let us hold fast the profession of our faith without wavering; (for he is faithful that promised;) And let us consider one another to provoke unto love and to good works: Not forsaking the assembling of ourselves together, as the manner of some is; but exhorting one another: and so much the more, as ye see the day approaching.*

That communal exhortation is invaluable to a believer in tribulation. Going through a personal trial. Hearing the victory Jesus has given others provides the strength to go on. 

To know the meek shall eat and be satisfied, seeking the Lord and finding Him, unto eternal life.

**John 6:35–37**
*And Jesus said unto them, I am the bread of life: he that cometh to me shall never hunger; and he that believeth on me shall never thirst. But I said unto you, That ye also have seen me, and believe not. All that the Father giveth me shall come to me; and him that cometh to me I will in no wise cast out.*

This is the meat we eat, and are satisfied, never to hunger again. Complete in Christ, and having eternal life.

<!-- post 17756 · 2026-08-16 07:44 · study -->

The truth of this gospel goes out to the ends of the world. And every knee shall bow. *For the kingdom is the LORD’s: and he is the governor among the nations.*

Ultimately, our hope rests on the truth that what God has promised to deliver, He will. And the present suffering pales in comparison for what will come. 

**Romans 8:18**
*For I reckon that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed in us.*

*A seed shall serve him; it shall be accounted to the Lord for a generation. They shall come, and shall declare his righteousness unto a people that shall be born, that he hath done this.*

Our suffering is cause to praise the Lord to the brethren, to being others to the knowledge of Christ, to proclaim His power and promise, and to partake in the spreading of His gospel to all people, that every tongue shall confess that He hath done this. 

Amen

## Psalms 23  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-20

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17759

<!-- post 17759 · 2026-08-20 05:22 · scripture -->

**Psalms 23**
*The LORD is my shepherd; I shall not want.*

*2 He maketh me to lie down in green pastures: he leadeth me beside the still waters.*

*3 He restoreth my soul: he leadeth me in the paths of righteousness for his name’s sake.*

*4 Yea, though I walk through the valley of the shadow of death, I will fear no evil: for thou art with me; thy rod and thy staff they comfort me.*

*5 Thou preparest a table before me in the presence of mine enemies: thou anointest my head with oil; my cup runneth over.*

*6 Surely goodness and mercy shall follow me all the days of my life: and I will dwell in the house of the LORD for ever.*

<!-- post 17760 · 2026-08-20 05:58 · study -->

**Psalm 23** is perhaps the second most famous passage of scripture, behind **John 3:16**, and like it, contains the gospel promise, the good news of the Lord towards believers. 

The world knows the Psalm because it has become the standard go to, in fiction and in real life, recited at funerals by priests and reverends, in every religion that claims the name of Christ. Sadly, for most, the departed have no idea that the promises are not automatic, and aren't achieved by their works, their character or how "good" they are. They are by faith in the Good Shepherd, alone. 

Of course, Jesus is that shepherd. 

**John 10:11** 
*I am the good shepherd: the good shepherd giveth his life for the sheep*.

He gave His life that we may be forgiven, and rose again that we may have eternal life. He did this for those that follow Him, and who have been entrusted to Him by the Father. Believers are His flock. 

And because He is our shepherd, we shall not want. 

What an amazing promise, which God Who cannot lie has given us. We shall not want. He provides all things:

**Philippians 4:19**
*But my God shall supply all your need according to his riches in glory by Christ Jesus.*

Not riches as this world judges, necessarily, but all our needs are met by Him by Christ Jesus. 

**2 Peter 1:3**
*According as his divine power hath given unto us all things that pertain unto life and godliness, through the knowledge of him that hath called us to glory and virtue.*

You may feel financially burdened, or pressed by health concerns. You might be struggling in your faith, to live by trust in God to deliver. But ultimately, the truth is that all we need, everything that pertains to life and godliness, is given freely, that we may have life and more abundantly in His name. 

The green pastures and still waters are generally equated to Heaven in the way the world uses **Psalm 23**, and certainly, what little we know of Paradise is the peace of being in the presence of the Lord. But this stillness is a promise of this life, for a believer. 

**Philippians 4:7**
*And the peace of God, which passeth all understanding, shall keep your hearts and minds through Christ Jesus.*

Our burdens aren't removed, but the yoke of carrying them is made light by His grace. Because in the end, whatever we are going through will be for our good. 

**Romans 8:28**
*And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose.*

We are renewed in Him, and regenerated by the Spirit in faith, our soul restored. We are led in His ways, and shown the truth from His word, for His name's sake. In Jesus we are made whole, and by His power revealed in us, by His word, we are shown the deeper things of God. 

*Yea, though I walk through the valley of the shadow of death, I will fear no evil: for thou art with me; thy rod and thy staff they comfort me.* 

This allegory is considered death itself by the secular world, but note the imagery of a shadow. The Bible says a shadow is not the very image of the things that cast it. 

We may be in a low point in our lives, a valley, and maybe even feel the shadow of death on our flesh as we endure this season in our lives, but the strength to fear no evil, to glory in our tribulation, comes from knowing the good Shepherd is with us. His rod and staff, our comfort. 

The rod of correction, the staff that guides us as we go astray. His protection, our safety. Knowing He laid down His life for us. That He will seek and save His lost sheep, and bring us home by His power.

What the unsaved world uses as a false comfort in death should be a comfort in this life for saved believers.

The table He has set for us, the bounty of His blessings, the deliverance from those who seek to destroy us, that they may know we fear the Lord, and glorify Him, is in this life. In the presence of our enemies. 

Yes, there is the idea of a feast set for us in the end times prophecy, a wedding feast for the Bridegroom. Served by Christ Jesus:

<!-- post 17761 · 2026-08-20 05:58 · study -->

**Isaiah 25:6**
*And in this mountain shall the Lord of hosts make unto all people a feast of fat things, a feast of wines on the lees, of fat things full of marrow, of wines on the lees well refined.*

**Luke 12:37**
*Blessed are those servants, whom the lord when he cometh shall find watching: verily I say unto you, that he shall gird himself, and make them to sit down to meat, and will come forth and serve them.*

...but the primary application in **Psalm 23** is the peace found in this life. At this time. Our cup runneth over with His grace, even now. The table has been set in Heaven, but the bounty we are given starts the moment you call upon His name in faith. 

The final verse places this context firmly in the "now" of the believer:

*Surely goodness and mercy shall follow me all the days of my life: and I will dwell in the house of the LORD for ever.*

Goodness and mercy follow us, today. We can rest assured that He has delivered us, and be blessed in this life, knowing we have eternity with Him in the next. 

We shall fear no evil, because we have eternal life and shall never perish. We will dwell in the house of the Lord forever, so rejoice even in perils and trials. Rest in the green pastures by the still waters. We have a good Shepherd Who loves us. Amen

## Psalms 24  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-21

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17763

<!-- post 17763 · 2026-08-21 06:30 · scripture -->

**Psalms 24**
*The earth is the LORD’s, and the fulness thereof; the world, and they that dwell therein.*

*2 For he hath founded it upon the seas, and established it upon the floods.*

*3 Who shall ascend into the hill of the LORD? or who shall stand in his holy place?*

*4 He that hath clean hands, and a pure heart; who hath not lifted up his soul unto vanity, nor sworn deceitfully.*

*5 He shall receive the blessing from the LORD, and righteousness from the God of his salvation.*

*6 This is the generation of them that seek him, that seek thy face, O Jacob. Selah.*

*7 Lift up your heads, O ye gates; and be ye lift up, ye everlasting doors; and the King of glory shall come in.*

*8 Who is this King of glory? The LORD strong and mighty, the LORD mighty in battle.*

*9 Lift up your heads, O ye gates; even lift them up, ye everlasting doors; and the King of glory shall come in.*

*10 Who is this King of glory? The LORD of hosts, he is the King of glory. Selah.*

<!-- post 17764 · 2026-08-21 06:55 · study -->

**Psalm 24** is a meditation on the truth of the Christ, the Lord, King of Hosts. Creator of all things, and the truth of the way of righteousness for those that trust in Him.

The gospel message of the Psalms, sung in the Temple, performed by the chief musicians and the congregation is the inspired doctrines of God, and the focus is entirely Jesus. 

They may have looked through a glass darkly, but the concepts of Christ are hammered home, in verse after verse, chorus and stanzas of praise for the name of God, the Holy One, the Lord. 

Here in the 24th Psalm, David asks *Who shall ascend into the hill of the LORD? or who shall stand in his holy place?*

Those with clean hands and pure hearts, who hasn't put their trust in vanity or lying lips, but who trusted the God of their salvation. 

*He shall receive the blessing from the LORD, and righteousness from the God of his salvation.*

We are made clean of our sins, our hearts purified by the imputed righteousness of God. We receive these blessings by grace through faith, and access to the Kingdom, not by our own righteousness but by submitting to the righteousness that is God's. 

**Romans 4:5–8**
*But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness. Even as David also describeth the blessedness of the man, unto whom God imputeth righteousness without works, Saying, Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered. Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not impute sin.*

There are false religions that deny the imputation of righteousness by faith in Christ. They say that imputation is not taught in scripture. This is a lie.

**2 Corinthians 5:21**
*For he hath made him to be sin for us, who knew no sin; that we might be made the righteousness of God in him.*

Our righteousness is as a filthy rag, our works could never justify us before a holy and perfect God. But these men believing vanity, and swearing deceitfully, teach that man needs to establish their own righteousness through works of the law, and many are misled by this lie. 

**Romans 10:2–4**
*For I bear them record that they have a zeal of God, but not according to knowledge. For they being ignorant of God’s righteousness, and going about to establish their own righteousness, have not submitted themselves unto the righteousness of God. For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to every one that believeth.*

Roman Catholicsm rejects the idea that we are made righteous by receiving righteousness from the God of our salvation. The Council of Trent (Canon 11 on Justification) anathematized the idea that people are justified “by the sole imputation of the justice of Christ, or by the sole remission of sins” and that righteousness is "infused" by works, sacraments and "grace transforming a person," so that their own righteousness is established, rather than submitting to the righteousness of God. 

But let God be true, and every man a liar. The generation of them that seek His face shall not be ashamed. All who come to the saving knowledge of Christ are made pure, in Him. Righteous, in Him. 

**Titus 3:5–7**
*Not by works of righteousness which we have done, but according to his mercy he saved us, by the washing of regeneration, and renewing of the Holy Ghost; Which he shed on us abundantly through Jesus Christ our Saviour; That being justified by his grace, we should be made heirs according to the hope of eternal life.*

The truth of imputed righteousness of God being given the believer is found throughout scripture. And thank God for His grace and mercy. That our sins are washed clean, justified by the blood, and remembered no more.

<!-- post 17765 · 2026-08-21 06:58 · study -->

The final verses of **Psalm 24** are pure prophecy, the adoration and glory of the King. 

The opening of the gates to receive Him. The Lord strong and mighty. Who is the King of glory? We know His name is Jesus. King of kings, Lord of Hosts. The everlasting doors of the Kingdom of God open, and receive Him. All glory is given to Him. The Creator Who is blessed forever, amen.

## Psalms 25  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-23

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17771

<!-- post 17771 · 2026-08-23 07:18 · scripture -->

**Psalms 25**
*Unto thee, O LORD, do I lift up my soul.*

*2 O my God, I trust in thee: let me not be ashamed, let not mine enemies triumph over me.*

*3 Yea, let none that wait on thee be ashamed: let them be ashamed which transgress without cause.*

*4 Shew me thy ways, O LORD; teach me thy paths.*

*5 Lead me in thy truth, and teach me: for thou art the God of my salvation; on thee do I wait all the day.*

*6 Remember, O LORD, thy tender mercies and thy lovingkindnesses; for they have been ever of old.*

*7 Remember not the sins of my youth, nor my transgressions: according to thy mercy remember thou me for thy goodness’ sake, O LORD.*

*8 Good and upright is the LORD: therefore will he teach sinners in the way.*

*9 The meek will he guide in judgment: and the meek will he teach his way.*

*10 All the paths of the LORD are mercy and truth unto such as keep his covenant and his testimonies.*

*11 For thy name’s sake, O LORD, pardon mine iniquity; for it is great.*

*12 What man is he that feareth the LORD? him shall he teach in the way that he shall choose.*

*13 His soul shall dwell at ease; and his seed shall inherit the earth.*

*14 The secret of the LORD is with them that fear him; and he will shew them his covenant.*

*15 Mine eyes are ever toward the LORD; for he shall pluck my feet out of the net.*

*16 Turn thee unto me, and have mercy upon me; for I am desolate and afflicted.*

*17 The troubles of my heart are enlarged: O bring thou me out of my distresses.*

*18 Look upon mine affliction and my pain; and forgive all my sins.*

*19 Consider mine enemies; for they are many; and they hate me with cruel hatred.*

*20 O keep my soul, and deliver me: let me not be ashamed; for I put my trust in thee.*

*21 Let integrity and uprightness preserve me; for I wait on thee.*

*22 Redeem Israel, O God, out of all his troubles.*

<!-- post 17772 · 2026-08-23 07:42 · study -->

The powerful prayer of **Psalm 25** is a model for all that trust in the Lord, and a doctrinally-rich reminder of why we should. That we who wait on the Lord will not be ashamed. 

Verse after verse, the psalmist has us call upon the name of the Lord, as we read or sing through the Psalm. And verse after verse we are reminded of His mercy and faithfulness. 

We lift up our souls, every fiber of our being, unto the Lord, and He delivers us from our enemies. Those that trust in Him are never ashamed, but those that reject His truth are. 

We are shown His ways, taught His statutes, walk His paths, and we are preserved in this life, and established in the next. 

The meek and his seed shall inherit the earth, and in meekness we humbly admit our sins and ask for God's mercy, to forget them. His lovingkindness is established from old, and the God of our salvation keeps us by His power, unto redemption. What an amazing God we have. How can we not serve Him in truth. Truth of Who He is, who we are, and the reconciliation we have with and in Him, despite our sins. 

The Lord teaches sinners like us in His way, and the meek share this truth with the world. 

His secret, His covenant, is shown to us. And that good news is spread abroad by those that fear Him, that others may know the mystery of the gospel, and be saved. 

*Mine eyes are ever toward the LORD; for he shall pluck my feet out of the net.*

**Proverbs 3:6**
*In all thy ways acknowledge him, and he shall direct thy paths.*

**Galatians 5:16**
*This I say then, Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfil the lust of the flesh*.

Looking to Jesus the author and finisher of our salvation, we are lifted from the nets, the snares of sin set for us by the wicked. Walking in the Spirit. Acknowledging the ways of the Lord. 

With importunity and persistent prayer, we remind Him of the goodness He has shown us, and ourselves tof the grace we have found in Him. Our troubles become lighter, as His yoke is easy. He brings us out of our distress. 

The pain we feel, He understands. The affliction that besets us is often due to sin. Seeking forgiveness, because our troubles may be chastening by a loving Father. Getting right so we may be lifted up by Him. Delivered, because we put our trust in Him. Never ashamed, because His word is true. Waiting on the day of the Lord, and the redemption of all Israel. All His people who trusted in the Lord. Amen

<!-- post 17773 · 2026-08-23 07:54 · study -->

In the original Hebrew, David used the format of an "alphabetic acrostic psalm," where stanzas begin with consecutive letters, to act as an mnemonic device, and form of poetry, that David uses multiple times in Psalms. 

This stylized form of writing is "lost" in translation, some textual critics may say, but the strained gnat of it all makes them miss the power of what we have in English. 

The repeated calls to trust the Lord, seek forgiveness, humbly petition and wait with patience is all beautifully expressed in Psalm 25 as we read it here. The prose of the Authorized English loses nothing to the power of God's word. 

So, while it is interesting to note the format, and discuss the ways it was used (here, imperfectly with missing and skipped letters) it isn't necessary for the truth to prick our hearts and give us comfort.

<!-- post 17774 · 2026-08-23 08:03 · study -->

However, recognizing **Psalm 25** as an attempted alphabetic acrostic in the original Hebrew, intended to run through the 22 letters of the alphabet, does give us the opportunity to see something of ourselves in it. 

In this Psalm, the alphabetic pattern is incomplete, the letter *vav* is missing, *resh* appears twice, and the final verse falls outside the sequence. The broken acrostic still carries the psalm’s prayer, and focuses on the Lord's mercy and power. 

Could the broken format look to the broken-hearted sinner, imperfectly calling upon a perfect God? Could it be that by breaking the expected  format, David shows the same brokenness of spirit we have in this life as we wait upon the redemption of Christ in the end? 

I don't know. But it sounds good. Selah.

## Psalms 26  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-24

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17776

<!-- post 17776 · 2026-08-24 05:54 · scripture -->

**Psalms 26**
*Judge me, O LORD; for I have walked in mine integrity: I have trusted also in the LORD; therefore I shall not slide.*

*2 Examine me, O LORD, and prove me; try my reins and my heart.*

*3 For thy lovingkindness is before mine eyes: and I have walked in thy truth.*

*4 I have not sat with vain persons, neither will I go in with dissemblers.*

*5 I have hated the congregation of evil doers; and will not sit with the wicked.*

*6 I will wash mine hands in innocency: so will I compass thine altar, O LORD:*

*7 That I may publish with the voice of thanksgiving, and tell of all thy wondrous works.*

*8 LORD, I have loved the habitation of thy house, and the place where thine honour dwelleth.*

*9 Gather not my soul with sinners, nor my life with bloody men:*

*10 In whose hands is mischief, and their right hand is full of bribes.*

*11 But as for me, I will walk in mine integrity: redeem me, and be merciful unto me.*

*12 My foot standeth in an even place: in the congregations will I bless the LORD.*

<!-- post 17777 · 2026-08-24 06:15 · study -->

**Matthew 7:1–2**
*Judge not, that ye be not judged. For with what judgment ye judge, ye shall be judged: and with what measure ye mete, it shall be measured to you again.*

Jesus' words are often twisted by others to mean a Christian should never judge another person, no matter how wicked they are or what sins they do. But, we know the warning is against hypocrisy, not right judgment. 

**Psalm 26** gives us a picture of what Jesus is actually teaching, where David asks the Lord to judge him by his doings, according to his faith, in comparison to the wicked around him. 

The understanding of proper judgement is key to good Christian living. It allows us to be separated, to be holy as God is holy, to put away the filth of the flesh and walk in the Spirit. To seek to be a disciplined servant of the Lord. 

We walk in integrity, and trust in the Lord alone, that we shall not slide. Firmly on His rock, not backsliding to live as the world does. 

It is not an automatic transformation. We are sanctified by the Spirit by the inward man, the new man, but our flesh is still prone to lusts of the eyes and the pride of life and we can stumble and fall. 

But if we seek the judgment of God, asking Him to try us and see that our heart is right, we are opening ourselves to correction and refinement, to better be used by God. 

Walking in His truth, keeping ourselves pure and humble, seeing others in the way God does, with grace and mercy and lovingkindness, we can make our case to the Lord, and compare ourselves to the wicked without the leaven of the Pharisees, hypocrisy. 

David lists the things he has not done, to separate himself towards God. 

He has not sat with vain persons, false believers. He has not had ecumenical fellowship with the dissemblers, the false prophets who teach lies. He has avoided the congregation of evil doers. The bitter fruit of the false religions of this world. He has not yoked with unbelievers. 

Instead, knowing he is innocent of these things, and sanctified, he is able to come before the altar of the Lord. To gather with the assembly of God's people. To walk into church without the burden of unresolved sin, with a good conscience towards God. 

He doesn't do this to be haughty, or to puff up himself in comparison to the wicked, but that he may proclaim the goodness of the Lord in the midst of the congregation, without hypocrisy. 

Meekly seeking God's judgment in our lives that we may be a better testimony is a good thing. Judgment that leads to repentance and forgiveness of others, that we may love the habitation of His house, that we may know how to behave in the church, as a pillar and foundation of truth, is a good thing. 

Asking the Lord to remember us, that we aren't caught up in His judgment of the wicked, but rather walk in our integrity, waiting for His redemption out of His wrath, seeking His mercy in our obedience. 

*My foot standeth in an even place: in the congregations will I bless the LORD.*

This should be our guiding principle. To rightly judge, beginning with ourselves that we may purge ourselves, be separated unto the Lord that we may boldly proclaim His mercy and bless the Lord in the congregation. Amen.

<!-- post 17778 · 2026-08-24 06:28 · study -->

Fundamentally, **Psalm 26** is an encouragement to purify ourselves and avoid hypocrisy while recognizing the evil of men, that we may go into the assembly sanctified. 

But this also presupposes that we are to go into the assembly. That we are not to forsake the assembly. That we go to church!

We have an obligation to bless the Lord among the brethren. To gather and sing praises towards Him. Because He has judged us, found us wanting, and yet while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us. That in God's lovingkindness, He made provisions of grace to redeem and reconcile us. 

For this we should want to sing His praises. But often, if we don't allow God judgment willingly, if we don't purify ourselves, if we gather with dissemblers, and allow our own hearts to mislead us, and justify ourselves despite our sin, we may find ourselves forsaking the assembly, not going in to the congregation, skipping church. 

Our conscience may be pricked, and there will be preaching against sin. It is easier to backslide when you do. And the feedback loop compounds, until you are among the vain persons. 

If you don't willfully seek the judgment of God in your life, He will still judge you. And in a backslidden state, a Christian can be caught up with the bloody men, and lose their life. Not their eternal life, certainly, but cut short in this one.

Seeking to avoid the refiner's fire of God's judgment can result in far greater pain and loss in His chastening than in the embarrassment and humbling of ourselves when we get right and submit ourselves to His ways. 

Be ready daily to submit to His judgment. His mercies are new each morning. It's never too late to get right while you're on this side of Heaven. Allow God to judge your walk, and stand in your integrity, that you may enter gleefully into the congregation. 

*That I may publish with the voice of thanksgiving, and tell of all thy wondrous works.*

<!-- post 17780 · 2026-08-25 06:05 · study -->

**Psalm 27** continues the pattern of praise for the salvation found in the Lord, by faith. 

The Lord is our light and salvation. Jesus is the way, the truth and the light. We who have trusted in Him, are kept by the power of God unto salvation. What do we have to fear from men? 

**Luke 12:4–5**
*And I say unto you my friends, Be not afraid of them that kill the body, and after that have no more that they can do. But I will forewarn you whom ye shall fear: Fear him, which after he hath killed hath power to cast into hell; yea, I say unto you, Fear him.*

The wicked who may seek your life cannot do anything but threaten you with Heaven. Whatever battle we face, we are already on the winning side. All in Christ have overcome this world. We can be confident in this, unto death. 

And praise God we aren't in a time where we face constant war and battles. That our greatest fight is spiritual. And against our own flesh. 

But there are those in the mission field, in countries where you can be beaten, imprisoned and killed for the cause of Christ. Meek and humble men and women who do great things for the Lord, daily. Winning souls and spreading the word of God in the face of persecution, without fear. 

Many Christians today won't even venture into safe suburban neighborhoods once a week to do the same. Often, out of fear. 

The fear of being a peculiar person. Of being socially rejected for the audacity of preaching Jesus to strangers. Of the "danger" of someone mocking you, or shutting the door in your face. 

David believed, and it was sufficient to face legions of enemies. To face a giant with a sling. Missionaries and their converts face arrest and imprisonment, beatings and death.  

American Christians wear "Faith Over Fear" graphic tees to the gym and think they are making a bold stand for Christ. 

We should be boldly proclaiming the trust we have in a mighty God and the reward He has promised through the Son. *Though war should rise against me, in this will I be confident.*

<!-- post 17784 · 2026-08-27 05:44 · study -->

Continuing Psalm 27, after establishing the faith on which he stands, and the confidence he has on the promises of God, David as psalmist cries out to the Lord for His mercy, to hear his prayer. 

As these are meant to be sung or said, as model prayers or devotional worship, we are called to do the same. To call upon the mercy of the Lord. To bless His longsuffering and grace towards us, and petition the Lord with prayer. 

**Hebrews 11:6**
*But without faith it is impossible to please him: for he that cometh to God must believe that he is, and that he is a rewarder of them that diligently seek him.*

When we seek the Lord, it is obedience to His command to seek His face. To search for Him. As individuals and as a nation of believers. 

**2 Chronicles 7:14**
*If my people, which are called by my name, shall humble themselves, and pray, and seek my face, and turn from their wicked ways; then will I hear from heaven, and will forgive their sin, and will heal their land.*

*Seek and ye shall find*, the Bible says. Our God is merciful to hear our prayers and to reward those that in faith, have sought Him out. What a humbling and amazing thing to be considered worthy of attention by our Creator. 

David continues, meekly asking for forgiveness if there are any transgressions that may have displeased the Lord. Asking for His favor, to not forsake us in times of trouble, yet having the understanding that our trouble may be due to our own unresolved sin. Either way, *O God of* our *salvation,* hear our prayers. 

In the very next breath, David affirms the steadfast faithfulness of the Lord, that even if our own parents forsake us, He remains true. 

Expressing fear and doubt while recognizing the truth of the promises of God is a human thing, and we see this play out on our own minds, even as it does David's. We are a weak and needy people, and we need God involved in every aspect of our lives. 

Remember this, and be open to His teaching. Be open to hearing His voice in scripture, recognizing the chastening in our life, seeing His mercy towards us, and wrath revealed from Heaven, and fearing Him, always. 

He shows us the path. He gives us a way out of every temptation. 

**1 Corinthians 10:13**
*There hath no temptation taken you but such as is common to man: but God is faithful, who will not suffer you to be tempted above that ye are able; but will with the temptation also make a way to escape, that ye may be able to bear it.*

We may ask for His help to see the deliverance He has for us, even as our adversaries seek to destroy us with their lies and schemes. 

*I had fainted, unless I had believed to see the goodness of the LORD in the land of the living.*

The evidence of God's grace and mercy to His people gives us faith and strength to carry on. This is a key reason why it is important to praise Him in the congregation, to edify those going through the trials of life, to have hope. That all things work together for good. To wait, patiently for the Lord to move. To be of good courage, knowing what He as done for others and has done for us in the past. Knowing our present suffering leads to the glory we are partakers in, through Christ.

**Romans 8:35–37**
*Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword? As it is written, For thy sake we are killed all the day long; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter. Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us.*

Amen

*Seek the Lord and his strength,*
*seek his face continually.*

## Psalms 28  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-28

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17787

<!-- post 17787 · 2026-08-28 05:44 · scripture -->

**Psalms 28**
*Unto thee will I cry, O LORD my rock; be not silent to me: lest, if thou be silent to me, I become like them that go down into the pit.*

*2 Hear the voice of my supplications, when I cry unto thee, when I lift up my hands toward thy holy oracle.*

*3 Draw me not away with the wicked, and with the workers of iniquity, which speak peace to their neighbours, but mischief is in their hearts.*

*4 Give them according to their deeds, and according to the wickedness of their endeavours: give them after the work of their hands; render to them their desert.*

*5 Because they regard not the works of the LORD, nor the operation of his hands, he shall destroy them, and not build them up.*

*6 Blessed be the LORD, because he hath heard the voice of my supplications.*

*7 The LORD is my strength and my shield; my heart trusted in him, and I am helped: therefore my heart greatly rejoiceth; and with my song will I praise him.*

*8 The LORD is their strength, and he is the saving strength of his anointed.*

*9 Save thy people, and bless thine inheritance: feed them also, and lift them up for ever.*

<!-- post 17788 · 2026-08-28 05:58 · study -->

The Lord is our rock and our salvation. To Him we cry in trouble. 

Here in **Psalm 28**, David again shows the importunity of prayer, reminding God of what He means to us, and how His promises to deliver His children, and make us holy, also carry promises of the destruction of the wicked. 

Reminding God of our understanding, reinforces the truth in our hearts and provides ready comfort. Because the world is filled with the wicked, and it is easy to become despondent over it. 

We are given a constant stream of negativity, of liars and injustice, workers of iniquity, seemingly prospering. Even those that are caught and tried by the system are often given clemency and cover, to continue in their evil deeds. 

We ask that God hear our voice, and to respond. To keep us separated from the wrath that is appointed to the workers of iniquity. 

Those that speak peace but have mischief in their hearts are all around us. In the news and in our social media feeds. We have a constant black pilling from the world, and can get discouraged. 

We also see the world waxing worse and worse, and the repercussions of past sins being visited on our generation, and it can overwhelm some with fear of being caught up in the collapse that is always just around the corner. 

But our perspective should be to entreat the Lord with supplication, that as He punishes the wicked, He protects His people. We should want them to receive recompense for their evil deeds, and trust He will protect us from His wrath. 

*Because they regard not the works of the LORD, nor the operation of his hands, he shall destroy them, and not build them up.*

The Lord has heard us, and is our strength and shield, the rock of salvation. We should feel joy rather than fear, and praise Him openly for the destruction that comes upon the wicked. 

Thy Kingdom come, o Lord. Even so, come quickly. Spare your anointed from your wrath. Save us out of our tribulation. Destroy the wicked. 

*Save thy people, and bless thine inheritance: feed them also, and lift them up for ever.* Amen

## Psalms 30  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-08-31

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17798

<!-- post 17798 · 2026-08-31 05:39 · scripture -->

**Psalms 30**
*I will extol thee, O LORD; for thou hast lifted me up, and hast not made my foes to rejoice over me.*

*2 O LORD my God, I cried unto thee, and thou hast healed me.*

*3 O LORD, thou hast brought up my soul from the grave: thou hast kept me alive, that I should not go down to the pit.*

*4 Sing unto the LORD, O ye saints of his, and give thanks at the remembrance of his holiness.*

*5 For his anger endureth but a moment; in his favour is life: weeping may endure for a night, but joy cometh in the morning.*

*6 And in my prosperity I said, I shall never be moved.*

*7 LORD, by thy favour thou hast made my mountain to stand strong: thou didst hide thy face, and I was troubled.*

*8 I cried to thee, O LORD; and unto the LORD I made supplication.*

*9 What profit is there in my blood, when I go down to the pit? Shall the dust praise thee? shall it declare thy truth?*

*10 Hear, O LORD, and have mercy upon me: LORD, be thou my helper.*

*11 Thou hast turned for me my mourning into dancing: thou hast put off my sackcloth, and girded me with gladness;*

*12 To the end that my glory may sing praise to thee, and not be silent. O LORD my God, I will give thanks unto thee for ever.*

<!-- post 17799 · 2026-08-31 06:02 · study -->

O LORD! In Psalm 30, David extolls the Lord for His gift of salvation, and calls all in the congregation to do the same. 

God has lifted us up, heard us when we called upon His name, and healed us from our sin. We are brought up from the grave and shall never perish. Our souls will not see Hell. We shall not go down to the pit. 

*Sing unto the LORD, O ye saints of his, and give thanks at the remembrance of his holiness.*

Rejoice! This verse makes me think of the Lord's supper. The thanksgiving and remembrance of Jesus' holiness. 

**1 Corinthians 11:24**
*And when he had given thanks, he brake it, and said, Take, eat: this is my body, which is broken for you: this do in remembrance of me.*

This communal act of thanksgiving and memorial - *For as often as ye eat this bread, and drink this cup, ye do shew the Lord's death till he come*. For this we should sing unto the Lord, extolling His mercy, forever.

*For his anger endureth but a moment; in his favour is life*. The chastening of sons by the Lord is fleeting. But grace, the unmerited favor of God, is given to the world through His Son. And in His favor is life. 

**Romans 6:23**
*For the wages of sin is death; but the gift of God is eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord.*

What a wonderful promise. How can we not sing for joy and give Him glory?

The comfort we have in Christ, knowing that *weeping may endure for a night, but joy cometh in the morning*, knowing for certain that in our prosperity, in the gift of God, we shall not be moved. Our enemies shall not overcome us. We are made to stand on a sure foundation, the rock that is Christ. By His favor. By His grace. 

We can trust that no matter what we face, even if we have angered God, that by turning to Him and crying unto Him, we have access to His grace through faith and the mediator of that faith, the man Christ Jesus. 

The psalmist calls on God, reminding Him of His grace and mercy, the favor with which He has blessed us. And one of the purposes of our salvation, our healing, our getting saved, is that we will live forever to sing His praise. 

The resurrection is sure. 

Some will say the context of the Psalm is thanksgiving for not being killed by David's enemies, and triumphantly giving credit to God, but ultimately it is a song extolling the grace of God that those who trust in Him shall never perish. 

*Hear, O LORD, and have mercy upon me: LORD, be thou my helper.* This is our prayer of salvation. Lord have mercy on me, a sinner. Knowing that God, Who cannot lie, has promised that all who call upon His name shall not perish, but be saved. 

*Thou hast turned for me my mourning into dancing: thou hast put off my sackcloth, and girded me with gladness*...Jesus saves. Death has no sting for those in Christ. And while we may celebrate victory in this life, it is the victory of the cross that we should give thanks and praise, forever, because the purpose of God's sparing us, temporally or spiritually, is that we may sing His praise:

*To the end that my glory may sing praise to thee, and not be silent. O LORD my God, I will give thanks unto thee for ever.*

*To the end*, that is, the purpose of His salvation, our singing praise to God. Ultimately, that is what it is all about. Recognizing the glory and grace of a perfect and holy God. Amen

*10 For my life is spent with grief, and my years with sighing: my strength faileth because of mine iniquity, and my bones are consumed.*

<!-- post 17802 · 2026-09-01 06:04 · study -->

**Luke 23:46**
*And when Jesus had cried with a loud voice, he said, Father, into thy hands I commend my spirit: and having said thus, he gave up the ghost.*

David's Psalms of trust in the Lord God, to be delivered and never ashamed, redeemed in His righteousness, are models of prayer, and Jesus upon the cross quotes them (or rather, they quote Him). How much more should we? 

The cries to listen to our prayers and save us out of trouble, acknowledging that He is our rock and our fortress, our Savior, our ready help in time of tribulation, they echo through time and are as relevant to Christians today as they were to the King of Israel, and the congregation that sang them 3,000 years ago. 

*Into thine hand I commit my spirit: thou hast redeemed me, O LORD God of truth.*

Trusting the God of truth to keep His word, having faith in His promises, is the foundation for our Christian life. Relying on His word to feed us with truth. Knowing the truth that sets us free. 

And loving the truth puts us in a position where we hate those that lie about the Lord. 

**Psalm 119:104**
*Through thy precepts I get understanding: therefore I hate every false way.*

This love of the truth and understanding of His word is a gift of the Spirit of God. 

**1 John 4:6**
*We are of God: he that knoweth God heareth us; he that is not of God heareth not us. Hereby know we the spirit of truth, and the spirit of error.*

All things are made known to us by the Spirit, through Christ who is the way, the truth, the light. 

And so we should be vexed by false prophets who teach damnable heresy.

**1 Timothy 4:1–2**
*Now the Spirit speaketh expressly, that in the latter times some shall depart from the faith, giving heed to seducing spirits, and doctrines of devils; Speaking lies in hypocrisy; having their conscience seared with a hot iron*

These false prophets are reprobates concerning the faith. Their lies make their followers twofold more the child of Hell than themselves. They are enemies of God, and we should hate them with a perfect hatred, while loving those misled by them enough to proclaim the truth.

<!-- post 17804 · 2026-09-02 05:47 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalm 31**, picking up in verse 9. The gladness and rejoicing in God's mercy and crying out to Him in the earlier stanzas, brings us the heart of the matter, the trouble in David's life, and reflected in our own struggles. 

He says I am in trouble, consumed with grief and physically and spiritually affected by the tribulation in his life. This is something everyone alive can relate to. Anyone alive long enough to be broken hearted. Betrayed. Lied about and persecuted. 

*For my life is spent with grief, and my years with sighing: my strength faileth because of mine iniquity, and my bones are consumed.*

When we are going through it, it can feel as though all we ever have known is grief. We can feel all alone, when not only our enemies abandon us, but our neighbors and friends won't even look at us. So miserable and alone. 

We may feel forgotten as a dead man, out of sight out of mind. Truly miserable. Feeling as though the whole world has conspired against us. Having no where to turn or anyone to trust. 

*But I trusted in thee, O LORD: I said, Thou art my God.* What a beautiful reminder that we have a friend in Jesus. We have a Lord who is trustworthy and Who cares. Whose face shines on us, and saves us. Not because of how good we are, but despite our sin. For His mercies' sake. Because He so loved us. 

We plead, *Let me not be ashamed, O LORD; for I have called upon thee*, and we know that God, Who cannot lie, has promised us deliverance. L*et the wicked be ashamed, and let them be silent in the grave.* We will sing His praise forever, while they are tormented by unquenchable fire, where the worm dieth not.

**Isaiah 66:24**
*And they shall go forth, and look upon the carcases of the men that have transgressed against me: for their worm shall not die, neither shall their fire be quenched; and they shall be an abhorring unto all flesh.*

**Mark 9:47–49**
*And if thine eye offend thee, pluck it out: it is better for thee to enter into the kingdom of God with one eye, than having two eyes to be cast into hell fire: Where their worm dieth not, and the fire is not quenched. For every one shall be salted with fire, and every sacrifice shall be salted with salt.*

All those that have denied the truth of God shall be silenced. They shall be made to be ashamed. Let the wicked be ashamed, but we have trusted the Lord. 

*Let the lying lips be put to silence; which speak grievous things proudly and contemptuously against the righteous.*

<!-- post 17805 · 2026-09-02 05:55 · study -->

*Oh how great is thy goodness, which thou hast laid up for them that fear thee; which thou hast wrought for them that trust in thee before the sons of men!*

The culmination of the Psalm is a tribute to the richness of God's mercy and fidelity in times of trouble, and the sure destruction of those that hate Him. 

God shall hide those that trust in Him from the wrath to come. Shield us from the wicked, guard us from the strife of their tongues, their slander and railing will come to nothing. 

Bless the Lord for His marvelous kindness. For the protection He has granted those that trust in Him. Even when we feel alone and desperate. Even the great prophet, Elijah, fresh from a great work for God, and a spiritual highpoint, felt alone and in his haste thought he was cut off from before God's eyes.

**Romans 11:3–4**
*Lord, they have killed thy prophets, and digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life. But what saith the answer of God unto him? I have reserved to myself seven thousand men, who have not bowed the knee to the image of Baal*.

We might have moments of doubt, when God's perfect timing isn't lining up with our own. When we think we cannot go on. That there are Jezebels and her men after us. That we are all alone. But God has reserved His own. We will be preserved. We can comfort ourselves and others with this truth. 

*O love the LORD, all ye his saints: for the LORD preserveth the faithful, and plentifully rewardeth the proud doer. Be of good courage, and he shall strengthen your heart, all ye that hope in the LORD.* 

Amen*.*

## Psalms 32  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-04

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17809

<!-- post 17809 · 2026-09-04 06:09 · scripture -->

**Psalms 32**
 *Blessed is he whose transgression is forgiven, whose sin is covered.*

*2 Blessed is the man unto whom the LORD imputeth not iniquity, and in whose spirit there is no guile.*

*3 When I kept silence, my bones waxed old through my roaring all the day long.*

*4 For day and night thy hand was heavy upon me: my moisture is turned into the drought of summer. Selah.*

*5 I acknowledged my sin unto thee, and mine iniquity have I not hid. I said, I will confess my transgressions unto the LORD; and thou forgavest the iniquity of my sin. Selah.*

*6 For this shall every one that is godly pray unto thee in a time when thou mayest be found: surely in the floods of great waters they shall not come nigh unto him.*

*7 Thou art my hiding place; thou shalt preserve me from trouble; thou shalt compass me about with songs of deliverance. Selah.*

*8 I will instruct thee and teach thee in the way which thou shalt go: I will guide thee with mine eye.*

*9 Be ye not as the horse, or as the mule, which have no understanding: whose mouth must be held in with bit and bridle, lest they come near unto thee.*

*10 Many sorrows shall be to the wicked: but he that trusteth in the LORD, mercy shall compass him about.*

*11 Be glad in the LORD, and rejoice, ye righteous: and shout for joy, all ye that are upright in heart.*

<!-- post 17810 · 2026-09-04 06:49 · study -->

**Psalm 32** opens with a very familiar and important doctrine, the understanding of God's forgiveness for sin in the saved believer's life. This foundational fact is repeated by Paul in the letter to the Romans;

**Romans 4:6–8**
*Even as David also describeth the blessedness of the man, unto whom God imputeth righteousness without works, Saying, Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered. Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not impute sin.*

The truth is that, in Christ, whether it was in David's day, or Paul's or our own, God forgives our past transgressions, covers our current sin, and will not hold our future sins and iniquity against us. 

This positional standing of righteousness before God, despite our sins, through His imputed righteousness is made possible, through the Holy Spirit, by the renewal, the regeneration, the quickening of our spirit. The new man within us. Blessed is the man *in whose spirit there is no guile.* 

We are given the Spirit of truth, that we may acknowledge our sin, knowing that we are forgiven of all sin, first, not by works but by faith. 

The same good news today as in David's day. And this Psalm serves as an evangelical appeal. 

David writes of a man who kept his silence, who hadn't called upon the name of the Lord, who suffered in their sins, bones waxing old and dried up by their roaring in the daytime, the heaviness of their iniquity weighing on them at night. 

But the honesty and humility of acknowledging sin, not hiding anything from God (as if we could), confessing our transgressions unto the Lord, and being forgiven, is a blessing. Rioting in the daytime and being kept up at night by our conscience, being convicted in the Spirit, gives way to the peace that passes understanding, through the forgiveness that is given to us by God's mercy and grace. 

David reminds us that there is a time where God may be sought, before it is too late. 

**Isaiah 55:6**
*Seek ye the LORD while he may be found, call ye upon him while he is near*

God's Spirit will not always strive with man. There comes a time when those that put off or reject the truth, not acknowledging that they are sinners, lying to God and themselves, are no longer able to come to Him. Where He no longer extends His hand, because they have hardened their hearts and will not seek Him, even as they face destruction. 

**Hebrews 4:7**
*Again, he limiteth a certain day, saying in David, To day, after so long a time; as it is said, To day if ye will hear his voice, harden not your hearts.*

We have the example of those in the days of Noah, who mocked him for his faith in preparing the ark, and whose disobedient spirits who Christ preached to in Hell, were destroyed by the flood, never coming nigh unto God. Just as those left to God's wrath to come will not repent, despite the horrors of His judgment. 

There is a time, it is today. There is a day, and it is the present. If you haven't accepted the truth that we are sinners in need of salvation, and that it is offered as a free gift to all who believe, through the grace of Almighty God, now is the time. 

**2 Corinthians 6:2**
*(For he saith, I have heard thee in a time accepted, and in the day of salvation have I succoured thee: behold, now is the accepted time; behold, now is the day of salvation.)*

<!-- post 17811 · 2026-09-04 06:58 · study -->

Those who have heard and responded to the truth, who in the appointed day prayed unto Him, can rest assured that we are saved. 

*Thou art my hiding place; thou shalt preserve me from trouble; thou shalt compass me about with songs of deliverance. Selah.*

This is the hope we have. The same hope the congregations of the children of Israel sang of in the temple of Solomon, with the songs of his father echoing this blessed promise, centuries before the first coming of Christ Jesus. 

And God responds to His children, in this inspired Psalm. Promising to teach and guide us in His ways. He asks we come willingly. That we have understanding to walk with Him in His ways, and not be like the horse or mule, needing a bit and bridle to pull us along. His yoke is light. The burden is easy. 

The wicked may suffer their sorrows of their own making, but we who are forgiven by His mercy because we trust in the Lord, are preserved. What an amazing relief to know we are already forgiven. That we may come boldly before God to sing His praises, glorifying Him for His mercies' sake. 

*Be glad in the LORD, and rejoice, ye righteous: and shout for joy, all ye that are upright in heart.* Amen!

<!-- post 17812 · 2026-09-04 07:35 · study -->

While it isn't entirely wrong to take the words transgression, sin and iniquity, and use them interchangeably, they do carry subtle differences in their meaning. 

Transgression is the wilful rebellion against the truth of God and His commands. The transgression of Adam, for example, having one law to obey, and choosing of their own volition (even as Eve was deceived, Adam most assuredly knew better), deciding to be disobedient. 

Sin, which the Bible says is the transgression of the Law, also carries with it the idea of missing the mark, falling short. 

**Romans 3:23**
*For all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God*

We aren't able, in the flesh, to hit the mark, the target being the perfect holiness of God. Only Jesus Christ did.

**2 Corinthians 5:21**
*For he hath made him to be sin for us, who knew no sin; that we might be made the righteousness of God in him.*

Iniquity comes from the Hebrew word, *Avon*, meaning to twist, bend, or distort. The perverted and crooked nature of fallen man. The bent toward evil, the guilt that stays with us, and the perverted character that drives the outward actions. It is the *guile* or deceit mentioned in verse 2.

Our natural tendencies towards willful rebellion, to sin, and to lie to ourselves and others, including God, about the evil that we do. Excusing ourselves with lies. 

Taken together, the blessed assurance that our transgressions, sins and iniquities are eternally forgiven demonstrates the mercy and grace of our Lord towards His children, through the suffering of Jesus. 

**Isaiah 53:5–6**
*But he was wounded for our transgressions, he was bruised for our iniquities: the chastisement of our peace was upon him; and with his stripes we are healed. All we like sheep have gone astray; we have turned every one to his own way; and the LORD hath laid on him the iniquity of us all.*

He suffered these things for our iniquity and transgressions, that we may be purged. That His righteousness be imputed on our ledger, even as His blood blots away our sins. 

Our transgressions are forgiven, our sin is covered, our iniquity will not be added to our account, because we are justified in Christ.

This accounting is a spiritual gift, applied to the ledger that in judgment, we are found not guilty by God. 

(Some call this Penal Substitutionary Atonement, the idea that Jesus paid the price of the debt we owe, so that we do not have to receive the wages of sin. Many reject this idea. Whatever you want to call it, it is undeniable that Christ suffered that those who trust on Him, won't)

On the cross, Jesus carried away our rebellion, covered our failures with His blood, and cleared the ledger of our crooked nature by imputing His own perfect righteousness to us. Past, present and future. That we may be made the righteousness of God.

<!-- post 17813 · 2026-09-04 08:07 · study -->

**Leviticus 16:2–30**
*And the LORD said unto Moses, Speak unto Aaron thy brother, that he come not at all times into the holy place within the vail before the mercy seat, which is upon the ark; that he die not: for I will appear in the cloud upon the mercy seat. Thus shall Aaron come into the holy place: with a young bullock for a sin offering, and a ram for a burnt offering. He shall put on the holy linen coat, and he shall have the linen breeches upon his flesh, and shall be girded with a linen girdle, and with the linen mitre shall he be attired: these are holy garments; therefore shall he wash his flesh in water, and so put them on. And he shall take of the congregation of the children of Israel two kids of the goats for a sin offering, and one ram for a burnt offering. And Aaron shall offer his bullock of the sin offering, which is for himself, and make an atonement for himself, and for his house. And he shall take the two goats, and present them before the LORD at the door of the tabernacle of the congregation. And Aaron shall cast lots upon the two goats; one lot for the LORD, and the other lot for the scapegoat. And Aaron shall bring the goat upon which the LORD’s lot fell, and offer him for a sin offering. But the goat, on which the lot fell to be the scapegoat, shall be presented alive before the LORD, to make an atonement with him, and to let him go for a scapegoat into the wilderness. And Aaron shall bring the bullock of the sin offering, which is for himself, and shall make an atonement for himself, and for his house, and shall kill the bullock of the sin offering which is for himself: And he shall take a censer full of burning coals of fire from off the altar before the LORD, and his hands full of sweet incense beaten small, and bring it within the vail: And he shall put the incense upon the fire before the LORD, that the cloud of the incense may cover the mercy seat that is upon the testimony, that he die not: And he shall take of the blood of the bullock, and sprinkle it with his finger upon the mercy seat eastward; and before the mercy seat shall he sprinkle of the blood with his finger seven times. Then shall he kill the goat of the sin offering, that is for the people, and bring his blood within the vail, and do with that blood as he did with the blood of the bullock, and sprinkle it upon the mercy seat, and before the mercy seat: And he shall make an atonement for the holy place, because of the uncleanness of the children of Israel, and because of their transgressions in all their sins: and so shall he do for the tabernacle of the congregation, that remaineth among them in the midst of their uncleanness. And there shall be no man in the tabernacle of the congregation when he goeth in to make an atonement in the holy place, until he come out, and have made an atonement for himself, and for his household, and for all the congregation of Israel. And he shall go out unto the altar that is before the LORD, and make an atonement for it; and shall take of the blood of the bullock, and of the blood of the goat, and put it upon the horns of the altar round about. And he shall sprinkle of the blood upon it with his finger seven times, and cleanse it, and hallow it from the uncleanness of the children of Israel. And when he hath made an end of reconciling the holy place, and the tabernacle of the congregation, and the altar, he shall bring the live goat: And Aaron shall lay both his hands upon the head of the live goat, and confess over him all the iniquities of the children of Israel, and all their transgressions in all their sins, putting them upon the head of the goat, and shall send him away by the hand of a fit man into the wilderness: And the goat shall bear upon him all their iniquities unto a land not inhabited: and he shall let go the goat in the wilderness.*

<!-- post 17814 · 2026-09-04 08:07 · study -->

*And Aaron shall come into the tabernacle of the congregation, and shall put off the linen garments, which he put on when he went into the holy place, and shall leave them there: And he shall wash his flesh with water in the holy place, and put on his garments, and come forth, and offer his burnt offering, and the burnt offering of the people, and make an atonement for himself, and for the people. And the fat of the sin offering shall he burn upon the altar. And he that let go the goat for the scapegoat shall wash his clothes, and bathe his flesh in water, and afterward come into the camp. And the bullock for the sin offering, and the goat for the sin offering, whose blood was brought in to make atonement in the holy place, shall one carry forth without the camp; and they shall burn in the fire their skins, and their flesh, and their dung. And he that burneth them shall wash his clothes, and bathe his flesh in water, and afterward he shall come into the camp. And this shall be a statute for ever unto you: that in the seventh month, on the tenth day of the month, ye shall afflict your souls, and do no work at all, whether it be one of your own country, or a stranger that sojourneth among you: For on that day shall the priest make an atonement for you, to cleanse you, that ye may be clean from all your sins before the LORD.*

This concept of atonement by the sacrifice, the scapegoat and the blood, is established in **Leviticus 16.**

Rather than the picture of the imperfect sacrifice, the blood of bulls and goats which could never take away our sin, we have the flawless Lamb, Who is also the ultimate High Priest, and one sacrifice for all time. 

**Hebrews 10:10–14**
*By the which will we are sanctified through the offering of the body of Jesus Christ once for all.*

<!-- post 17815 · 2026-09-04 08:07 · study -->

*And every priest standeth daily ministering and offering oftentimes the same sacrifices, which can never take away sins: But this man, after he had offered one sacrifice for sins for ever, sat down on the right hand of God; From henceforth expecting till his enemies be made his footstool. For by one offering he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified*.

He had no sin of His own to sacrifice for. He did not enter a man-made temple, but Heaven itself. He did not offer the blood of animals, but His own flawless blood.

Because of what Jesus did as both the High Priest and the Sacrifice, the work is finished. The ledger is cleared permanently. God no longer imputes our iniquity, our sins are eternally covered, and our transgressions have been carried as far as the east is from the west. And God will remember them no more.

<!-- post 17816 · 2026-09-04 08:27 · study -->

*Hebrews 10:11*
*And every priest standeth daily ministering and offering oftentimes the same sacrifices, which can never take away sins*

In the physical Tabernacle and the Temple of Solomon, there were no chairs. A priest could never sit down because his job was never done. The blood of bulls and goats only provided a temporary cover for the *remembrance of sins made again every year*, meaning the priest had to stay on his feet, continuously working, because he could never truly take away sins. 

But then we see Jesus, the author and most importantly, the finisher of our faith, sits down, on the right hand of the Father. 

**Hebrews 10:12–14**
*But this man, after he had offered one sacrifice for sins for ever, sat down on the right hand of God; From henceforth expecting till his enemies be made his footstool. For by one offering he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified.*

The contrast of those who stood, continually ministering, versus Jesus, Who sat down forever, having perfected them that are sanctified, is a subtle but crucial detail. An interesting side note to the study, that we shouldn't overlook.
