## Psalms 33  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-07

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17826

<!-- post 17826 · 2026-09-07 07:54 · scripture -->

**Psalms 33**
*Rejoice in the LORD, O ye righteous: for praise is comely for the upright.*

*2 Praise the LORD with harp: sing unto him with the psaltery and an instrument of ten strings.*

*3 Sing unto him a new song; play skilfully with a loud noise.*

*4 For the word of the LORD is right; and all his works are done in truth.*

*5 He loveth righteousness and judgment: the earth is full of the goodness of the LORD.*

*6 By the word of the LORD were the heavens made; and all the host of them by the breath of his mouth.*

*7 He gathereth the waters of the sea together as an heap: he layeth up the depth in storehouses.*

*8 Let all the earth fear the LORD: let all the inhabitants of the world stand in awe of him.*

*9 For he spake, and it was done; he commanded, and it stood fast.*

*10 The LORD bringeth the counsel of the heathen to nought: he maketh the devices of the people of none effect.*

*11 The counsel of the LORD standeth for ever, the thoughts of his heart to all generations.*

*12 Blessed is the nation whose God is the LORD; and the people whom he hath chosen for his own inheritance.*

*13 The LORD looketh from heaven; he beholdeth all the sons of men.*

*14 From the place of his habitation he looketh upon all the inhabitants of the earth.*

*15 He fashioneth their hearts alike; he considereth all their works.*

*16 There is no king saved by the multitude of an host: a mighty man is not delivered by much strength.*

*17 An horse is a vain thing for safety: neither shall he deliver any by his great strength.*

*18 Behold, the eye of the LORD is upon them that fear him, upon them that hope in his mercy;*

*19 To deliver their soul from death, and to keep them alive in famine.*

*20 Our soul waiteth for the LORD: he is our help and our shield.*

*21 For our heart shall rejoice in him, because we have trusted in his holy name.*

*22 Let thy mercy, O LORD, be upon us, according as we hope in thee.*

<!-- post 17827 · 2026-09-07 08:15 · study -->

**Psalm 33** opens with the clearest enforcement of writing, singing and performing songs of praise and worship to Almighty God. On stringed instruments, especially. Performing skillfully and with a loud noise within the congregation, songs with the doctrine of scripture, with the truth of His mighty works being honored. 

God's righteousness and judgment, His power and creation, the fear and adoration He commands from those that love the Lord to sing unto Him.

Book of Psalms explicitly names several musical instruments, including the harp, psaltery, timbrel, cymbal, trumpet, cornet, organ, and pipe. These are given instructions by the Psalmist and the music leader, and are used to provide melody and concordance to the congregational singing. An activity that we are encouraged to perform in the New Testament church. 

**Colossians 3:16**
*Let the word of Christ dwell in you richly in all wisdom; teaching and admonishing one another in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, singing with grace in your hearts to the Lord.*

Music is a form of learning and worship, as much as prayer, and it is edifying to the soul when focused on this spiritual truth, that it is for His sake that we make melodies. 

**Ephesians 5:19** 
*Speaking to yourselves in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, singing and making melody in your heart to the Lord*

What it is not, is an excuse to be carnal, to satisfy the flesh with worldly beats and discordant instruments, mimicking a rock concert or depraved discotheque. 

Congregational singing stirs the soul, lifting your voice up to the Lord. But many worship services turn into a tantric trance, lifting up hands and swaying, captivated by the music and beats, lasting hours at some churches. With songs that are thinly veiled pop love songs, with little to no doctrine applied.

Other churches take the instruments completely out of the equation, which seems at odds with the details given in the Bible. 

There is a middle ground, and a place for various musical accompaniments, while maintaining the orderly and decent service that all things at church should be done in.

<!-- post 17828 · 2026-09-07 08:22 · study -->

One thing that is clear, Heaven is a place full of song and worship. 

**Revelation 5:9–10**
*And they sung a new song, saying, Thou art worthy to take the book, and to open the seals thereof: for thou wast slain, and hast redeemed us to God by thy blood out of every kindred, and tongue, and people, and nation; And hast made us unto our God kings and priests: and we shall reign on the earth.*

And

**Revelation 14:2–3**
*And I heard a voice from heaven, as the voice of many waters, and as the voice of a great thunder: and I heard the voice of harpers harping with their harps: And they sung as it were a new song before the throne, and before the four beasts, and the elders: and no man could learn that song but the hundred and forty and four thousand, which were redeemed from the earth.*

And again

**Revelation 15:2–4**
*And I saw as it were a sea of glass mingled with fire: and them that had gotten the victory over the beast, and over his image, and over his mark, and over the number of his name, stand on the sea of glass, having the harps of God. And they sing the song of Moses the servant of God, and the song of the Lamb, saying, Great and marvellous are thy works, Lord God Almighty; just and true are thy ways, thou King of saints. Who shall not fear thee, O Lord, and glorify thy name? for thou only art holy: for all nations shall come and worship before thee; for thy judgments are made manifest.*

You can see in their singing and playing, that it pleases God, and the words being sung give witness to the truth that He is great and His judgments holy, and it is right to give the Lord praise and blessings through music. In that great by and by, we will be there, singing His praise. What a day that will be!

When we all get to heaven, / What a day of rejoicing that will be! / When we all see Jesus, / We’ll sing and shout the victory!

<!-- post 17829 · 2026-09-08 05:20 · study -->

Like all good spiritual songs, **Psalm 33** is dripping with doctrine, especially the inspired Psalms, because:

*For the word of the LORD is right; and all his works are done in truth*

His word and works are right and truth. Foundational doctrine. Amen. 

*He loveth righteousness and judgment: the earth is full of the goodness of the LORD.* Again, I say, Amen!

*By the word of the LORD were the heavens made; and all the host of them by the breath of his mouth*.

A song about singing the praises of the Lord that also teaches why. The creation of all things, by His Word, and brought into being by inspiration. The hosts of Heaven, the gatherings of the sea. His truth and righteous judgment. 

*Let all the earth fear the LORD: let all the inhabitants of the world stand in awe of him.* Amen, amen, amen!

We should sing of His glory. Why? Because of His might, we should fear Him, Who spoke all things into being. Who commanded and it was done. Whose Word made the foundations stand fast. Amen!

When taken as an instrument of teaching, Psalms serve to give perfect understanding to those that are singing. Models of prayer and adoration, given as examples and encouragement to those that fear the Lord. 

*The LORD bringeth the counsel of the heathen to nought: he maketh the devices of the people of none effect.*

The pride and arrogance of man, who without God, or His wisdom, attempt to plan their greatness, only to be dashed against the Rock. Only to have it all come to nothing. 

Contrast this futility with this truth; *The counsel of the LORD standeth for ever, the thoughts of his heart to all generations*. 

The knowledge of His ways, revealed by His word, through the oracles and prophets of God, bless a people. 

*Blessed is the nation whose God is the LORD; and the people whom he hath chosen for his own inheritance.* 

Note this is inclusive, not exclusive to one nation. Those He hath chosen for His own inheritance, any people that trust in Him, any nation that makes Him their God, is blessed. 

**Galatians 3:29**
*And if ye be Christ’s, then are ye Abraham’s seed, and heirs according to the promise.*

Because believers are elect in Christ, to receive these blessings as joint-heirs. 

**Romans 8:16–17**
*The Spirit itself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God: And if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together.*

This offer is extended to the whole world. At the time of David, the king understood that Israel was elected to be a beacon of God's light, not an elitist and special nation, to only know the true God. 

As the Bible says in **Isaiah 49:6** *I will also give thee for a light to the Gentiles, that thou mayest be my salvation unto the end of the earth.*

The only advantage Israel had was receiving the oracles of God, and the promise to David to bring about the Christ from his line, and stewardship of the city where He chose to place His name. Otherwise they'd have been as Sodom.

<!-- post 17830 · 2026-09-08 05:28 · study -->

*The LORD looketh from heaven; he beholdeth all the sons of men. From the place of his habitation he looketh upon all the inhabitants of the earth. He fashioneth their hearts alike; he considereth all their works.*

We are all the same in His eyes. We are all guilty before Him, convicted in sin. All are in need of a savior. 

The modern form of Jewish ethnic supremacy, a concept that as God's chosen people, it is because of their own qualities and holiness, is wicked. God is not a respecter of persons. 

He judges individuals by faith and nations by works. And the offer to give His blessings to any king who chose to place their trust in Him, rather than strength of horses and size of armies, was always extended. 

Nineveh was spared by their faith, believing the reluctant prophet Jonah. King Nebuchadnezzar's humbling 7 years in the wilderness struck with madness, led to him proclaiming the truth of God to the world. The Queen of the South came to Solomon in order to understand the truth of God.

*Behold, the eye of the LORD is upon them that fear him, upon them that hope in his mercy; To deliver their soul from death, and to keep them alive in famine.* Whosoever will come, He shall deliver. 

And so we sing. 

*Our soul waiteth for the LORD: he is our help and our shield. For our heart shall rejoice in him, because we have trusted in his holy name. Let thy mercy, O LORD, be upon us, according as we hope in thee.*

Amen!

## Psalms 34  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-09

<!-- post 17831 · 2026-09-09 05:56 · scripture -->

**Psalms 34**
*I will bless the LORD at all times: his praise shall continually be in my mouth.*

*2 My soul shall make her boast in the LORD: the humble shall hear thereof, and be glad.*

*3 O magnify the LORD with me, and let us exalt his name together.*

*4 I sought the LORD, and he heard me, and delivered me from all my fears.*

*5 They looked unto him, and were lightened: and their faces were not ashamed.*

*6 This poor man cried, and the LORD heard him, and saved him out of all his troubles.*

*7 The angel of the LORD encampeth round about them that fear him, and delivereth them.*

*8 O taste and see that the LORD is good: blessed is the man that trusteth in him.*

*9 O fear the LORD, ye his saints: for there is no want to them that fear him.*

*10 The young lions do lack, and suffer hunger: but they that seek the LORD shall not want any good thing.*

*11 Come, ye children, hearken unto me: I will teach you the fear of the LORD.*

*12 What man is he that desireth life, and loveth many days, that he may see good?*

*13 Keep thy tongue from evil, and thy lips from speaking guile.*

*14 Depart from evil, and do good; seek peace, and pursue it.*

*15 The eyes of the LORD are upon the righteous, and his ears are open unto their cry.*

*16 The face of the LORD is against them that do evil, to cut off the remembrance of them from the earth.*

*17 The righteous cry, and the LORD heareth, and delivereth them out of all their troubles.*

*18 The LORD is nigh unto them that are of a broken heart; and saveth such as be of a contrite spirit.*

*19 Many are the afflictions of the righteous: but the LORD delivereth him out of them all.*

*20 He keepeth all his bones: not one of them is broken.*

*21 Evil shall slay the wicked: and they that hate the righteous shall be desolate.*

*22 The LORD redeemeth the soul of his servants: and none of them that trust in him shall be desolate.*

<!-- post 17832 · 2026-09-09 06:01 · study -->

Another moving Psalm of deliverance, that has the gospel call front and center, and serves as yet another model prayer, blessing the Lord, praising Him, calling for His Kingdom, to the destruction of the enemy, seeking forgiveness, and acknowledgement of His faithfulness to deliver those that fear Him and call upon Him to be saved. 

Singing praise, making boast of the Lord. Letting our light shine and acknowledging Him in all our ways, that the humble, those predisposed to believe, will be drawn to repentance. 

*Let us exalt His name together!* Jesus. 

*I sought the Lord, and He heard me, and delivered me from all my fears.*

<!-- post 17833 · 2026-09-09 06:43 · study -->

Those that trust in the Lord know they shall never want. We are protected from evil by a host of angels. We are given our daily bread. 

**Psalms 37:25**
*I have been young, and now am old; yet have I not seen the righteous forsaken, nor his seed begging bread*.

We may not be rich, we may not be feasting on expensive meals. But the Lord will provide for his own. 

And the Lord promises us that we shall have a long life with good in it if we keep ourselves from speaking lies and doing evil, and rather seek peace and pursue it. Loving our neighbors. 

While the Commandments of the Lord serve to let us know that we have fallen short of His perfect ways, they are given that it be well with us. That if we follow them, we have an easier life full of blessings. They are for our benefit, not to oppress us. 

We are judged by the law of Liberty, not saved by our works. But our works can benefit us in our service to God. 

God's eyes are upon us and he listens for our prayers, when we walk in the truth. And he is against those who commit evil, and he chastens every son He receives.  

*Many are the afflictions of the righteous: but the LORD delivereth him out of them all.*

**2 Timothy 3:12**
*Yea, and all that will live godly in Christ Jesus shall suffer persecution*.

While we are promised a longer life with more blessings, we are not promised a carefree life that has no suffering. Often the suffering we experience is for our benefit to help us grow in the Lord, and to increase our faith. 

Praise and obedience and calling upon the Lord, and the tribulations that we suffer, all work together for good. Because they increase our faith. 

The LORD redeemeth the soul of his servants: and none of them that trust in him shall be desolate.

This is the promise given to all believers. That once we are saved we are always saved.b that we shall never be left desolate. Will never be ashamed for our trust in the Lord. 

He will redeem The souls of his servants and bring us out of tribulation and into Glory, in His holy name. Amen

<!-- post 17834 · 2026-09-10 05:39 · study -->

*O fear the LORD, ye his saints: for there is no want to them that fear him.*

Believers, sanctified in the Spirit by faith in the risen Christ, are given the promise that the Lord will redeem us, His servants. 

But His provision for us extends beyond the fact of eternal salvation, and gives us hope and comfort here, in this life. Our daily bread, shelter, clothing, we shall not want for any good thing. 

But more, He's given us His word, and His standards for separation that we may fear the Lord and be blessed by having a longer life and see good within the years we are allotted. 

His burden is light. Don't speak evil of others, don't lie. Don't seek to harm others, but do good and seek peace.

**Romans 12:18**
*If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men.*

Vengeance is Mine, sayeth the Lord. He will repay. We not only believe He is, and a rewarder of those that diligently seek Him, but that He is our shield and strength against our enemies. We do not need to fight a battle, but trust in Him to fight for us. We simply need to walk in the Spirit, love our enemies, hate His, and seek others' profit. Doing good. 

If we want our Father's blessing, live righteously. He hears the righteous and answers their cry. 

We are known by our love for the brethren. By our edifying one another. 

**Hebrews 10:22–25**
*Let us draw near with a true heart in full assurance of faith, having our hearts sprinkled from an evil conscience, and our bodies washed with pure water. Let us hold fast the profession of our faith without wavering; (for he is faithful that promised;) And let us consider one another to provoke unto love and to good works: Not forsaking the assembling of ourselves together, as the manner of some is; but exhorting one another: and so much the more, as ye see the day approaching.*

You don't want the face of God against you in your time of need. 

**Hebrews 10:26–29**
*For if we sin wilfully after that we have received the knowledge of the truth, there remaineth no more sacrifice for sins, But a certain fearful looking for of judgment and fiery indignation, which shall devour the adversaries. He that despised Moses’ law died without mercy under two or three witnesses: Of how much sorer punishment, suppose ye, shall he be thought worthy, who hath trodden under foot the Son of God, and hath counted the blood of the covenant, wherewith he was sanctified, an unholy thing, and hath done despite unto the Spirit of grace?*

God will chasten His children. He will shorten your life and withhold blessings, and even bring you home for not doing His work at His pleasure. 

**Hebrews 10:31**
*It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God.*

Note that we are expected to love and care for our fellow saints. And where do we do this? On the called out assembly of believers. Our local church. Forsaking this opportunity is spiteful to the Spirit of grace which has bought you. Go to church!

<!-- post 17835 · 2026-09-10 05:53 · study -->

In addition to the worship and praise of God, the recognition that His burden is light and He rewards us for bearing it, and the comfort that in our suffering and trouble, He is there, working it for good, **Psalm 34** contains a prophetic verse of Christ;

*He keepeth all his bones: not one of them is broken.*

**John 19:31–37**
*The Jews therefore, because it was the preparation, that the bodies should not remain upon the cross on the sabbath day, (for that sabbath day was an high day,) besought Pilate that their legs might be broken, and that they might be taken away. Then came the soldiers, and brake the legs of the first, and of the other which was crucified with him. But when they came to Jesus, and saw that he was dead already, they brake not his legs: But one of the soldiers with a spear pierced his side, and forthwith came there out blood and water. And he that saw it bare record, and his record is true: and he knoweth that he saith true, that ye might believe. For these things were done, that the scripture should be fulfilled, A bone of him shall not be broken. And again another scripture saith, They shall look on him whom they pierced.*

Nothing in Scripture is incidental, accidental or coincidental. And ultimately, everything points to Jesus.

**Exodus 12:46**
*In one house shall it be eaten; thou shalt not carry forth ought of the flesh abroad out of the house; neither shall ye break a bone thereof.*

The Passover lamb being a clear type of Christ, the Lamb slain before the foundation of the world. The instructions repeated again, to emphasize the importance:

**Numbers 9:12**
*They shall leave none of it unto the morning, nor break any bone of it: according to all the ordinances of the passover they shall keep it.*

The picture of Christ given throughout Old Testament scripture shows the power of His word, knowing the end from the beginning. 

**1 Corinthians 1:27–29**
*But God hath chosen the foolish things of the world to confound the wise; and God hath chosen the weak things of the world to confound the things which are mighty; And base things of the world, and things which are despised, hath God chosen, yea, and things which are not, to bring to nought things that are: That no flesh should glory in his presence.*

There is no wisdom of this world that can explain the prophetic nature of scripture. It is foolishness to those that reject the truth. Yet the sheer overwhelming number of things which are not that become things that are, shows the glory of God and the folly of man. 

 And the faithfulness of prophecy shows us the power of God to keep His promises, and increase our faith. Amen

## Psalms 35  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-15

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17842

<!-- post 17842 · 2026-09-15 05:17 · scripture -->

**Psalms 35**
*Plead my cause, O LORD, with them that strive with me: fight against them that fight against me.*

*2 Take hold of shield and buckler, and stand up for mine help.*

*3 Draw out also the spear, and stop the way against them that persecute me: say unto my soul, I am thy salvation.*

*4 Let them be confounded and put to shame that seek after my soul: let them be turned back and brought to confusion that devise my hurt.*

*5 Let them be as chaff before the wind: and let the angel of the LORD chase them.*

*6 Let their way be dark and slippery: and let the angel of the LORD persecute them.*

*7 For without cause have they hid for me their net in a pit, which without cause they have digged for my soul.*

*8 Let destruction come upon him at unawares; and let his net that he hath hid catch himself: into that very destruction let him fall.*

*9 And my soul shall be joyful in the LORD: it shall rejoice in his salvation.*

*10 All my bones shall say, LORD, who is like unto thee, which deliverest the poor from him that is too strong for him, yea, the poor and the needy from him that spoileth him?*

*11 False witnesses did rise up; they laid to my charge things that I knew not.*

*12 They rewarded me evil for good to the spoiling of my soul.*

*13 But as for me, when they were sick, my clothing was sackcloth: I humbled my soul with fasting; and my prayer returned into mine own bosom.*

*14 I behaved myself as though he had been my friend or brother: I bowed down heavily, as one that mourneth for his mother.*

*15 But in mine adversity they rejoiced, and gathered themselves together: yea, the abjects gathered themselves together against me, and I knew it not; they did tear me, and ceased not:*

*16 With hypocritical mockers in feasts, they gnashed upon me with their teeth.*

*17 Lord, how long wilt thou look on? rescue my soul from their destructions, my darling from the lions.*

*18 I will give thee thanks in the great congregation: I will praise thee among much people.*

*19 Let not them that are mine enemies wrongfully rejoice over me: neither let them wink with the eye that hate me without a cause.*

*20 For they speak not peace: but they devise deceitful matters against them that are quiet in the land.*

*21 Yea, they opened their mouth wide against me, and said, Aha, aha, our eye hath seen it.*

*22 This thou hast seen, O LORD: keep not silence: O Lord, be not far from me.*

*23 Stir up thyself, and awake to my judgment, even unto my cause, my God and my Lord.*

*24 Judge me, O LORD my God, according to thy righteousness; and let them not rejoice over me.*

*25 Let them not say in their hearts, Ah, so would we have it: let them not say, We have swallowed him up.*

*26 Let them be ashamed and brought to confusion together that rejoice at mine hurt: let them be clothed with shame and dishonour that magnify themselves against me.*

*27 Let them shout for joy, and be glad, that favour my righteous cause: yea, let them say continually, Let the LORD be magnified, which hath pleasure in the prosperity of his servant.*

*28 And my tongue shall speak of thy righteousness and of thy praise all the day long.*

<!-- post 17843 · 2026-09-15 05:38 · study -->

**Psalm 35** is a model of prayer that carries with it both the plea for deliverance, and imprecatory, or negative prayers against enemies who seek our hurt. 

In David's case, we understand that he was falsely accused, had enemies trying to literally kill him and destroy him. 

We may not have the threat against our lives, but we can relate. 

The Lord is our Judge of judges. We go boldly before the throne of grace, asking anything, that we may plead our cause. Asking God to get involved in our lives, to fight our fights. 

We have such an awesome advocate in Christ Jesus and a Father Whose mercy and judgement is righteous and true. It is foolishness not to go before Him in prayer, knowing that He seeks our prosperity. Not the riches and accumulations of things, as the false teachers preach, but our overall benefit and success spiritually, that we may do His will. 

So, of course when we have enemies in this life that seek our hurt, it would be natural that we get God involved. 

*Draw out also the spear, and stop the way against them that persecute me*. That is to form a flank before us, a hedge of protection, like a wall of men, spear extended. A host to shield us from the advances against us. To show us and comfort us with the truth that He is our salvation. 

Let our enemies be confounded and confused. Let them be scattered as the chaff. Let the angel of the Lord be our guardian. His children have guardian angels;

**Hebrews 1:14**
*Are they not all ministering spirits, sent forth to minister for them who shall be heirs of salvation?*

We can call upon Him to give them direction to not only shield us, but it persecute them that seek to do us harm. 

But we shouldn't misunderstand that these ministering spirits are ours to control, or to plea to them. Many pray directly to angels, or archangels, which is wicked. 

**Revelation 22:8–9**
*And I John saw these things, and heard them. And when I had heard and seen, I fell down to worship before the feet of the angel which shewed me these things. Then saith he unto me, See thou do it not: for I am thy fellowservant, and of thy brethren the prophets, and of them which keep the sayings of this book: worship God.*

We pray, as David did, to the Living God. He directs His hosts. 

The Bible says that false teachers will mislead people into worshiping angels. 

**Colossians 2:18**
*Let no man beguile you of your reward in a voluntary humility and worshipping of angels, intruding into those things which he hath not seen, vainly puffed up by his fleshly mind*.

Pray directly to God in all things. Pray for Him to dispatch His angels to defend us and attack our enemies, as David demonstrates here.

<!-- post 17844 · 2026-09-15 05:50 · study -->

Like David, our enemies may seek to trap us, secretly. Digging pits for us to fall into, snares to catch us, to spoil us, taking what is ours, even if it is simply our reputation, and in doing so, ruin our testimony to others. 

If the seek to ruin us in secret, then let the Lord's destruction come upon them without warning. Let their plans come to nothing and better still, catch them up in their own devices. 

*And my soul shall be joyful in the LORD: it shall rejoice in his salvation.*

But, what about *love thy enemies*? 

Notice that David is pleading for their destruction, not only for their acts against him, but because of their false witness and betrayal. 

They rewarded his good with evil. 

When they were sick, he humbled himself in prayer and fasting. He sought intercession for them with the Lord. He shows them love. 

This is what we are to do, what Jesus asked of us. 

**Matthew 5:44**
*But I say unto you, Love your enemies, bless them that curse you, do good to them that hate you, and pray for them which despitefully use you, and persecute you*

This seems contradictory, but it's not. When they are weak, or in need, we are to love them. To pray for their healing and to give to them the charity we wish they'd show us. 

We may win them as brothers, or heap hot coals on their heads by our kindness. 

But we do not have any obligations to pray they succeed in the evil they do, and while we should not seek vengeance ourselves, we can leave it to the Judge of all to take up our cause and dispatch justice against them. 

If we behave as a friend or brother, and mourn with them as their mother, and they still choose to persecute us? That's on them. They can rejoice in our pain, gather together to our hurt, but the Lord will see and repay. 

*With hypocritical mockers in feasts, they gnashed upon me with their teeth.*

They may make a feast of our lives, inviting other wicked men to join them, until they seemingly turn the whole world against us, yet we have the Lord. 

**Romans 8:31**
*What shall we then say to these things? If God be for us, who can be against us?*

<!-- post 17845 · 2026-09-15 06:02 · study -->

Pleading with the Lord to not tarry, to come quickly to our aid, with the knowledge that we will sing His praises for His salvation, is a key piece of prayer. 

We have discussed importunity in prayer, asking God again and again to please help us in our time of need. When we are past the point of what we think we can stand, to give us relief. 

We have nothing to give but our love and praise in exchange. 

Some try to bargain with God, usually when we pray amiss, seeking our will rather than His. "I'll go to church more, I will read my Bible more." I will stop this sin or that. We offer sacrifices of our own time or effort or even money. 

But that's not what God seeks. Those are things that are the least we could do, even as unprofitable servants. 

What we do have is praise for His power and His strength. And He is due all the glory. 

*I will give thee thanks in the great congregation: I will praise thee among much people.*

When we bargain with God for what we want, when we add our efforts and barter for His, we give ourselves a means to boast. We can fool ourselves into thinking our works played a part in the outcome. We begin to forget how helpless we were, and how God deserves all the glory. We begin to see our efforts as the reason God "owed it to me" rather than His love and righteousness. 

And so, we may keep asking God for His intervention, and we may remind Him that our gratitude will be expressed to His worship and to show others His strength and power. 

But avoid bargaining or anything that shares the glory with Almighty God. Trust that He loves you and will, in His due time, make whatever it is you are going through to be handled to our good and His will. 

**Romans 8:28**
*And we know that all things work together for good to them that love God, to them who are the called according to his purpose.*

<!-- post 17846 · 2026-09-15 06:04 · study -->

Pray that our enemies be confounded. That those that do not fear the Lord come to see the bitter end of the wicked. Let man see His wrath on the unrighteous. Let the praise and rejoicing of His people reveal the mercy of God from Heaven. 

*Let them shout for joy, and be glad, that favour my righteous cause: yea, let them say continually, Let the LORD be magnified, which hath pleasure in the prosperity of his servant. And my tongue shall speak of thy righteousness and of thy praise all the day long.* Amen

**Psalms 36**
*The transgression of the wicked saith within my heart, that there is no fear of God before his eyes.*

*2 For he flattereth himself in his own eyes, until his iniquity be found to be hateful.*

*3 The words of his mouth are iniquity and deceit: he hath left off to be wise, and to do good.*

*4 He deviseth mischief upon his bed; he setteth himself in a way that is not good; he abhorreth not evil.*

*5 Thy mercy, O LORD, is in the heavens; and thy faithfulness reacheth unto the clouds.*

*6 Thy righteousness is like the great mountains; thy judgments are a great deep: O LORD, thou preservest man and beast.*

*7 How excellent is thy lovingkindness, O God! therefore the children of men put their trust under the shadow of thy wings.*

*8 They shall be abundantly satisfied with the fatness of thy house; and thou shalt make them drink of the river of thy pleasures.*

*9 For with thee is the fountain of life: in thy light shall we see light.*

*10 O continue thy lovingkindness unto them that know thee; and thy righteousness to the upright in heart.*

<!-- post 17848 · 2026-09-16 05:36 · study -->

A short song ruminating on the wicked, **Psalm 36** turns this doctrine into prayer for the children of God, singing His praise. 

The workers of iniquity come to a bitter end. They are cast down and fallen. Hell will be their home. 

**Matthew 7:22–23**
*Many will say to me in that day, Lord, Lord, have we not prophesied in thy name? and in thy name have cast out devils? and in thy name done many wonderful works? And then will I profess unto them, I never knew you: depart from me, ye that work iniquity.*

They trust themselves, their "wonderful works," and with their lips they may claim the Lord, but their heart is far from Him. 

Their pride comes as a snare. They do not fear God, and it is evidenced by the boldness of their sin. They lie to themselves, flattering their deeds to justify them in their own eyes until their sin is found hateful, that is, to be despised. 

They lie to themselves, are haters of truth. They reject wisdom and well doing, and rather seek mischief and evil for others. They go out of their way to treat others spitefully, while not hating evil as we are commanded to. 

**Romans 1:32**
*Who knowing the judgment of God, that they which commit such things are worthy of death, not only do the same, but have pleasure in them that do them.*

We are commanded to hate evil and cleave to what is good. They take pleasure in iniquity, even in seeing other wicked men committing it. 

But for them that fear God, His mercy extends forever, His faithfulness spans the heavens. His righteousness stands like great mountains and His judgments as the great deep of oceans. 

We sing praise to His lovingkindness and seek the protection of being under His wing. His provision for us is the *fatness of* His *house*, being in Christ makes us to drink if the living waters, the fountain of life, the river of His pleasure, constantly flowing to those who love God. 

He is the true light, and in it we see Jesus. 

**John 1:4–5**
*In him was life; and the life was the light of men. And the light shineth in darkness; and the darkness comprehended it not.*

The contrast between the wicked and righteous is the fear of the Lord. The love of truth and abhorring evil. The prideful versus the humility of falling before God, and seeking His lovingkindness and protection, and receiving His imputed righteousness upon the upright of heart. Amen

<!-- post 17850 · 2026-09-17 05:53 · study -->

**Psalm 37** continues the theme of the wicked compared to the upright, but the tone here shifts away from the plea to be delivered from present and ready evil, to triumphant declaration of the mercy of God towards His saints. The blessed hope and the truth of judgment as a comfort for the believer. 

Paul writes in **Philippians 4:6**
*Be careful for nothing*... We are commanded not to fear and anxiety over potential persecution is a sin. We are told here to *fret not because of evildoers*. 

The courage we are expected to show as Christians isn't based on our own efforts or strength, it is based on the power of our God. And consider the next stanza of the opening verse, *neither be thou envious against the workers of iniquity.*

We will see that they have their rewards, our inheritance is to be preserved in this life from hunger and taken care of with our basic needs. The meek shall inherit the earth, while the workers of iniquity shall be destroyed and their seed cut off. 

We may understand that evil loses in the end, and we have already overcome the world. Faith in this truth should lift us up. 

But more, these two concepts go together in a physiological truth. God has built us in a way that makes our gratitude for what we have the source for being less anxious. 

This isn't just a "Hey, don't worry and don't envy the wicked, it's gonna work out" platitude. 

Science shows that gratitude, being satisfied and thankful for what you have decreases activity in the amygdala, which is the part of the brain that triggers fear, panic, and stress. It moves our bodies away from the fearful "fight-or-flight" mode and into the "rest-and-digest" mode. 

It is impossible for those that are truly grateful to God for their station in life, their economic conditions, the blessings He has given us, and the protection of His mighty hand, to fret. To feel anxiety. To fear. 

Practicing thankfulness boosts dopamine and serotonin, which naturally lower stress hormones like cortisol.

Our brain struggles to focus on threat-detection (anxiety) and present-moment safety (gratitude) at the same time.

**Philippians 4:6** continues, ...*but in every thing by prayer and supplication with thanksgiving let your requests be made known unto God.* 

Science once again catches up with the truth God has given us in His word. 

And so, when we are commanded to not be envious of evildoers, and given reasons to be thankful, God is giving us the means to not fret. 

Gratitude for all He does, being satisfied and knowing how to be abased and abound, naturally makes us less anxious. To fear not.

<!-- post 17851 · 2026-09-18 05:32 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalm 37**, God promises that the wicked shall be cut down like grass, while those that trust in the Lord and do good shall dwell in the land and be fed. 

This is a crucial detail in the Christian life, and the fate of our families and nation. The conditional understanding, that those who trust in the Lord, His people, the children of God by faith in Christ Jesus, are given the opportunity to have life, in abundance, though obedience to the commandments of God. To do good. 

We are given eternal life by faith in Christ Jesus alone. Trusting God. But our lives on this earth are judged by the good that we do.

People are judged by faith. Nations are judged by Christians' works. 

**2 Chronicles 7:14**
*If my people, which are called by my name, shall humble themselves, and pray, and seek my face, and turn from their wicked ways; then will I hear from heaven, and will forgive their sin, and will heal their land.*

It is a popular verse, quoted often from pulpits across America. And it shows that there is a solution to the chaos and depravity affecting our society, and that solution is not found in consuming political commentary or voting. It is turning towards God and away from evil, as His people. 

**Matthew 5:13**
*Ye are the salt of the earth: but if the salt have lost his savour, wherewith shall it be salted? it is thenceforth good for nothing, but to be cast out, and to be trodden under foot of men.*

Salt is a preservative that keeps meat from spoiling. It not only stops the rotting, but improves the flavor and texture of it when cooked. When tried by the fire. 

We are to be that salt, and when the Christian loses his savour, we see that we become useless, and are left to be trodden down, and rightfully so. 

This is in contrast with the earlier verse;

**Matthew 5:10–12**
*Blessed are they which are persecuted for righteousness’ sake: for theirs is the kingdom of heaven. Blessed are ye, when men shall revile you, and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil against you falsely, for my sake. Rejoice, and be exceeding glad: for great is your reward in heaven: for so persecuted they the prophets which were before you.*

We often hear Christians complain about being vexed by the filthy conversation of the wicked, and who treat the inevitable trodding down caused by our lack of commitment and separation, as persecution for righteousness sake. They are not the same. 

In fact, many Christians live as the world, not doing good that they may dwell in the land, and act surprised when the wicked prosper and our nation is flooded with strangers, bringing their idolatry and paganism with them. 

They act as though they are being persecuted by politicians and corrupt leaders, while in reality we are the cause. 

We become fearful and resentful, fretting about evildoers and being envious of the workers of iniquity. 

We ignore God's prescriptions for what ails us, and so we forsake the promises of safety and provision He has given us.

<!-- post 17852 · 2026-09-18 05:47 · study -->

God promises those that trust in Him and do good will be given the desires of our hearts. 

**Jeremiah 17:9–10**
*The heart is deceitful above all things, and desperately wicked: who can know it? I the LORD search the heart, I try the reins, even to give every man according to his ways, and according to the fruit of his doings.*

When we are right with God, and do according to His ways, our heart's desires are His. They are upright and good, because our will becomes His when in all our ways we acknowledge Him, and allow the Lord to direct our paths. 

He tries the reins, and sees our good works and rewards us openly. We consider the poor and broken-hearted. We take up the cause of the fatherless and widows. We spread the good news of eternal life in Christ Jesus. We live lives separated unto the gospel, and consider the profit of others above our own. Our desires become for the good of others. 

*Lord help me to live from day to day*
*In such a self-forgetful way,*
*That even when I kneel to pray*
*My prayer shall be for others*.

This hymn encapsulates the concept. 

**James 1:27**
*Pure religion and undefiled before God and the Father is this, To visit the fatherless and widows in their affliction, and to keep himself unspotted from the world.*

When we consider others, when we love our neighbors and even our enemies, when we practice pure religion, the outward works of our faith, it should be for others. 

And our keeping ourselves from the filth of the world is part of that, that God will see our good works, and give us our desires, and make all things come to pass. 

*He shall bring forth thy righteousness as the light, and thy judgment as the noonday.*

We are the light of the world. Our purpose is to magnify the light within us that others, yes others, see it and glorify God.

**Matthew 5:16**
*Let your light so shine before men, that they may see your good works, and glorify your Father which is in heaven.*

By trusting the Lord and doing good, we preserve our nation, we get to dwell in the land, we are fed, and we see our hearts turn from desiring our own wicked lusts, to seeking good for others.

## Psalms 38  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-21

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17854

<!-- post 17854 · 2026-09-21 05:26 · scripture -->

**Psalms 38**
*O LORD, rebuke me not in thy wrath: neither chasten me in thy hot displeasure.*

*2 For thine arrows stick fast in me, and thy hand presseth me sore.*

*3 There is no soundness in my flesh because of thine anger; neither is there any rest in my bones because of my sin.*

*4 For mine iniquities are gone over mine head: as an heavy burden they are too heavy for me.*

*5 My wounds stink and are corrupt because of my foolishness.*

*6 I am troubled; I am bowed down greatly; I go mourning all the day long.*

*7 For my loins are filled with a loathsome disease: and there is no soundness in my flesh.*

*8 I am feeble and sore broken: I have roared by reason of the disquietness of my heart.*

*9 Lord, all my desire is before thee; and my groaning is not hid from thee.*

*10 My heart panteth, my strength faileth me: as for the light of mine eyes, it also is gone from me.*

*11 My lovers and my friends stand aloof from my sore; and my kinsmen stand afar off.*

*12 They also that seek after my life lay snares for me: and they that seek my hurt speak mischievous things, and imagine deceits all the day long.*

*13 But I, as a deaf man, heard not; and I was as a dumb man that openeth not his mouth.*

*14 Thus I was as a man that heareth not, and in whose mouth are no reproofs.*

*15 For in thee, O LORD, do I hope: thou wilt hear, O Lord my God.*

*16 For I said, Hear me, lest otherwise they should rejoice over me: when my foot slippeth, they magnify themselves against me.*

*17 For I am ready to halt, and my sorrow is continually before me.*

*18 For I will declare mine iniquity; I will be sorry for my sin.*

*19 But mine enemies are lively, and they are strong: and they that hate me wrongfully are multiplied.*

*20 They also that render evil for good are mine adversaries; because I follow the thing that good is.*

*21 Forsake me not, O LORD: O my God, be not far from me.*

*22 Make haste to help me, O Lord my salvation.*

<!-- post 17855 · 2026-09-21 06:05 · study -->

It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the Loving God. The Lord's chastening can be fierce, and our wish should be to acknowledge our wrongdoing, and fear God. 

Recognizing the predicaments we may find ourselves in are our own making, and calling upon the Lord to help is the only way out of whatever we have fallen into. Whatever snare we are caught in. Whatever arrows the Lord sends our way as a wake-up call, there is no relief except confession and a plea for mercy from the longsuffering God of our salvation. 

In **Genesis 4**, we find Cain being cursed with punishment from the Lord for the first murder, the blood of Cain's brother, righteous Abel, and he says to the Lord;

**Genesis 4:13**
*And Cain said unto the LORD, My punishment is greater than I can bear.*

There was no remorse for what he had done, just sorrow at the consequence. But David paints the picture of a repentant sinner, recognizing that the punishment must be just, because it comes from God. 

*There is no soundness in my flesh because of thine anger; neither is there any rest in my bones because of my sin.*

Where Cain went about his day, and lied without a qualm when asked of God about Abel; *Am I my brother's keeper?* 

In contrast, David, as a saved believer, has become unable to rest because of the guilt his sin carries. It is his iniquities that are the burden he cannot bear. 

Faced with his sin and the consequences, it is David's sin that worries him more. 

We should keep this perspective. To not go the way of Cain. Notice too, that rather than seeking healing and relief from the punishment by confession and having a contrite heart, Cain's concern is for his own life. God leaves a mark on Cain to ensure he lives and suffers for it. 

But we who are His are commanded to hate iniquity. Even our own. Especially our own. 

**Romans 12:9**
*Let love be without dissimulation. Abhor that which is evil; cleave to that which is good.*

**Proverbs 8:13**
*The fear of the LORD is to hate evil: pride, and arrogancy, and the evil way, and the froward mouth, do I hate*.

David sees the wounds caused by his sin as festering and infected, stinking. Literal or metaphorical, we all see the damage of sin in our lives. The harm it's caused. 

We may not be spared the consequences, but we need not continue in the chastening that comes with unconfessed sin. 

The loathsome disease that rots our flesh, that makes us feeble and sore broken. The disquietude of our hearts. This may be spiritual, it may be in our soul and how we feel, it may be the effect of sin in our bodies, literal and tangible. 

Sin manifests in our hearts and in our bodies, and brings men to conviction when we fear God. 

David, who in **Psalm 32** sang of the blessedness of the man whose sins are forgiven, covered and not imputed against them, is now singing of the wounds, failing strength, bitter loneliness as a result of his actions. Abandoned and betrayed. 

In his sin, he has made friends with those that seek His life. He was deaf to their intentions. He did not speak up against them. Now he's between the reproof of God and the mischief of the wicked, and has no recourse but to seek the Lord. 

*For in thee, O LORD, do I hope: thou wilt hear, O Lord my God.*

No matter how desperate our situation, no matter how foolish or wicked we've been, no matter the cause for our misery; be it the chastening of the Lord or the betrayal of those we've given breadth to do evil, the mercies of our Father, seeking His face and His grace, are always there for those who have trusted in Him. He has promised. God Who cannot lie. Almighty God. So we know we can turn to Him. 

Because He is our hope. Because evildoers may magnify themselves in our failures. Because we are willing to admit we are helpless without Him. To come up halt, stumbling and falling, unable to go on. Sorrow continually before us. 

Because we declare our iniquity, admitting our wrongdoing, being sorry for what we have done. 

For these reasons, we can cry out to the Lord with confidence to deliver us.

<!-- post 17856 · 2026-09-21 06:05 · study -->

Finally, David reminds God that many of his adversaries are wicked themselves, they render evil for good, persecuting David for doing things righteously, and hating him for it. 

Crying out for God to make haste and plead his cause, asking Him to not leave us or forsake us, reminding Him of His promises, and really just acknowledging to the all-knowing Lord that we remember them and trust in those promises.

<!-- post 17857 · 2026-09-21 06:10 · study -->

This model prayer begins with a plea to God to not judge us too harshly, for Him to know we understand, we got the message. To confess our iniquity. To abhor it. To cry out the pain we are in, and seek relief. To admit our role in our misery, even if it is the evil of others taking advantage of our failures. And reminding ourselves by reminding God of His promises and our trust in Him. 

*Forsake me not, O LORD: O my God, be not far from me. Make haste to help me, O Lord my salvation.* Amen

<!-- post 17858 · 2026-09-21 14:53 · study -->

**2 Corinthians 7:9–11**
*Now I rejoice, not that ye were made sorry, but that ye sorrowed to repentance: for ye were made sorry after a godly manner, that ye might receive damage by us in nothing. For godly sorrow worketh repentance to salvation not to be repented of: but the sorrow of the world worketh death. For behold this selfsame thing, that ye sorrowed after a godly sort, what carefulness it wrought in you, yea, what clearing of yourselves, yea, what indignation, yea, what fear, yea, what vehement desire, yea, what zeal, yea, what revenge! In all things ye have approved yourselves to be clear in this matter.*

This exchange perfectly encapsulates the fundamental difference between the sorrow of the world, held by Cain, and the godly sorrow, expressed by David. 

The burden Cain couldn't bear was the punishment, but he sorrowed not unto repentance. He never changed his mind about what he had done. 

David's sorrow is godly, he cannot bear his own iniquity and hates his sin that brought him low, rather than resenting the punishment he'd received. 

The difference between worldly sorrow that works death versus godly sorrow that works repentance to salvation, being delivered from the scourging, the ancillary consequences and from the scorners and those that plot mischief, taking advantage of our broken and contrite state. 

**Hebrews 12:11–13**
*Now no chastening for the present seemeth to be joyous, but grievous: nevertheless afterward it yieldeth the peaceable fruit of righteousness unto them which are exercised thereby. Wherefore lift up the hands which hang down, and the feeble knees; And make straight paths for your feet, lest that which is lame be turned out of the way; but let it rather be healed.*

## Psalms 39  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-23

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17861

<!-- post 17861 · 2026-09-23 05:04 · scripture -->

**Psalms 39**
*I said, I will take heed to my ways, that I sin not with my tongue: I will keep my mouth with a bridle, while the wicked is before me.*

*2 I was dumb with silence, I held my peace, even from good; and my sorrow was stirred.*

*3 My heart was hot within me, while I was musing the fire burned: then spake I with my tongue,*

*4 LORD, make me to know mine end, and the measure of my days, what it is: that I may know how frail I am.*

*5 Behold, thou hast made my days as an handbreadth; and mine age is as nothing before thee: verily every man at his best state is altogether vanity. Selah.*

*6 Surely every man walketh in a vain shew: surely they are disquieted in vain: he heapeth up riches, and knoweth not who shall gather them.*

*7 And now, Lord, what wait I for? my hope is in thee.*

*8 Deliver me from all my transgressions: make me not the reproach of the foolish.*

*9 I was dumb, I opened not my mouth; because thou didst it.*

*10 Remove thy stroke away from me: I am consumed by the blow of thine hand.*

*11 When thou with rebukes dost correct man for iniquity, thou makest his beauty to consume away like a moth: surely every man is vanity. Selah.*

*12 Hear my prayer, O LORD, and give ear unto my cry; hold not thy peace at my tears: for I am a stranger with thee, and a sojourner, as all my fathers were.*

*13 O spare me, that I may recover strength, before I go hence, and be no more.*

<!-- post 17862 · 2026-09-23 05:22 · study -->

**Psalm 39** is sorrowful. It marks the hardship of being in the flesh, in a fallen world and the vanity of life, as a spiritual child of God. 

Man's days are like a vapor. The difficulty of being vexed by the filthy conversation of the wicked, and by the weakness of our mortal bodies can cause us to question the Lord, *what wait I for?* Paul felt the same tug towards death as David, *For to me to live is Christ, and to die is gain.*

**Philippians 1:21–24**
*For to me to live is Christ, and to die is gain. But if I live in the flesh, this is the fruit of my labour: yet what I shall choose I wot not. For I am in a strait betwixt two, having a desire to depart, and to be with Christ; which is far better: Nevertheless to abide in the flesh is more needful for you.*

For those of us whose hope is in the Lord to be delivered, these sentiments can become powerful. Especially in our repeated sin, our lack of control at times. Feeling the stripes of God's chastening. The effects of time. The vanity of it all. 

Solomon, David's son certainly felt this. 

**Ecclesiastes 1:14**
*I have seen all the works that are done under the sun; and, behold, all is vanity and vexation of spirit.*

Here in **Psalm 39**, David begins with confession, and reveals the frustration he has with himself.

<!-- post 17863 · 2026-09-23 05:51 · study -->

David writes that he made it a point of emphasis, that he would not sin with his mouth, to take heed of what he said. Keeping his tongue as with a bridle on a mule or a horse. 

James, the brother of Christ, writes of the importance of this in his epistle.

**James 3:2–10**
*For in many things we offend all. If any man offend not in word, the same is a perfect man, and able also to bridle the whole body. Behold, we put bits in the horses’ mouths, that they may obey us; and we turn about their whole body. Behold also the ships, which though they be so great, and are driven of fierce winds, yet are they turned about with a very small helm, whithersoever the governor listeth. Even so the tongue is a little member, and boasteth great things. Behold, how great a matter a little fire kindleth! And the tongue is a fire, a world of iniquity: so is the tongue among our members, that it defileth the whole body, and setteth on fire the course of nature; and it is set on fire of hell. For every kind of beasts, and of birds, and of serpents, and of things in the sea, is tamed, and hath been tamed of mankind: But the tongue can no man tame; it is an unruly evil, full of deadly poison. Therewith bless we God, even the Father; and therewith curse we men, which are made after the similitude of God. Out of the same mouth proceedeth blessing and cursing. My brethren, these things ought not so to be.*

And it ought not be. But as David goes on to say, it can be difficult. He set out to do what was right, keeping silent even when it may have done good to speak, careful in his speech to not say the wrong thing, and in his silence, his sorrow was stirred.

Mature Christians, especially parents with adult children, or pastors with a flock, should recognize this feeling. When we see others doing evil, or worse, people we love being led away towards it, and we know if we speak out, correct them, especially in anger, that we'd do more harm than good. 

The situation where we aren't angry with the person, but we know what the Bible says. Where we gently suggest and correct, but withhold our own opinion because we know it will lead us to sin, and give the others cause to justify their own evil. Where we will do more harm than good. 

*My heart was hot within me, while I was musing the fire burned*.

But David's first instinct is to open his mouth to the Lord, and in his sorrow and burning heart, he asks *make me to know mine end, and the measure of my days, what it is: that I may know how frail I am*. Asking, how much longer to I have to suffer this?

Answering his own question, he notes the short lifespan of man, the handbreadth of years, especially compared to the eternal God. *Verily every man at his best state is altogether vanity.*

In the end, we know our life here is brief, and the pain we endure is nothing, especially compared to the glory to come and the eternity with the Lord. 

**Romans 8:18**
*For I reckon that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed in us.*

This perspective, the finite life vs everlasting, can be a comfort. But it also can be a sobering reminder. That when taken together, men's lives are vain. People experience needless worry, anxiety, and inner turmoil over fleeting things like the accumulations of wealth. 

Life is brutal and short. Our own tongue betrays us. Those we see around us constantly striving for temporary gain, often at the expense of their spiritual good, only to die, taking nothing of it with them into eternity. 

*And now, Lord, what wait I for? my hope is in thee.*

For those of us with Heaven as our home, who knows that being absent from the body is to be present with the Lord, the futility of this world can be a sorrowful burden at times. 

And the knowledge that the things that vex us, that the silence we take up is not only to keep us from sin, but recognizing that our complaints may well stem from how much we deserve what we get.

<!-- post 17864 · 2026-09-23 05:51 · study -->

The strokes upon our back can be more than we can take. As children of God, being scourged, we can be consumed by His blows. 

We grow old and weary from His correction. The wages of our sin is taxing, of the flesh and the heart. 

Better to be silent, and contrite. But still, how long o Lord?  

*Hear my prayer, O LORD, and give ear unto my cry; hold not thy peace at my tears: for I am a stranger with thee, and a sojourner, as all my fathers were. O spare me, that I may recover strength, before I go hence, and be no more.*

David's Psalms frequently call out in Godly sorrow, seeking restoration. 

**Psalms 51:12**  
*Restore unto me the joy of thy salvation; and uphold me with thy free spirit.*

We may frequently ask God to bring us nearer to Thee. To *spare me that I may recover strength,* to better use the time we have left, not in vain pursuits, but in service to our Savior.

These are the prayers we can relate to, in our broken hearted times. Comfort we can count on during tribulations. Given for our example, and praise God for understanding our infirmities, but giving us hope. Amen.

## Psalms 40  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-09-24

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17867

<!-- post 17867 · 2026-09-24 05:36 · scripture -->

**Psalms 40**
*I waited patiently for the LORD; and he inclined unto me, and heard my cry.*

*2 He brought me up also out of an horrible pit, out of the miry clay, and set my feet upon a rock, and established my goings.*

*3 And he hath put a new song in my mouth, even praise unto our God: many shall see it, and fear, and shall trust in the LORD.*

*4 Blessed is that man that maketh the LORD his trust, and respecteth not the proud, nor such as turn aside to lies.*

*5 Many, O LORD my God, are thy wonderful works which thou hast done, and thy thoughts which are to us-ward: they cannot be reckoned up in order unto thee: if I would declare and speak of them, they are more than can be numbered.*

*6 Sacrifice and offering thou didst not desire; mine ears hast thou opened: burnt offering and sin offering hast thou not required.*

*7 Then said I, Lo, I come: in the volume of the book it is written of me,*

*8 I delight to do thy will, O my God: yea, thy law is within my heart.*

*9 I have preached righteousness in the great congregation: lo, I have not refrained my lips, O LORD, thou knowest.*

*10 I have not hid thy righteousness within my heart; I have declared thy faithfulness and thy salvation: I have not concealed thy lovingkindness and thy truth from the great congregation.*

*11 Withhold not thou thy tender mercies from me, O LORD: let thy lovingkindness and thy truth continually preserve me.*

*12 For innumerable evils have compassed me about: mine iniquities have taken hold upon me, so that I am not able to look up; they are more than the hairs of mine head: therefore my heart faileth me.*

*13 Be pleased, O LORD, to deliver me: O LORD, make haste to help me.*

*14 Let them be ashamed and confounded together that seek after my soul to destroy it; let them be driven backward and put to shame that wish me evil.*

*15 Let them be desolate for a reward of their shame that say unto me, Aha, aha.*

*16 Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee: let such as love thy salvation say continually, The LORD be magnified.*

*17 But I am poor and needy; yet the Lord thinketh upon me: thou art my help and my deliverer; make no tarrying, O my God.*

<!-- post 17868 · 2026-09-24 05:59 · study -->

After the somber and broken-hearted appeal of **Psalm 39**, **Psalm 40** gives us the rejoicing of those that trust in the Lord, and declares His power to all.

How wonderful is our patient waiting on the Lord, having called out His name for deliverance, to be rewarded by seeing Him turn towards us to save us in love! 

He frees us from the miry pit of sin, He delivers us out of tribulation and temptations. He brings us from the shifting sands of doubt and fear, and sets us upon a rock. Our rock, Christ Jesus. The solid ground where He establishes our goings, and directs our paths. Praise Jesus for His great salvation!

We who are saved have a new song in our heart, the gospel which we preach, the good news of eternal life and forgiveness in Christ, *even praise unto our God: many shall see it, and fear, and shall trust in the LORD*.

*Blessed is that man that maketh the LORD his trust, and respecteth not the proud, nor such as turn aside to lies.* 

Is there any better news than this? Repenting from lies to trust in the Lord, and the blessings that we are given by His grace in exchange. Eternal life, His perfect word, His judgments. The Spirit of truth. The rejection of the proud. The deliverance from the evil of others. Made sons of God and joint-heirs with Jesus. Many are the wonderful works that our Lord God has done! 

The tone of worship and blessings towards God in this prayer song of gratitude is as infectious as it is doctrinally rich. 

The thoughts of the Lord towards us, that He even considered a wretch like me, the wonderful things He has for us cannot be numbered. There's not enough time or words in this life for us to declare all He has done for us.

All we can do is cry out, "Thank you!" And share this good news with others. 

*Sacrifice and offering thou didst not desire; mine ears hast thou opened: burnt offering and sin offering hast thou not required.* 

The blood of bulls and goats never took away sin. It may have cleansed the flesh to be in the presence of God in the temple made with hands, but to enter into His presence forever, in the Temple of Heaven, all He has ever required was faith in His promises. 

**Romans 10:17**
*So then faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the word of God*.

Have they not heard? The word of God has gone out to the world and not returned void. But those that harden their hearts, and stiffen their necks and refuse to obey the gospel of God. 

As Jesus and the prophets taught:

**Matthew 13:13–16**
*Therefore speak I to them in parables: because they seeing see not; and hearing they hear not, neither do they understand. And in them is fulfilled the prophecy of Esaias, which saith, By hearing ye shall hear, and shall not understand; and seeing ye shall see, and shall not perceive: For this people’s heart is waxed gross, and their ears are dull of hearing, and their eyes they have closed; lest at any time they should see with their eyes and hear with their ears, and should understand with their heart, and should be converted, and I should heal them. But blessed are your eyes, for they see: and your ears, for they hear.*

God has blessed us with the conviction of the Spirit, that even in the miry pit, we heard Him, and see with our eyes the mighty God, being converted and healed. Calling upon the name of the Lord. 

**Romans 10:13**
*For whosoever shall call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.*

<!-- post 17870 · 2026-09-25 05:23 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalm 40**, rejoicing that God has given men ears to hear the truth, and the glory to God for His protection and comfort, we are called to spread this word, proclaiming it to all men. 

We are delighted to do His will. His law written in our hearts. 

**Romans 7:22**
*For I delight in the law of God after the inward man*

Even as our flesh may be prone to wonder, the outward man waging war against the spirit, we can know His righteous paths, and preach them among the beloved believers. Declaring His faithfulness and the salvation of the Lord to all who will hear. Singing His praises, letting His light shine through us, magnifying His glory. Proclaiming His great Gospel.

<!-- post 17871 · 2026-09-25 05:33 · study -->

Having acknowledged all He has done for us, and encouraging ourselves and others to turn to Him for salvation through the blessing of the Lord, David's Psalm turns introspective. He confesses the sin that has beset him, and compasses him about with the repercussions of it. 

*Withhold not thou thy tender mercies from me, O LORD: let thy lovingkindness and thy truth continually preserve me. For innumerable evils have compassed me about: mine iniquities have taken hold upon me, so that I am not able to look up; they are more than the hairs of mine head: therefore my heart faileth me.* 

We cannot acknowledge salvation without acknowledging what God is saving us from. 

Just as David and Paul write of having the law written in their heart, and delighting in His word, they both lament the sinful flesh that requires a Savior. 

**Romans 7:24–25**
*O wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me from the body of this death? I thank God through Jesus Christ our Lord. So then with the mind I myself serve the law of God; but with the flesh the law of sin.*

We ask they the Lord deliver us. That it is His will that be done. That those who are moved to scorn, mocking our trust in the Lord be made ashamed. 

That God use us to shame those that wish to do us evil, to His glory. 

Praise, thanksgiving, confession, deliverance, and destruction of those who hold the truth in unrighteousness. This is a model of prayer. That His will be done on earth as it is in Heaven. 

And in the end, the joy of His salvation overwhelming us with gratitude and praise, that such a holy and Almighty God takes heed of our prayers and notices us. Loving us so much, while we are yet sinners. Thinking of us. Providing for us. Delivering us. 

*Let all those that seek thee rejoice and be glad in thee: let such as love thy salvation say continually, The LORD be magnified. But I am poor and needy; yet the Lord thinketh upon me: thou art my help and my deliverer; make no tarrying, O my God.* Amen

<!-- post 13883 · 2024-04-11 05:48 · study -->

**Hebrews 10**
*For the law having a shadow of good things to come, and not the very image of the things, can never with those sacrifices which they offered year by year continually make the comers thereunto perfect.*

*2 For then would they not have ceased to be offered? because that the worshippers once purged should have had no more conscience of sins.*

*3 But in those sacrifices there is a remembrance again made of sins every year.*

*4 For it is not possible that the blood of bulls and of goats should take away sins.*

*5 Wherefore when he cometh into the world, he saith, Sacrifice and offering thou wouldest not, but a body hast thou prepared me:*

*6 In burnt offerings and sacrifices for sin thou hast had no pleasure.*

*7 Then said I, Lo, I come (in the volume of the book it is written of me,) to do thy will, O God.*

*8 Above when he said, Sacrifice and offering and burnt offerings and offering for sin thou wouldest not, neither hadst pleasure therein; which are offered by the law;*

*9 Then said he, Lo, I come to do thy will, O God. He taketh away the first, that he may establish the second.*

*10 By the which will we are sanctified through the offering of the body of Jesus Christ once for all.*

*11 And every priest standeth daily ministering and offering oftentimes the same sacrifices, which can never take away sins:*

*12 But this man, after he had offered one sacrifice for sins for ever, sat down on the right hand of God;*

*13 From henceforth expecting till his enemies be made his footstool.*

*14 For by one offering he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified.*

*15 Whereof the Holy Ghost also is a witness to us: for after that he had said before,*

*16 This is the covenant that I will make with them after those days, saith the Lord, I will put my laws into their hearts, and in their minds will I write them;*

*17 And their sins and iniquities will I remember no more.*

*18 Now where remission of these is, there is no more offering for sin.*

*19 Having therefore, brethren, boldness to enter into the holiest by the blood of Jesus,*

*20 By a new and living way, which he hath consecrated for us, through the veil, that is to say, his flesh;*

*21 And having an high priest over the house of God;*

*22 Let us draw near with a true heart in full assurance of faith, having our hearts sprinkled from an evil conscience, and our bodies washed with pure water.*

*23 Let us hold fast the profession of our faith without wavering; (for he is faithful that promised;)*

*24 And let us consider one another to provoke unto love and to good works:*

*25 Not forsaking the assembling of ourselves together, as the manner of some is; but exhorting one another: and so much the more, as ye see the day approaching.*

*26 For if we sin wilfully after that we have received the knowledge of the truth, there remaineth no more sacrifice for sins,*

*27 But a certain fearful looking for of judgment and fiery indignation, which shall devour the adversaries.*

*28 He that despised Moses' law died without mercy under two or three witnesses:*

*29 Of how much sorer punishment, suppose ye, shall he be thought worthy, who hath trodden under foot the Son of God, and hath counted the blood of the covenant, wherewith he was sanctified, an unholy thing, and hath done despite unto the Spirit of grace?*

*30 For we know him that hath said, Vengeance belongeth unto me, I will recompense, saith the Lord. And again, The Lord shall judge his people.*

*31 It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God.*

*32 But call to remembrance the former days, in which, after ye were illuminated, ye endured a great fight of afflictions;*

*33 Partly, whilst ye were made a gazingstock both by reproaches and afflictions; and partly, whilst ye became companions of them that were so used.*

*34 For ye had compassion of me in my bonds, and took joyfully the spoiling of your goods, knowing in yourselves that ye have in heaven a better and an enduring substance.*

*35 Cast not away therefore your confidence, which hath great recompence of reward.*

*36 For ye have need of patience, that, after ye have done the will of God, ye might receive the promise.*

*37 For yet a little while, and he that shall come will come, and will not tarry.*

*38 Now the just shall live by faith: but if any man draw back, my soul shall have no pleasure in him.*

*39 But we are not of them who draw back unto perdition; but of them that believe to the saving of the soul.*
