The spirit of Esau, who forsook his inheritance for a bowl of pottage, lives in Christians, who for political expediency seek to give it away. 

**Hebrews 12:14–17**
*Follow peace with all men, and holiness, without which no man shall see the Lord: Looking diligently lest any man fail of the grace of God; lest any root of bitterness springing up trouble you, and thereby many be defiled; Lest there be any fornicator, or profane person, as Esau, who for one morsel of meat sold his birthright. For ye know how that afterward, when he would have inherited the blessing, he was rejected: for he found no place of repentance, though he sought it carefully with tears.*

This tearful repentance will be the case for many in the end times. Those who placed modern politics and bad theology above the joy unspeakable found in Jesus for all who believe, Jew or Gentile. Man or woman. Bond or free.

<!-- post 17976 · 2026-10-15 20:16 · study -->

This first half of this **Psalm 48** study is really a work of love, and allowed me to lay out a positive, biblical understanding of Zion and Zionism, that's not simply countering a Dispensationalist's claims.

<!-- post 17977 · 2026-10-17 07:46 · study -->

*God is known in her palaces for a refuge. For, lo, the kings were assembled, they passed by together. They saw it, and so they marvelled; they were troubled, and hasted away. Fear took hold upon them there, and pain, as of a woman in travail.*

This promise of God, to be a ready shelter for His people and to cause their enemies to marvel and fear His power, and flee, is demonstrated multiple times in scripture. 

During the entry and conquest of the promised land, we see it happen to the 5 Kings and their armies. 

**Joshua 10:5**
*Therefore the five kings of the Amorites, the king of Jerusalem, the king of Hebron, the king of Jarmuth, the king of Lachish, the king of Eglon, gathered themselves together, and went up, they and all their hosts, and encamped before Gibeon, and made war against it.*

God promises Joshua that He will win them the victory.

**Joshua 10:8**  
*And the LORD said unto Joshua, Fear them not: for I have delivered them into thine hand; there shall not a man of them stand before thee.*

And God kept His promise. 

**Joshua 10:10**
*And the LORD discomfited them before Israel, and slew them with a great slaughter at Gibeon, and chased them along the way that goeth up to Bethhoron, and smote them* to *Azekah, and unto Makkedah.*

Ironically, despite the defeat of the King of Jerusalem, among all the others, Joshua was not able to conquer Jerusalem itself. This honor would fall on King David himself. 

**2 Samuel 5:6–7**
*And the king and his men went to Jerusalem unto the Jebusites, the inhabitants of the land: which spake unto David, saying, Except thou take away the blind and the lame, thou shalt not come in hither: thinking, David cannot come in hither. Nevertheless David took the strong hold of Zion: the same is the city of David.*

But this was not the only example of God founding the enemies of the people, especially in the defense of Zion.

<!-- post 17978 · 2026-10-17 08:16 · study -->

We've previously covered the attempted siege of Jerusalem by Sennacherib and the Assyrian Forces, from **2 Kings 19**, which is another example. But a more fitting instance occured when Jehosaphat was king.

A  massive alliance of kings from Moab, Ammon, and Edom marched together to destroy Jerusalem. 

**2 Chronicles 20:3–7**
*And Jehoshaphat feared, and set himself to seek the LORD, and proclaimed a fast throughout all Judah. And Judah gathered themselves together, to ask help of the LORD: even out of all the cities of Judah they came to seek the LORD. And Jehoshaphat stood in the congregation of Judah and Jerusalem, in the house of the LORD, before the new court, And said, O LORD God of our fathers, art not thou God in heaven? and rulest not thou over all the kingdoms of the heathen? and in thine hand is there not power and might, so that none is able to withstand thee? Art not thou our God, who didst drive out the inhabitants of this land before thy people Israel, and gavest it to the seed of Abraham thy friend for ever?*

As all Jerusalem prays, the Spirit of the Lord comes upon Jahaziel the son of Zechariah;

**2 Chronicles 20:15–17**
*And he said, Hearken ye, all Judah, and ye inhabitants of Jerusalem, and thou king Jehoshaphat, Thus saith the LORD unto you, Be not afraid nor dismayed by reason of this great multitude; for the battle is not yours, but God’s. To morrow go ye down against them: behold, they come up by the cliff of Ziz; and ye shall find them at the end of the brook, before the wilderness of Jeruel. Ye shall not need to fight in this battle: set yourselves, stand ye still, and see the salvation of the LORD with you, O Judah and Jerusalem: fear not, nor be dismayed; to morrow go out against them: for the LORD will be with you.*

The response by Jehosaphat and the Levites, and all the people, was to trust in the Lord and sing His praises. 

They thought of God's lovingkindness, they taught His word in the midst of the temple. They sang His praise to the ends of the earth. The mount of Zion rejoiced and Jerusalem's daughters were glad because of His judgements. 

**2 Chronicles 20:18–22**
*And Jehoshaphat bowed his head with his face to the ground: and all Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem fell before the LORD, worshipping the LORD. And the Levites, of the children of the Kohathites, and of the children of the Korhites, stood up to praise the LORD God of Israel with a loud voice on high. And they rose early in the morning, and went forth into the wilderness of Tekoa: and as they went forth, Jehoshaphat stood and said, Hear me, O Judah, and ye inhabitants of Jerusalem; Believe in the LORD your God, so shall ye be established; believe his prophets, so shall ye prosper. And when he had consulted with the people, he appointed singers unto the LORD, and that should praise the beauty of holiness, as they went out before the army, and to say, Praise the LORD; for his mercy endureth for ever. And when they began to sing and to praise, the LORD set ambushments against the children of Ammon, Moab, and mount Seir, which were come against Judah; and they were smitten.*

The battle won by the enemy being discomforted and confounded. They turned their own swords upon each other. Smitten by the power of the Lord. 

As promised, the spoil of the armies was delivered into the hands of Judah. And the people there praised His name.

*For this God is our God for ever and ever: he will be our guide even unto death.* Amen

## Psalms 49  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-10-19

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17984

<!-- post 17984 · 2026-10-19 05:06 · scripture -->

**Psalm 49**
*Hear this, all ye people; give ear, all ye inhabitants of the world:*

*2 Both low and high, rich and poor, together.*

*3 My mouth shall speak of wisdom; and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding.*

*4 I will incline mine ear to a parable: I will open my dark saying upon the harp.*

*5 Wherefore should I fear in the days of evil, when the iniquity of my heels shall compass me about?*

*6 They that trust in their wealth, and boast themselves in the multitude of their riches;*

*7 None of them can by any means redeem his brother, nor give to God a ransom for him:*

*8 (For the redemption of their soul is precious, and it ceaseth for ever:)*

*9 That he should still live for ever, and not see corruption.*

*10 For he seeth that wise men die, likewise the fool and the brutish person perish, and leave their wealth to others.*

*11 Their inward thought is, that their houses shall continue for ever, and their dwelling places to all generations; they call their lands after their own names.*

*12 Nevertheless man being in honour abideth not: he is like the beasts that perish.*

*13 This their way is their folly: yet their posterity approve their sayings. Selah.*

*14 Like sheep they are laid in the grave; death shall feed on them; and the upright shall have dominion over them in the morning; and their beauty shall consume in the grave from their dwelling.*

*15 But God will redeem my soul from the power of the grave: for he shall receive me. Selah.*

*16 Be not thou afraid when one is made rich, when the glory of his house is increased;*

*17 For when he dieth he shall carry nothing away: his glory shall not descend after him.*

*18 Though while he lived he blessed his soul: and men will praise thee, when thou doest well to thyself.*

*19 He shall go to the generation of his fathers; they shall never see light.*

*20 Man that is in honour, and understandeth not, is like the beasts that perish.*

<!-- post 17985 · 2026-10-19 05:47 · study -->

Psalm 49 is a meditation on the futility of life, in the building of of riches, honor and name, when death awaits everyone, rich or poor, wise or fool.

This reflection on life's vanities ties to Solomon's writings in **Ecclesiastes**, from the call to harken to the preacher to the vanity of life and the only remedy, fearing the Lord and receiving His blessing. 

**Ecclesiastes 2:16**
*For there is no remembrance of the wise more than of the fool for ever; seeing that which now is in the days to come shall all be forgotten. And how dieth the wise man? as the fool.*

This echoes the same sentiment found in verse 10;

*For he seeth that wise men die, likewise the fool and the brutish person perish, and leave their wealth to others.*

**Ecclesiastes 5:15**
*As he came forth of his mother's womb, naked shall he return to go as he came, and shall take nothing of his labour, which he may carry away in his hand.*

Solomon learned at the feet of his father, and after careful study of all the things his wealth and power availed him of, with the wisdom above other men, granted by God's grace, he would come to the same conclusions David sang of. 

The call to incline your ear to hear wisdom, to listen to the dark sayings and parables. To seek understanding is a major theme of Solomon's **Proverbs**;

**Proverbs 4:7**
*Wisdom is the principal thing; therefore get wisdom: and with all thy getting get understanding.*

David teaches us the same lessons in Psalms. The wisdom of God is most valuable, because in that knowledge we may find eternal life. 

David expresses confidence in his salvation. He is not afraid when the time comes to be given the wages of his sin, the iniquity of his heels, the death that awaits us all, the consequences of our own making. 

Why should we fear in the days of evil, that comes our own making? He knows his redemption is a gift of God. But the man trusting his riches has no hope at all in that day. They cannot redeem themselves or their brothers, and cannot pay the ransom due God for their sins. 

Again, this idea that man's riches are worthless in the judgment, that they do nothing for the dead, is repeated by Solomon;

**Proverbs 11:4** 
*Riches profit not in the day of wrath: but righteousness delivereth from death.*

**Proverbs 27:24**
*For riches are not for ever: and doth the crown endure to every generation?*

Their way is folly. The wise man dies like the fool, like the brute, and all their wealth is left for others. But the precious redemption of the soul, that one lives forever and shall not see corruption, that is of God, through faith in the Lord Christ Jesus. 

**1 Timothy 2:5–6**
*For there is one God, and one mediator between God and men, the man Christ Jesus; Who gave himself a ransom for all, to be testified in due time.*

David laments the fact that worldly prosperity deceives the wealthy, their own riches fooling them into a false sense of security. This is the larger context of the preaching of Christ;

**Matthew 19:23–26**
*Then said Jesus unto his disciples, Verily I say unto you, That a rich man shall hardly enter into the kingdom of heaven. And again I say unto you, It is easier for a camel to go through the eye of a needle, than for a rich man to enter into the kingdom of God. When his disciples heard it, they were exceedingly amazed, saying, Who then can be saved? But Jesus beheld them, and said unto them, With men this is impossible; but with God all things are possible.*

The only hope of man, rich or poor, is God. Only in Him is salvation possible.

**Proverbs 15:16**
*Better is little with the fear of the Lord than great treasure and trouble therewith.*

The blessings of this life are not a hope for the next. The praise of men is vanity before the Throne of an almighty and holy God. 

Agur's  prayer in **Proverbs** directly addresses this wisdom;

**Proverbs 30:8-9**
*Remove far from me vanity and lies: give me neither poverty nor riches; feed me with food convenient for me: Lest I be full, and deny thee, and say, Who is the Lord? or lest I be poor, and steal, and take the name of my God in vain.*

<!-- post 17986 · 2026-10-19 05:47 · study -->

Better to be satisfied with what the Lord has given us, and trust and fear Him, than to allow wealth to take us from fearing the Lord, or poverty that brings bitterness to our soul. 

**Psalm 49** perfectly encapsulates these wise sayings, showing that David's hope is not in riches, but in the salvation of the Lord.

*But God will redeem my soul from the power of the grave: for he shall receive me. Selah.*
 
So, no matter the station you have been born to, or the prosperity you've been blessed with, fear God and follow His ways, seeking His wisdom and be blessed by His redemption. Learn from his dark sayings and parables, before it is too late.

*Man that is in honour, and understandeth not, is like the beasts that perish*.

<!-- post 17987 · 2026-10-19 06:15 · study -->

The call by David to hear and understand his "dark sayings and parables," brings to mind the way Jesus taught in His ministry. 

**Matthew 13:34–35**
*All these things spake Jesus unto the multitude in parables; and without a parable spake he not unto them: That it might be fulfilled which was spoken by the prophet, saying, I will open my mouth in parables; I will utter things which have been kept secret from the foundation of the world*.

Jesus revealed to His disciples that parables act as a spiritual filter. To the humble seeker of wisdom, they provide deeper understanding; to the spiritually blind, they remain obscure "dark sayings."

**Matthew 13:10–13**
*And the disciples came, and said unto him, Why speakest thou unto them in parables? He answered and said unto them, Because it is given unto you to know the mysteries of the kingdom of heaven, but to them it is not given. For whosoever hath, to him shall be given, and he shall have more abundance: but whosoever hath not, from him shall be taken away even that he hath. Therefore speak I to them in parables: because they seeing see not; and hearing they hear not, neither do they understand*.

The unfolding revelation to man throughout scripture was never meant to keep wisdom hidden. Especially, not forever. 

Unlike the religions of man, with their esoteric knowledge, vague and shallow sayings fed in dribs and drabs, with no clarity ever given, God's word opens itself up, and Jesus ultimately shows what all these "dark sayings" and prophetic parables actually mean. 

 **John 16:25**
*These things have I spoken unto you in proverbs: but the time cometh, when I shall no more speak unto you in proverbs, but I shall shew you plainly of the Father.*

During His earthly ministry, even Jesus' most faithful disciples required His explanation of the parables He spoke in, to the crowds and Pharisees. 

**Mark 4:33–34**
*And with many such parables spake he the word unto them, as they were able to hear it. But without a parable spake he not unto them: and when they were alone, he expounded all things to his disciples.*

But the full revelation of scripture came in the days following His resurrection, when all things were finally made known. 

To the men He met on the way, in **Luke 24**, Jesus, *beginning at Moses and all the prophets, he expounded unto them in all the scriptures the things concerning himself*.

And to the gathering of the disciples, He did the same;

**Luke 24:44–45**
*And he said unto them, These are the words which I spake unto you, while I was yet with you, that all things must be fulfilled, which were written in the law of Moses, and in the prophets, and in the psalms, concerning me. Then opened he their understanding, that they might understand the scriptures*.

He explicitly references the three divisions of the Old Testament, (the Law, the Prophets, and the Psalms, like Psalm 49) and grants them the spiritually supernatural ability to finally see past the "dark sayings" to understand the plain truth of the Gospel. 

These things are revealed to us by the Spirit, Who teaches us all things. We are able to see the entire picture, fully revealed in Christ, and the understanding imparted by the Apostles in the Epistles for added clarity and depth. Only the future Revelation of Christ Jesus is still through a glass darkly, (although the gist is pretty clear on timing and events).

We see that the Old Testament scriptures aren't an empty recitation of genealogy, geography and events. They aren't the vain contemplations of man. They are the words of eternal life, and all these were written to point the way to Jesus, and provide us with understanding.

**1 Corinthians 10:11**
*Now all these things happened unto them for ensamples: and they are written for our admonition, upon whom the ends of the world are come.*

Not only does God's word exist to show us His ways, but ultimately it is the tool He has given us to fulfill the Great Commission, leading others to the truth.

<!-- post 17988 · 2026-10-19 06:15 · study -->

**Luke 24:46–48**
*And said unto them, Thus it is written, and thus it behoved Christ to suffer, and to rise from the dead the third day: And that repentance and remission of sins should be preached in his name among all nations, beginning at Jerusalem. And ye are witnesses of these things.*

We are witnesses of these things. We have the testimony of God, revealed. We are given the wisdom and power of His word to teach repentance towards God and remission of sins by His Son, in Jesus' name. 

Not in dark sayings and parables, although they help reveal the truth, but by the power of our witness that all is revealed in Christ. Amen.

## Psalms 50  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-10-20

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17989

<!-- post 17989 · 2026-10-20 05:00 · scripture -->

**Psalm 50**
*The mighty God, even the LORD, hath spoken, and called the earth from the rising of the sun unto the going down thereof.*

*2 Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty, God hath shined.*

*3 Our God shall come, and shall not keep silence: a fire shall devour before him, and it shall be very tempestuous round about him.*

*4 He shall call to the heavens from above, and to the earth, that he may judge his people.*

*5 Gather my saints together unto me; those that have made a covenant with me by sacrifice.*

*6 And the heavens shall declare his righteousness: for God is judge himself. Selah.*

*7 Hear, O my people, and I will speak; O Israel, and I will testify against thee: I am God, even thy God.*

*8 I will not reprove thee for thy sacrifices or thy burnt offerings, to have been continually before me.*

*9 I will take no bullock out of thy house, nor he goats out of thy folds.*

*10 For every beast of the forest is mine, and the cattle upon a thousand hills.*

*11 I know all the fowls of the mountains: and the wild beasts of the field are mine.*

*12 If I were hungry, I would not tell thee: for the world is mine, and the fulness thereof.*

*13 Will I eat the flesh of bulls, or drink the blood of goats?*

*14 Offer unto God thanksgiving; and pay thy vows unto the most High:*

*15 And call upon me in the day of trouble: I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me.*

*16 But unto the wicked God saith, What hast thou to do to declare my statutes, or that thou shouldest take my covenant in thy mouth?*

*17 Seeing thou hatest instruction, and casteth my words behind thee.*

*18 When thou sawest a thief, then thou consentedst with him, and hast been partaker with adulterers.*

*19 Thou givest thy mouth to evil, and thy tongue frameth deceit.*

*20 Thou sittest and speakest against thy brother; thou slanderest thine own mother’s son.*

*21 These things hast thou done, and I kept silence; thou thoughtest that I was altogether such an one as thyself: but I will reprove thee, and set them in order before thine eyes.*

*22 Now consider this, ye that forget God, lest I tear you in pieces, and there be none to deliver.*

*23 Whoso offereth praise glorifieth me: and to him that ordereth his conversation aright will I shew the salvation of God.*

<!-- post 17990 · 2026-10-20 05:57 · study -->

**Psalm 50**, is a song written directly from the voice of God to His people, His own perspective directly to man. It is instruction in doctrine and prophecy, by His word. In the first person. 

He spoke these things through a psalmist other than David. The superscript titles this as "A Psalm of Asaph," making it the very first of the 12 "Asaphite Psalms" in the Bible. 

Asaph was a prominent Levite, appointed by King David as the chief musician and worship leader before the Ark of the Covenant when it was brought to Jerusalem. More than just a song director, the Bible credits him as a prophet. A seer. 

**2 Chronicles 29:30**
*Moreover Hezekiah the king and the princes commanded the Levites to sing praise unto the LORD with the words of David, and of Asaph the seer. And they sang praises with gladness, and they bowed their heads and worshipped.*

While prophesying may refer to preaching the word of God, seer is the specific term for a visionary who is given glimpses of the future by God, prophetic understanding of things that are not but will be. 

He begins his song by introducing its Author. The mighty God. All scripture is given by inspiration of God, it is God breathed, and spoken. But this will be the word of God, as a message of judgment for His people. 

It establishes first the primacy of God, the Creator. By His Word the earth and the heavens were made.

**John 1:1–4**
*In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God. The same was in the beginning with God. All things were made by him; and without him was not any thing made that was made. In him was life; and the life was the light of men.*

It is important when we see credit for creation to God in the Bible, to remember that this includes Jesus.

**1 Corinthians 8:6**
*But to us there is but one God, the Father, of whom are all things, and we in him; and one Lord Jesus Christ, by whom are all things, and we by him.*

We have a New Testament understanding when we reflect on the Old Testament revelations. The nature of the Godhead is revealed in the creation. And it is relevant to this Psalm. 

Out of Zion, the place God chose to put His name, God hath shined. 

His presence in the Tabernacle and the eventual Temple, God with His people through their worship, is an echo to the true coming of the Lord in the flesh, and His return on His day to judge the nations, upon that holy mountain. 

*Our God shall come, and shall not keep silence: a fire shall devour before him, and it shall be very tempestuous round about him.*

The Second Coming, the return of Christ, is the day we all look forward to with gladness. He will come with power, a consuming fire proceeding from Him, and the sword of His word from His mouth shall slay the wicked. 

**2 Thessalonians 1:7–9**
*And to you who are troubled rest with us, when the Lord Jesus shall be revealed from heaven with his mighty angels, In flaming fire taking vengeance on them that know not God, and that obey not the gospel of our Lord Jesus Christ: Who shall be punished with everlasting destruction from the presence of the Lord, and from the glory of his power*

The future judgment of the world was a key part of the Israelites understanding, though through a glass darkly. However, having the full revelation of the complete Bible, we see clearly what they only glimpsed in faith. 

He comes as a judge, the Son calling up to Heaven, and down to Earth, with a shout, with trumpet, the call to judgment of His people, before His wrath is poured out on the world. 

*Gather my saints together unto me; those that have made a covenant with me by sacrifice. And the heavens shall declare his righteousness: for God is judge himself. Selah.*

The gathering of the elect, those sanctified by the Spirit in Christ, is not a "new doctrine," as some would claim. The Rapture, or the gathering of the elect being caught up together to meet the Lord in the air, was a key part of prophecy for the Lord's people.

<!-- post 17991 · 2026-10-20 05:57 · study -->

The covenant was made by sacrifice; the blood of the covenant made by Moses and the people with God was sealed with blood that represented the blood of Christ, and the promise of a better covenant with God by sacrifice, the sacrifice of the Son.

**Hebrews 9:19–23**
*For when Moses had spoken every precept to all the people according to the law, he took the blood of calves and of goats, with water, and scarlet wool, and hyssop, and sprinkled both the book, and all the people, Saying, This is the blood of the testament which God hath enjoined unto you. Moreover he sprinkled with blood both the tabernacle, and all the vessels of the ministry. And almost all things are by the law purged with blood; and without shedding of blood is no remission. It was therefore necessary that the patterns of things in the heavens should be purified with these; but the heavenly things themselves with better sacrifices than these.*

The heavens declare the righteousness of Christ Jesus, and His righteousness is upon all His people. God is the judge Himself. The Atonement, Himself. The mediator, Himself. 

The saints are secure in His righteousness. But God speaks to Israel in judgment. Beginning with His people. 

*Hear, O my people, and I will speak; O Israel, and I will testify against thee: I am God, even thy God.*

The future prophecy versus the current call. 

The continuous sacrifices made before God to give the physical nation of Israel the honor of being His people, keep His hand from reproving them harshly. They maintained the Levitical ordinances which He based promises on. The sacrifices that needed to be done constantly to maintain ritual cleanliness and keep His presence among them had been done. He isn't asking for more sacrifice. No additional bulls or goats to shed their blood. 

Again, we have a better sacrifice, once and for all, a covenant made by the blood of Jesus, the burnt offering for our sin. 

**Hebrews 9:11–14**
*But Christ being come an high priest of good things to come, by a greater and more perfect tabernacle, not made with hands, that is to say, not of this building; Neither by the blood of goats and calves, but by his own blood he entered in once into the holy place, having obtained eternal redemption for us. For if the blood of bulls and of goats, and the ashes of an heifer sprinkling the unclean, sanctifieth to the purifying of the flesh: How much more shall the blood of Christ, who through the eternal Spirit offered himself without spot to God, purge your conscience from dead works to serve the living God?*

But, in the old covenant, the blood of bulls and goats were used, continually, signifying the Sacrifice to come, and this the Levites did. 

God is telling His people at this time He isn't asking for more, and in fact it is not the blood of bulls and goats, and their burnt offering that He seeks. 

Every beast is His. The cattle upon a thousand hills. If He were hungry, and in need of the savour of the fat and the sustenance of meats, He has His pick and fill. He doesn't need them. Their sacrifices are a picture and a necessity for the covenant, but they do not please Him in and of themselves. 

**Hosea 6:6**
*For I desired mercy, and not sacrifice; and the knowledge of God more than burnt offerings.*

Sentiment repeated by Christ;

**Matthew 12:7**
*But if ye had known what this meaneth, I will have mercy, and not sacrifice, ye would not have condemned the guiltless.*

Here, in **Psalm 50**, God makes it clear that the offering is thanksgiving. They are vows to be paid to maintain their part in the covenant as His nation. Tithes and first fruits as acts of faith. 

It is not, as other gods, a meal for Him to consume, but a thanksgiving offered to show gratitude for all that He has supplied them, and to keep themselves unspotted, and be His people. 

God desires mercy towards others. Right judgment, defending the weak and hating the workers of iniquity.

<!-- post 17992 · 2026-10-20 05:57 · study -->

**Matthew 23:23**
*Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye pay tithe of mint and anise and cummin, and have omitted the weightier matters of the law, judgment, mercy, and faith: these ought ye to have done, and not to leave the other undone*.

These aren't the repetitive acts done by priests, but matters of the heart which God judges, and that He seeks from us. 

Having established His ways, God continues in v15 to give the Gospel message. The good news to all men in all ages; the dispensation of grace:

*And call upon me in the day of trouble: I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me.* 

Calling upon the name of the Lord for salvation, that we may glorify Him to all men. The promise of salvation and the heart of the great commission, coupled with the truth that we magnify His light, when we are in Him, that man may see our works of righteousness, mercy and thanksgiving, and glorify God. 

**Matthew 5:16**
*Let your light so shine before men, that they may see your good works, and glorify your Father which is in heaven.*

This is our purpose as believers. To give the judgment of God and an example of His mercy, praising His name in thanksgiving, all as an outlet to be seen by man, that He may be glorified.

<!-- post 17993 · 2026-10-20 06:51 · study -->

Now, having been summoned to His judgment, and having stipulated His position, God begins the indictment against His people, giving the matters of His suit, which He Himself will judge. 

God, in showing that He has no need for the blood of bulls and goats, fowls or anything else, begins by laying the charge of formalism against His covenant people. 

The religious practices are being maintained, the bare minimum of service to keep the covenant. The Lord isn't accusing them of failing this aspect, but in the weighter matters, as Jesus accused the Pharisees. 

God established promises and practices to maintain them as His people, but they are failing to declare His statutes, or to proclaim His law. 

The entire point of Israel was to be a light to all men. Not to selfishly reap the benefits while doing nothing to teach all nations as an example. 

**Deuteronomy 4:5–6**
*Behold, I have taught you statutes and judgments, even as the LORD my God commanded me, that ye should do so in the land whither ye go to possess it. Keep therefore and do them; for this is your wisdom and your understanding in the sight of the nations, which shall hear all these statutes, and say, Surely this great nation is a wise and understanding people.*

They had squandered their blessings by not fulfilling this role. 

**Matthew 15:8**
*This people draweth nigh unto me with their mouth, and honoureth me with their lips; but their heart is far from me.*

God is directly accusing the wicked among His people of missing the entire point, a charge Jesus will use to indict the religious leaders of the Jews. 

**Matthew 23:27–28**
*Woe unto you, scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites! for ye are like unto whited sepulchres, which indeed appear beautiful outward, but are within full of dead men’s bones, and of all uncleanness. Even so ye also outwardly appear righteous unto men, but within ye are full of hypocrisy and iniquity.*

As an application for Christians today, the indictment is the same leaven of the Pharisees; hypocrisy. 

**Hebrews 10:8**
*Above when he said, Sacrifice and offering and burnt offerings and offering for sin thou wouldest not, neither hadst pleasure therein; which are offered by the law*.

Just as the Pharisees had the OT sacrifices, we have the once and for all sacrifice of Christ, and total remission of sins. 

**Hebrews 10:17–18**
*And their sins and iniquities will I remember no more. Now where remission of these is, there is no more offering for sin.*

Just as He says to His people That he isn't seeking to add more sacrifice to those that they are already, God isn't asking us for additional acts of atonement. He remembers our sins no more. There is no more sacrifice to be had. 

**Hebrews 10:26–27**
*For if we sin wilfully after that we have received the knowledge of the truth, there remaineth no more sacrifice for sins, But a certain fearful looking for of judgment and fiery indignation, which shall devour the adversaries.*

Just like Israel in **Psalm 50**, we are covered by the blood. We are his people. But are we bound by the formalism and outward appearance of being clean, while failing to maintain the good work that God has saved us for? That gives Him glory?

Or are we looking over our shoulder, expecting a similar charge to be made by God against us as He is making here? 

If we're not declaring his righteousness and glory in Thanksgiving, and showing mercy and judgment to others, loving our neighbor, how are we taking his covenant in our mouths? How are we telling the world that we are Christians when we aren't acting like it? Who does it benefit?

**James 2:14–17**
*What doth it profit, my brethren, though a man say he hath faith, and have not works? can faith save him? If a brother or sister be naked, and destitute of daily food, And one of you say unto them, Depart in peace, be ye warmed and filled; notwithstanding ye give them not those things which are needful to the body; what doth it profit? Even so faith, if it hath not works, is dead, being alone.*

<!-- post 17994 · 2026-10-20 06:51 · study -->

This isn't legalism. This isn't God saying that to be his we have to do works to be saved, or to be His. We are not justified before God by our works, but before men. But God does have works for us to do. 

**Ephesians 2:10**
*For we are his workmanship, created in Christ Jesus unto good works, which God hath before ordained that we should walk in them.*

The indictment against His people is that while we have availed ourselves of his covenant and promises, we have squandered and wasted the talents of silver He is given us.

<!-- post 17995 · 2026-10-20 06:51 · study -->

**Matthew 25:14–30**
*For the kingdom of heaven is as a man travelling into a far country, who called his own servants, and delivered unto them his goods. And unto one he gave five talents, to another two, and to another one; to every man according to his several ability; and straightway took his journey. Then he that had received the five talents went and traded with the same, and made them other five talents. And likewise he that had received two, he also gained other two. But he that had received one went and digged in the earth, and hid his lord’s money. After a long time the lord of those servants cometh, and reckoneth with them. And so he that had received five talents came and brought other five talents, saying, Lord, thou deliveredst unto me five talents: behold, I have gained beside them five talents more. His lord said unto him, Well done, thou good and faithful servant: thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord. He also that had received two talents came and said, Lord, thou deliveredst unto me two talents: behold, I have gained two other talents beside them. His lord said unto him, Well done, good and faithful servant; thou hast been faithful over a few things, I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord. Then he which had received the one talent came and said, Lord, I knew thee that thou art an hard man, reaping where thou hast not sown, and gathering where thou hast not strawed: And I was afraid, and went and hid thy talent in the earth: lo, there thou hast that is thine. His lord answered and said unto him, Thou wicked and slothful servant, thou knewest that I reap where I sowed not, and gather where I have not strawed: Thou oughtest therefore to have put my money to the exchangers, and then at my coming I should have received mine own with usury. Take therefore the talent from him, and give it unto him which hath ten talents. For unto every one that hath shall be given, and he shall have abundance: but from him that hath not shall be taken away even that which he hath. And cast ye the unprofitable servant into outer darkness: there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth.*

<!-- post 17996 · 2026-10-20 06:54 · study -->

To whom much is given much is expected. There are professing Christians in this world who have never accepted the gift that God has extended to us. They've taken the offer and buried it and done nothing with it. They didn't take it in as their own and nurture it and use it to increase God's kingdom. 

Again this isn't a works-based salvation. If the unprofitable servant had taken his one talent of silver and kept it in his care, he'd still have been saved. But rather he buried it, and went through the motions. 

God goes out of his way in his indictment against Israel in **Psalm 50** to stipulate that His people who are righteous are protected under his care. His problem is with the false professors of His name who think that the formalism of their worship is sufficient to spare them from His wrath.

<!-- post 17997 · 2026-10-20 07:11 · study -->

God's accusations against them are sharp. They can tend with thieves and are partakers with adulterers. Both the literal engagement with the worldly, and the spiritual, of general ecumenicalism. They speak evil things, and lie. They not only don't love their neighbors, but they lie against their own brothers. 

And God, God who is longsuffering and merciful and wood that all men come to repentance, gave them space and time to reconsider their ways. Instead they considered themselves holy, like God is Holy. They thought that in their religious practices, and keeping the letter of the law that they could ignore the purpose of the law and its spirit. 

It's the same indictment here as in **Matthew 23**. And it could be said Of so many supposed Christians today. 

It's interesting to me that **Hebrews 10** bookends the idea of being no more need for sacrifice for those in Christ, and there being no more sacrifice for willful sin, around the command to attend church. 

**Hebrews 10:23–25**
*Let us hold fast the profession of our faith without wavering; (for he is faithful that promised;) And let us consider one another to provoke unto love and to good works: Not forsaking the assembling of ourselves together, as the manner of some is; but exhorting one another: and so much the more, as ye see the day approaching.*

We are commanded to not forsake the assembly. And the purpose of that is to love one another. Edifying each other. Building each other up. So much more as the day approaches. 

But today, there are those who see church as a check box for their righteousness. Proving that they are holy as God is holy simply by their attendance. And in so many acts that they do, they wash the outside of the vessel, while inside they are whited tombs filled with dead men's bones. 

Those people to whom religion is a series of rituals and obligations that only benefit themselves, who see sacraments as their sacrifice rather than the blood of Christ. Christians in name only who go through the motions and never once consider God. 

They forget God and he will tear them to pieces. They bury their talent out of fear, while never truly fearing God. 

But God, ever gracious ends on a positive note. 

*Whoso offereth praise glorifieth me: and to him that ordereth his conversation aright will I shew the salvation of God.*

Our purpose of being his people is to praise his name that he be glorified among men. We are the salt of the earth. We are what is preserving the world against judgment. And so he's encouraging us to continue in that praise and thankgiving and glorifying God among men. 

Because it is possible for salt to lose its savor. For lukewarm Christians to have the candle of their church put out. To be of no further use of God so he calls you home if you're saved and if you're not, casting you into outer darkness. 

**Matthew 5:13**
*Ye are the salt of the earth: but if the salt have lost his savour, wherewith shall it be salted? it is thenceforth good for nothing, but to be cast out, and to be trodden under foot of men.*

God's case against Israel is God's case against all who call themselves by His name and fail to live to proclaim His glory. 

Let us then stay salty, maintain our savor, do all things with Mercy and judgment forgiving those who trespass against us, considering the plight of the poor and the widow and the fatherless. Exhorting one another.

So that at the coming of Christ, when He brings firey judgment upon the world, We will be found acceptable. Where He will say that we are good and faithful servants. Amen

## Psalms 51  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-10-23

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/18010

<!-- post 18010 · 2026-10-23 04:54 · scripture -->

**Psalm 51** 
*Have mercy upon me, O God, according to thy lovingkindness: according unto the multitude of thy tender mercies blot out my transgressions.*

*2 Wash me throughly from mine iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin.*

*3 For I acknowledge my transgressions: and my sin is ever before me.*

*4 Against thee, thee only, have I sinned, and done this evil in thy sight: that thou mightest be justified when thou speakest, and be clear when thou judgest.*

*5 Behold, I was shapen in iniquity; and in sin did my mother conceive me.*

*6 Behold, thou desirest truth in the inward parts: and in the hidden part thou shalt make me to know wisdom.*

*7 Purge me with hyssop, and I shall be clean: wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.*

*8 Make me to hear joy and gladness; that the bones which thou hast broken may rejoice.*

*9 Hide thy face from my sins, and blot out all mine iniquities.*

*10 Create in me a clean heart, O God; and renew a right spirit within me.*

*11 Cast me not away from thy presence; and take not thy holy spirit from me.*

*12 Restore unto me the joy of thy salvation; and uphold me with thy free spirit.*

*13 Then will I teach transgressors thy ways; and sinners shall be converted unto thee.*

*14 Deliver me from bloodguiltiness, O God, thou God of my salvation: and my tongue shall sing aloud of thy righteousness.*

*15 O Lord, open thou my lips; and my mouth shall shew forth thy praise.*

*16 For thou desirest not sacrifice; else would I give it: thou delightest not in burnt offering.*

*17 The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit: a broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt not despise.*

*18 Do good in thy good pleasure unto Zion: build thou the walls of Jerusalem.*

*19 Then shalt thou be pleased with the sacrifices of righteousness, with burnt offering and whole burnt offering: then shall they offer bullocks upon thine altar.*

<!-- post 18011 · 2026-10-23 05:26 · study -->

**Psalm 51** is the clearest and most sober cry of contrition and supplication for forgiveness by a believer, in the face of a judgment by the Almighty God, that we have in Scripture. 

It is David at his lowest. "A Psalm of David, when Nathan the prophet came unto him, after he had gone in to Bath-sheba."

We know the story. David doesn't go off to war as kings were expected to. He stays at his place and sees Bathsheba, the wife of his soldier, Uriah the Hittite, bathing on her nearby roof. Filled with lust, he has her. And as a consequence of that adultery, David conspired to have Uriah killed in battle, and wed his pregnant widow. 

Nathan confronts the King, and the Lord curses David for his sin;

**2 Samuel 12:7–10**
*And Nathan said to David, Thou art the man. Thus saith the LORD God of Israel, I anointed thee king over Israel, and I delivered thee out of the hand of Saul; And I gave thee thy master’s house, and thy master’s wives into thy bosom, and gave thee the house of Israel and of Judah; and if that had been too little, I would moreover have given unto thee such and such things. Wherefore hast thou despised the commandment of the LORD, to do evil in his sight? thou hast killed Uriah the Hittite with the sword, and hast taken his wife to be thy wife, and hast slain him with the sword of the children of Ammon. Now therefore the sword shall never depart from thine house; because thou hast despised me, and hast taken the wife of Uriah the Hittite to be thy wife.*

The judgment of God was given, the child should die. 

**2 Samuel 12:14–15**
*Howbeit, because by this deed thou hast given great occasion to the enemies of the LORD to blaspheme, the child also that is born unto thee shall surely die. And Nathan departed unto his house. And the LORD struck the child that Uriah’s wife bare unto David, and it was very sick.*

David fasts and prays and stays by the child's side, but in the end the illness takes the boy. 

**2 Samuel 12:18–19**
*And it came to pass on the seventh day, that the child died. And the servants of David feared to tell him that the child was dead: for they said, Behold, while the child was yet alive, we spake unto him, and he would not hearken unto our voice: how will he then vex himself, if we tell him that the child is dead? But when David saw that his servants whispered, David perceived that the child was dead: therefore David said unto his servants, Is the child dead? And they said, He is dead.*

David, devastated, cleans himself and goes into the house of the Lord to worship. It is in this context that this Psalm was written.

Rather than a root of bitterness against the judgement of God, as many are tempted to take on in the face of such fierce judgement, David concedes and comes to the Lord, asking for mercy. As he has so often in **Psalms**, he begins in worship, praising God for His lovingkindness and mercy. Reminding himself, and the Lord of His great qualities, and appeals to the ultimate judge to blot out his transgressions. 

This is a formula of prayer that we should commit to our prayer life. Under better circumstances and joyous events, or in grave repentance and regret for whatever sins we have committed, the Lord should be approached in humility and meekness of spirit. Hallowing His holy name. 

The request is to forgive the confessed transgressions, knowing He is merciful and faithful to forgive His children. 

**1 John 1:9**
*If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness*.

This same promise applies to believers, today. The sure knowledge that our heavenly Father is faithful to forgive us, even the sins of the level of David. To wash us throughly, to cleanse us. To blot the iniquity from our record, and heal us, and our relationship with God. 

But note, it is if we confess our sins. David acknowledged his transgressions. We cannot lie to God and we should not lie to ourselves. We cannot hide from our sin when we've faced judgment, and our conscience keeps the sin ever before us in guilt.

<!-- post 18012 · 2026-10-23 05:26 · study -->

The joy and benefit of the forgiveness of God comes in relief from that burden, and removing the fear of chastening, that comes from a contrite heart seeking His mercy. 

*Against thee, thee only, have I sinned, and done this evil in thy sight...that thou mightest be justified when thou speakest, and be clear when thou judgest.*

David confesses that it is the transgression towards God that out him in judgment, and so he admits to God that not only has he sinned against Him, but that David acknowledges his understanding that God's judgments are right and deserved. 

Again, it is very common to see people in sin, when facing the judgment of God for it, to grow to resent Him and despise His judgements. To make excuses for their acts, to deny their intent, to place what has resulted from their wicked ways on the shoulders of a God whom they reject as judge. 

These people are destroyed with their bitterness, wracked with guilt, have a broken relationship with the God that bought them, and are often called home early, the result of their being incapable of being honest and appealing to His mercy. 

But David shows us the better way.

<!-- post 18013 · 2026-10-23 06:01 · study -->

David continues his confession; 

*Behold, I was shapen in iniquity; and in sin did my mother conceive me.*

He uses hyperbolic language to say that he is a sinner, born of a sinner, in his appeal for mercy. 

There is much made of this passage, and all manner of doctrines that have been spun out of it. The primary being "original sin" as the idea that babies are born guilty, and condemned. 

But the very account being mentioned show that David did not believe any such thing. 

**2 Samuel 12:21–23**
*Then said his servants unto him, What thing is this that thou hast done? thou didst fast and weep for the child, while it was alive; but when the child was dead, thou didst rise and eat bread. And he said, While the child was yet alive, I fasted and wept: for I said, Who can tell whether GOD will be gracious to me, that the child may live? But now he is dead, wherefore should I fast? can I bring him back again? I shall go to him, but he shall not return to me*.

David, who knew he was redeemed by God, saved by faith, under the grace of the Lord, states plainly that he will go to be with his dead child in the end. That Heaven is their home. 

While Psalms are dripping with doctrine, we should still always compare scripture with scripture, and gain understanding by the totality of the Bible, rather than out of context proof texts.

<!-- post 18021 · 2026-10-25 06:47 · study -->

Continuing in **Psalm 51**, having established the context and getting the contention on the false doctrine of original sin guilt that comes from a shallow reading of the text, we see the purpose of the Psalm is the honest confession of a righteous man. 

David is not placing the blame of what he has done on anyone else, and accepts full responsibility for whatever God judges against him. His fear of the Lord has led him to Godly repentance and reconciliation. 

*Behold, thou desirest truth in the inward parts: and in the hidden part thou shalt make me to know wisdom*.

This is the meekness and honesty about our sin that God seeks, that in it, we can actually learn from Him and grow. 

We discussed in the previous **Psalm 50** study that God doesn't seek the sacrifices and burnt offerings, but a meek and contrite heart. He is pleased as a Father when we come to Him for restitution and forgiveness. His mercy and grace are limitless, all He asks for is honesty in our heart. 

*For thou desirest not sacrifice; else would I give it: thou delightest not in burnt offering.*

David confesses, and calls upon the Lord to purge him with hyssop, the sponge like plant that was used to apply the blood on the posts during the first passover, the same that was present when the Lord was crucified and they gave Him gall to drink. 

The washing of forgiveness by the blood of Christ is ours, that we become white as snow. 

**Isaiah 1:18**
*Come now, and let us reason together, saith the LORD: though your sins be as scarlet, they shall be as white as snow; though they be red like crimson, they shall be as wool.*

David pleads to be made to hear joy and gladness, to be cheered by the power of God's word to heal the brokenness he feels from the Father's chastening. 

He asks God to hide His face from David's sins. To see them and remember them no more. To blot out his iniquity. 

We know as saved Christians that this promise of God, to forgive our sins past, present and future, that He will remember them no more and place them as far from us as east is from west, is our blessed hope in the redemption that is found in Christ. 

We take comfort in this truth when we are faithful to confess to Him;

**1 John 1:7–10**
*But if we walk in the light, as he is in the light, we have fellowship one with another, and the blood of Jesus Christ his Son cleanseth us from all sin. If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us. If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness. If we say that we have not sinned, we make him a liar, and his word is not in us.*

This promise shows not only the faithfulness of God to forgive, but also that our sin in the flesh is not held against our ledger in Heaven. It is not imputed against those that are in Christ. 

**1 John 3:9**
*Whosoever is born of God doth not commit sin; for his seed remaineth in him: and he cannot sin, because he is born of God.*

John is not contradicting himself by saying if we say we have no sin, we are liars and simultaneously saying we cannot sin, being born of God.

He is describing the same feelings from the failure of the flesh David writes about here. 

When we sin, it is not the new man, created in Christ, but the flesh. 

**Romans 7:15–17**
*For that which I do I allow not: for what I would, that do I not; but what I hate, that do I. If then I do that which I would not, I consent unto the law that it is good. Now then it is no more I that do it, but sin that dwelleth in me.*

Our flesh sins. If we deny that we are liars. But by being born again in Christ, we do not sin. The difference is that the flesh, which we can mortify to sin, and restrain from our lusts, is dead. Nailed to the cross with Christ. 

**Galatians 5:24–25**
*And they that are Christ’s have crucified the flesh with the affections and lusts. If we live in the Spirit, let us also walk in the Spirit.*

<!-- post 18022 · 2026-10-25 06:47 · study -->

Our spirit is quickened and covered by the imputed righteousness of Christ Jesus. It is His righteousness that covers our transgressions and the Spirit that seals us unto redemption.

So David, in his sorrow and remorse reminds God of this truth, and by doing so, reminds himself. 

*Cast me not away from thy presence; and take not thy holy Spirit from me. Restore unto me the joy of thy salvation and uphold me with thy free spirit.* 

David is not writing that he is afraid to lose his salvation, but seeking the confidence in it again that has been marred by his transgressions and the fear from the chastening he has received. 

He is seeking to renew his relationship with God through his confession and appeals to mercy. 

And the purpose? Is it to selfish ends of not wanting to feel guilty and to get back on the blessings side of God? No. 

*Then will I teach transgressors thy ways; and sinners shall be converted unto thee.*

It is the mercy God shows us as His children that leads us to share His praise to others, that the lost see His light upon us and they glorify Him in Heaven. It is not only the wrath that leaves the world without excuse, but the peace that passes understanding we are given by getting right with the Lord. 

When He delivers us from our guilt, forgiving our sin, restoring our relationship as sons, we cannot help but sing aloud of His righteousness. 

The sinner who trusts Christ is celebrated in Heaven by all the hosts of God...

**Luke 15:7**  
*I say unto you, that likewise joy shall be in heaven over one sinner that repenteth, more than over ninety and nine just persons, which need no repentance.*

...How much more the prodigal son who returns to his Father?

**Luke 15:18–20**
*I will arise and go to my father, and will say unto him, Father, I have sinned against heaven, and before thee, And am no more worthy to be called thy son: make me as one of thy hired servants. And he arose, and came to his father. But when he was yet a great way off, his father saw him, and had compassion, and ran, and fell on his neck, and kissed him.*

The Father runs to meet his returning son, showering him with love. Before any confession of guilt or offer of penence or anything other than turning back towards God, the Father goes to him in comparison and restitution of their relationship. 

God wants to forgive us more than we want to be forgiven. And we rejoice in His grace, that we may shew forth His praise. 

Again, God doesn't want sacrifice. He doesn't want our works of penence and vows to change. He takes no delight in the ritual formulas of burnt offerings, and performative acts of contrition.

*The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit: a broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt not despise.*

It is the healing of a loving relationship that He wants for us. Even at our lowest, we should remember this, and turn to Him again. 

It is then, in reconciliation and peace with God, through Christ Jesus our Lord, that the works we do and the praise we sing will please Him. Then will our religion match our hearts, in healing and in service to the mighty God. Amen

## Psalms 52  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-10-26

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/18024

<!-- post 18024 · 2026-10-26 05:37 · scripture -->

**Psalm 52** 
*Why boastest thou thyself in mischief, O mighty man? the goodness of God endureth continually.*

*2 Thy tongue deviseth mischiefs; like a sharp razor, working deceitfully.*

*3 Thou lovest evil more than good; and lying rather than to speak righteousness. Selah.*

*4 Thou lovest all devouring words, O thou deceitful tongue.*

*5 God shall likewise destroy thee for ever, he shall take thee away, and pluck thee out of thy dwelling place, and root thee out of the land of the living. Selah.*

*6 The righteous also shall see, and fear, and shall laugh at him:*

*7 Lo, this is the man that made not God his strength; but trusted in the abundance of his riches, and strengthened himself in his wickedness.*

*8 But I am like a green olive tree in the house of God: I trust in the mercy of God for ever and ever.*

*9 I will praise thee for ever, because thou hast done it: and I will wait on thy name; for it is good before thy saints.*

<!-- post 18025 · 2026-10-26 06:10 · study -->

In **Psalm 52**, the psalmist presents the polar opposite of the contrite heart of **Psalm 51**, the mischief-making man who doesn't avail himself to the longsuffering mercy of God. 

The wicked and their deceitful tongue, inventing lies, telling slander against God and God's people, boasting of their blessings, not thankful but vain in their imagination, loving evil more than good, these are the men who continually reject the call to fear the Lord. 

Those of us who are saved can become incredulous at the prideful stance of the wicked, who reject the fear of God and lean on their own strength. We can often grow confounded and even depressed over the ways of the wicked. Like just Lot, living in Sodom, being afflicted by the wickedness around him

**2 Peter 2:8**  
(*For that righteous man dwelling among them, in seeing and hearing, vexed his righteous soul from day to day with their unlawful deeds;)*

The Bible is not afraid to call these people out, directly and to their face. They are boastful, mockingly called "mighty men," liars, evil, haters of righteousness, lovers of slanderous and wicked words, lovers of mischief, having deceitful tongues. 

We should not fear to identify exactly the perverted in our midst when they reject and mock the gospel of God and the word we preach. 

Nor should we tiptoe around the heart of the matter; Their immanent destruction and the torment in the fire of Hell that awaits them. 

**Jude 1:22–23**
*And of some have compassion, making a difference: And others save with fear, pulling them out of the fire; hating even the garment spotted by the flesh.*

Those who do not see the wrath of God revealed from Heaven upon others as a reason to repent and believe, and won't see the mercy and hear the praise of the meek, feeling the compassionate love of believers magnify God's grace, need hard preaching to let them know exactly what they've chosen, in hopes that they might be saved. 

That their love of devouring words will lead to God likewise destroying them forever. Literally, being devoured by flame and worm in outer darkness, forever. Eternal suffering at the hands of a just and mighty God. 

*God shall likewise destroy thee for ever, he shall take thee away, and pluck thee out of thy dwelling place, and root thee out of the land of the living.* *Selah*.

If "selah" is meant to be a pause, a moment to reflect on what was just sung or said, is there anywhere more fitting than in that moment, having heard that sobering truth? 

God will destroy you. He will take your life and root you out of the land of the living, to place you in a place of eternal destruction. 

And the righteous shall see their end and fear God, and laugh at the calamity they brought about on themselves. The end their mischief brought about, turned back upon them.

**Proverbs 26:27** 
*Whoso diggeth a pit shall fall therein: and he that rolleth a stone, it will return upon him*.

The ironic nature of the bitter end experienced by the boastful sinner is often evident and clear to those that fear God. The humor of the Lord is appreciated by His children. We laugh at their calamity. 

When the wicked are made a parable or a byword among the people, it should give other men pause. The wrath revealed from Heaven should give instruction to the simple to turn towards God. It shows the righteous in Christ God's power and promise. 

**Proverbs 1:7**
*The fear of the LORD is the beginning of knowledge: but fools despise wisdom and instruction.*

The warning is ignored by the fool. And God will not be mocked.

**Proverbs 1:25–30**
*But ye have set at nought all my counsel, and would none of my reproof: I also will laugh at your calamity; I will mock when your fear cometh; When your fear cometh as desolation, and your destruction cometh as a whirlwind; when distress and anguish cometh upon you. Then shall they call upon me, but I will not answer; they shall seek me early, but they shall not find me: For that they hated knowledge, and did not choose the fear of the LORD: They would none of my counsel: they despised all my reproof.*

<!-- post 18026 · 2026-10-26 06:10 · study -->

These men that did not trust in God, but made their own puny strength and their worldly, perishing riches their stronghold, will be brought low. By their evil deeds they seek to maintain power and control, and by the same they will be brought low by our God, Who is a consuming fire. 

But we are His. We have humbled ourselves to rely on His holy name. To be meek servants, poor in spirit, that we may receive His grace, and the abundance of life in His love. 

*I will praise thee for ever, because thou hast done it: and I will wait on thy name; for it is good before thy saints.*

They waited for Jesus, we look back to His cross and the glory of His resurrection. We shall praise His name among the people. We shall look at His mercy and wrath, and know He is our salvation. We will praise Him forever, as His saints. Amen

## Psalms 68  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2024-06-16

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/14356

<!-- post 14356 · 2024-06-16 08:41 · scripture -->

**Psalm 68**
*Let God arise, let his enemies be scattered: let them also that hate him flee before him.*

*2 As smoke is driven away, so drive them away: as wax melteth before the fire, so let the wicked perish at the presence of God.*

*3 But let the righteous be glad; let them rejoice before God: yea, let them exceedingly rejoice.*

*4 Sing unto God, sing praises to his name: extol him that rideth upon the heavens by his name Jah, and rejoice before him.*

*5 A father of the fatherless, and a judge of the widows, is God in his holy habitation.*

*6 God setteth the solitary in families: he bringeth out those which are bound with chains: but the rebellious dwell in a dry land.*

*7 O God, when thou wentest forth before thy people, when thou didst march through the wilderness; Selah:*

*8 The earth shook, the heavens also dropped at the presence of God: even Sinai itself was moved at the presence of God, the God of Israel.*

*9 Thou, O God, didst send a plentiful rain, whereby thou didst confirm thine inheritance, when it was weary.*

*10 Thy congregation hath dwelt therein: thou, O God, hast prepared of thy goodness for the poor.*

*11 The Lord gave the word: great was the company of those that published it.*

*12 Kings of armies did flee apace: and she that tarried at home divided the spoil.*

*13 Though ye have lien among the pots, yet shall ye be as the wings of a dove covered with silver, and her feathers with yellow gold.*

*14 When the Almighty scattered kings in it, it was white as snow in Salmon.*

*15 The hill of God is as the hill of Bashan; an high hill as the hill of Bashan.*

*16 Why leap ye, ye high hills? this is the hill which God desireth to dwell in; yea, the Lord will dwell in it for ever.*

*17 The chariots of God are twenty thousand, even thousands of angels: the Lord is among them, as in Sinai, in the holy place.*

*18 Thou hast ascended on high, thou hast led captivity captive: thou hast received gifts for men; yea, for the rebellious also, that the Lord God might dwell among them.*

*19 Blessed be the Lord, who daily loadeth us with benefits, even the God of our salvation. Selah.*

*20 He that is our God is the God of salvation; and unto God the Lord belong the issues from death.*

*21 But God shall wound the head of his enemies, and the hairy scalp of such an one as goeth on still in his trespasses.*

*22 The Lord said, I will bring again from Bashan, I will bring my people again from the depths of the sea:*

*23 That thy foot may be dipped in the blood of thine enemies, and the tongue of thy dogs in the same.*

*24 They have seen thy goings, O God; even the goings of my God, my King, in the sanctuary.*

*25 The singers went before, the players on instruments followed after; among them were the damsels playing with timbrels.*

*26 Bless ye God in the congregations, even the Lord, from the fountain of Israel.*

*27 There is little Benjamin with their ruler, the princes of Judah and their council, the princes of Zebulun, and the princes of Naphtali.*

*28 Thy God hath commanded thy strength: strengthen, O God, that which thou hast wrought for us.*

*29 Because of thy temple at Jerusalem shall kings bring presents unto thee.*

*30 Rebuke the company of spearmen, the multitude of the bulls, with the calves of the people, till every one submit himself with pieces of silver: scatter thou the people that delight in war.*

*31 Princes shall come out of Egypt; Ethiopia shall soon stretch out her hands unto God.*

*32 Sing unto God, ye kingdoms of the earth; O sing praises unto the Lord; Selah:*

*33 To him that rideth upon the heavens of heavens, which were of old; lo, he doth send out his voice, and that a mighty voice.*

*34 Ascribe ye strength unto God: his excellency is over Israel, and his strength is in the clouds.*

*35 O God, thou art terrible out of thy holy places: the God of Israel is he that giveth strength and power unto his people. Blessed be God.*

## Psalms 72  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2024-06-09

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/14313

<!-- post 14313 · 2024-06-09 07:58 · scripture -->

**Psalm 72**
*Give the king thy judgments, O God, and thy righteousness unto the king's son.*

*2 He shall judge thy people with righteousness, and thy poor with judgment.*

*3 The mountains shall bring peace to the people, and the little hills, by righteousness.*

*4 He shall judge the poor of the people, he shall save the children of the needy, and shall break in pieces the oppressor.*

*5 They shall fear thee as long as the sun and moon endure, throughout all generations.*

*6 He shall come down like rain upon the mown grass: as showers that water the earth.*

*7 In his days shall the righteous flourish; and abundance of peace so long as the moon endureth.*

*8 He shall have dominion also from sea to sea, and from the river unto the ends of the earth.*

*9 They that dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him; and his enemies shall lick the dust.*

*10 The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents: the kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts.*

*11 Yea, all kings shall fall down before him: all nations shall serve him.*

*12 For he shall deliver the needy when he crieth; the poor also, and him that hath no helper.*

*13 He shall spare the poor and needy, and shall save the souls of the needy.*

*14 He shall redeem their soul from deceit and violence: and precious shall their blood be in his sight.*

*15 And he shall live, and to him shall be given of the gold of Sheba: prayer also shall be made for him continually; and daily shall he be praised.*

*16 There shall be an handful of corn in the earth upon the top of the mountains; the fruit thereof shall shake like Lebanon: and they of the city shall flourish like grass of the earth.*

*17 His name shall endure for ever: his name shall be continued as long as the sun: and men shall be blessed in him: all nations shall call him blessed.*

*18 Blessed be the Lord God, the God of Israel, who only doeth wondrous things.*

*19 And blessed be his glorious name for ever: and let the whole earth be filled with his glory; Amen, and Amen.*

*20 The prayers of David the son of Jesse are ended.*

*Give the king thy judgments, O God, and thy righteousness unto the king's son.*

## Psalms 73  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2023-08-25

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/11719

<!-- post 11719 · 2023-08-25 06:47 · scripture -->

**Psalm 73**
*Truly God is good to Israel, even to such as are of a clean heart.*

*2 But as for me, my feet were almost gone; my steps had well nigh slipped.*

*3 For I was envious at the foolish, when I saw the prosperity of the wicked.*

*4 For there are no bands in their death: but their strength is firm.*

*5 They are not in trouble as other men; neither are they plagued like other men.*

*6 Therefore pride compasseth them about as a chain; violence covereth them as a garment.*

*7 Their eyes stand out with fatness: they have more than heart could wish.*

*8 They are corrupt, and speak wickedly concerning oppression: they speak loftily.*

*9 They set their mouth against the heavens, and their tongue walketh through the earth.*

*10 Therefore his people return hither: and waters of a full cup are wrung out to them.*

*11 And they say, How doth God know? and is there knowledge in the most High?*

*12 Behold, these are the ungodly, who prosper in the world; they increase in riches.*

*13 Verily I have cleansed my heart in vain, and washed my hands in innocency.*

*14 For all the day long have I been plagued, and chastened every morning.*

*15 If I say, I will speak thus; behold, I should offend against the generation of thy children.*

*16 When I thought to know this, it was too painful for me;*

*17 Until I went into the sanctuary of God; then understood I their end.*

*18 Surely thou didst set them in slippery places: thou castedst them down into destruction.*

*19 How are they brought into desolation, as in a moment! they are utterly consumed with terrors.*

*20 As a dream when one awaketh; so, O Lord, when thou awakest, thou shalt despise their image.*

*21 Thus my heart was grieved, and I was pricked in my reins.*

*22 So foolish was I, and ignorant: I was as a beast before thee.*

*23 Nevertheless I am continually with thee: thou hast holden me by my right hand.*

*24 Thou shalt guide me with thy counsel, and afterward receive me to glory.*

*25 Whom have I in heaven but thee? and there is none upon earth that I desire beside thee.*

*26 My flesh and my heart faileth: but God is the strength of my heart, and my portion for ever.*

*27 For, lo, they that are far from thee shall perish: thou hast destroyed all them that go a whoring from thee.*

*28 But it is good for me to draw near to God: I have put my trust in the Lord God, that I may declare all thy works.*

<!-- post 7233 · 2022-12-19 05:56 · study -->

A little end times Bible prophecy in Psalms. Selah

<!-- post 7238 · 2022-12-19 12:32 · study -->

It's important to understand that the idea of penance, or working off sin as if it were a debt that we owe, isn't how it works.

Pun intended. 

There's no working off sin, there is the wage of sin which is death and Hell. Something that you earn by sinning. It's a false Doctrine, to say that if we do enough work the counteracts whatever sin we've committed. We are punished for sin, with Hell. But Jesus took the punishment in our place.

**Psalm 80**
*Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, thou that leadest Joseph like a flock; thou that dwellest between the cherubims, shine forth.*

*2 Before Ephraim and Benjamin and Manasseh stir up thy strength, and come and save us.*

*3 Turn us again, O God, and cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.*

*4 O Lord God of hosts, how long wilt thou be angry against the prayer of thy people?*

*5 Thou feedest them with the bread of tears; and givest them tears to drink in great measure.*

*6 Thou makest us a strife unto our neighbours: and our enemies laugh among themselves.*

*7 Turn us again, O God of hosts, and cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.*

*8 Thou hast brought a vine out of Egypt: thou hast cast out the heathen, and planted it.*

*9 Thou preparedst room before it, and didst cause it to take deep root, and it filled the land.*

*10 The hills were covered with the shadow of it, and the boughs thereof were like the goodly cedars.*

*11 She sent out her boughs unto the sea, and her branches unto the river.*

*12 Why hast thou then broken down her hedges, so that all they which pass by the way do pluck her?*

*13 The boar out of the wood doth waste it, and the wild beast of the field doth devour it.*

*14 Return, we beseech thee, O God of hosts: look down from heaven, and behold, and visit this vine;*

*15 And the vineyard which thy right hand hath planted, and the branch that thou madest strong for thyself.*

*16 It is burned with fire, it is cut down: they perish at the rebuke of thy countenance.*

*17 Let thy hand be upon the man of thy right hand, upon the son of man whom thou madest strong for thyself.*

*18 So will not we go back from thee: quicken us, and we will call upon thy name.*

*19 Turn us again, O Lord God of hosts, cause thy face to shine; and we shall be saved.*

<!-- post 15941 · 2025-04-20 08:52 · scripture -->

**Psalm 88**
*O lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before thee:*

*2 Let my prayer come before thee: incline thine ear unto my cry;*

*3 For my soul is full of troubles: and my life draweth nigh unto the grave.*

*4 I am counted with them that go down into the pit: I am as a man that hath no strength:*

*5 Free among the dead, like the slain that lie in the grave, whom thou rememberest no more: and they are cut off from thy hand.*

*6 Thou hast laid me in the lowest pit, in darkness, in the deeps.*

*7 Thy wrath lieth hard upon me, and thou hast afflicted me with all thy waves. Selah.*

*8 Thou hast put away mine acquaintance far from me; thou hast made me an abomination unto them: I am shut up, and I cannot come forth.*

*9 Mine eye mourneth by reason of affliction: Lord, I have called daily upon thee, I have stretched out my hands unto thee.*

*10 Wilt thou shew wonders to the dead? shall the dead arise and praise thee? Selah.*

*11 Shall thy lovingkindness be declared in the grave? or thy faithfulness in destruction?*

*12 Shall thy wonders be known in the dark? and thy righteousness in the land of forgetfulness?*

*13 But unto thee have I cried, O Lord; and in the morning shall my prayer prevent thee.*

*14 Lord, why castest thou off my soul? why hidest thou thy face from me?*

*15 I am afflicted and ready to die from my youth up: while I suffer thy terrors I am distracted.*

*16 Thy fierce wrath goeth over me; thy terrors have cut me off.*

*17 They came round about me daily like water; they compassed me about together.*

*18 Lover and friend hast thou put far from me, and mine acquaintance into darkness.*

Thank you Jesus for enduring the torment of Hell for me, a sinner.

**Psalm 91**
 *He that dwelleth in the secret place of the most High shall abide under the shadow of the Almighty.*

*2 I will say of the Lord, He is my refuge and my fortress: my God; in him will I trust.*

*3 Surely he shall deliver thee from the snare of the fowler, and from the noisome pestilence.*

*4 He shall cover thee with his feathers, and under his wings shalt thou trust: his truth shall be thy shield and buckler.*

*5 Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night; nor for the arrow that flieth by day;*

*6 Nor for the pestilence that walketh in darkness; nor for the destruction that wasteth at noonday.*

*7 A thousand shall fall at thy side, and ten thousand at thy right hand; but it shall not come nigh thee.*

*8 Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold and see the reward of the wicked.*

*9 Because thou hast made the Lord, which is my refuge, even the most High, thy habitation;*

*10 There shall no evil befall thee, neither shall any plague come nigh thy dwelling.*

*11 For he shall give his angels charge over thee, to keep thee in all thy ways.*

*12 They shall bear thee up in their hands, lest thou dash thy foot against a stone.*

*13 Thou shalt tread upon the lion and adder: the young lion and the dragon shalt thou trample under feet.*

*14 Because he hath set his love upon me, therefore will I deliver him: I will set him on high, because he hath known my name.*

*15 He shall call upon me, and I will answer him: I will be with him in trouble; I will deliver him, and honour him.*

*16 With long life will I satisfy him, and shew him my salvation.*

**1 Chronicles 4:9-10**
*And Jabez was more honourable than his brethren: and his mother called his name Jabez, saying, Because I bare him with sorrow. And Jabez called on the God of Israel, saying, Oh that thou wouldest bless me indeed, and enlarge my coast, and that thine hand might be with me, and that thou wouldest keep me from evil, that it may not grieve me! And God granted him that which he requested.*
